# VRC-A — Claude Code Instructions

## Project Overview
Android app called VRC-A (VRChat Assistant).
Built with Kotlin, Jetpack Compose, Firebase Firestore, and GitHub Actions CI.
There are three build variants: a public user-facing APK, a separate admin APK, and a Meta Quest **headset** APK.
Display name embedded in UI strings: "Ashoska Mitsu Sisko".

## Repository Structure
- Main app source: `app/src/main/kotlin/com/vrca/`
- **UI revamp + onboarding spec**: `docs/ui-revamp.md` — approved design doc (not yet implemented) covering the public-build UI compaction, first-open onboarding tutorial, Settings account management (VRChat/Discord logout + inline re-login, OSC hard-blocked while logged out of VRChat), ToS/login redesign, and implementation order. Read it before starting any public UI work.
- Kotlin package: `com.vrca` (applicationId stays `com.scrapw.chatbox` for install continuity)
- Subpackages: `admin/`, `app/`, `data/`, `discord/`, `keepalive/`, `nowplaying/`, `osc/`, `overlay/`, `sync/`, `ui/` (with `common/`, `conversation/`, `screen/`, `settings/`, `theme/`, `viewmodel/`), `update/`, `vrchat/`
- Admin vs public build separation: `BuildConfig.IS_ADMIN_BUILD`
- **Headset (Meta Quest) build variant (M1 scaffold shipped)**: a third product flavor `headsetApp` (applicationId **`com.gremlin.inc.headset`**, `BuildConfig.IS_HEADSET_BUILD=true`; `IS_HEADSET_BUILD=false` on public/admin) alongside `publicApp`/`adminApp`. Its own Firebase app (`app/src/headsetApp/google-services.json`, same `vrc-a-b592e` project) + a headset-only `VideoTranscode.kt` stub (mirrors publicApp; the transcoder is admin-only) + a headset manifest overlay (`app/src/headsetApp/AndroidManifest.xml`) that merges `android:screenOrientation="sensorLandscape"` onto `MainActivity` so the app opens in Quest's 16:10 **1024×640 dp default panel** ("monitor" shape) instead of a tall phone. **Monitor-shape framing is width-responsive, NOT flavor-gated**: `VrcaScreen`'s Scaffold content is wrapped in a `BoxWithConstraints` that centers page content at `HEADSET_MAX_CONTENT_WIDTH` (720.dp) once available width ≥ `HEADSET_WIDE_THRESHOLD` (640.dp) — the Quest panel (1024 dp) is well above it so content reads as a centered monitor window; a phone (portrait ~360–420 dp) stays below it so it's untouched (also helps tablets/foldables). Top/bottom bars keep full panel width as the window chrome; only the content centers. Build: `./gradlew assembleHeadsetAppDebug`. Distribution target is the **Meta Horizon Store / App Lab** (dev/testing via sideload/MQDH). **Fixed panel (no user resize — best-effort)**: the headset manifest sets `android:resizeableActivity="false"` on BOTH `<application>` and `MainActivity`, PLUS `android:minAspectRatio="1.6"`+`android:maxAspectRatio="1.6"` on `MainActivity` to pin the 16:10 shape. **Honest limit (device-confirmed): Horizon OS still lets the user resize a 2D panel regardless** — Android has no hard "no resize" for VR panels. The real safeguard is the width-responsive framing + the Home **3/2/1-column thresholds** (below), which degrade gracefully at any size, so resizing is harmless even when it can't be prevented. **Headset Home = THREE columns** (`HomePage.kt`, `HOME_THREE_COL_THRESHOLD`=820.dp, cleared by the 1024dp panel; drops to 2-col ≥560dp then 1-col): **left** = skinny preview, **middle** = Line Toggles + Manual Send (they fit together), **right** = the instance roster pinned far-right. Each column scrolls internally; the Manual Send card hosts a hoisted `expandedState` + a `BringIntoViewRequester` so expanding it scrolls the middle column down to reveal the full editor. Headset top-bar notification badge is `offset(x=-2,y=4)` so it sits ON the bell instead of clipping past the bar's top edge; the Home NAV setup badge (`setupNeedsAttention`) is suppressed on the headset build (redundant — the same items already show in the top-bar notification button). **Instance-roster — LOG READER CONNECTED (M2 hookup shipped; roster is LIVE from the log)**: `VrcLogParser` (`com.vrca.vrchat`, PURE Kotlin, throwaway-testable) turns VRChat output-log lines into a `LogEvent` stream (`Joining wrld_…:instance~nonce`, `Entering Room:`, `OnPlayerJoined/Left <name> (usr_…)` — usr id optional for the older name-only format, `OnLeftRoom`, `Switching <name> to avatar <avatar>`) and folds them via `apply(state, event, nowMs)`/`replay(lines,…)` into an immutable `InstanceState` (location/world/nonce + a `roster` map keyed by userId, or `name:<name>` fallback). **`InstanceRosterManager` (`com.vrca.vrchat`, headset-started only)** is the glue: an idempotent `start(ctx)` launches an IO loop that folds the newest log via `VrcLogParser`, enriches each member's platform via `VrchatAuthManager.fetchUserInfo`→`prettyPlatform` (cached per userId, 300ms-paced; **only a SUCCESSFUL lookup is cached — a failed one (429/network) is left uncached so the 2s publish loop retries it, capped at 6 attempts; this fixed "non-friends never show a platform", they're fetched after friends so they were the ones hitting the rate limit on the initial burst and getting permanently cached as blank**), and publishes a `RosterUi{status(NEEDS_PERMISSION/NO_LOG/IDLE/LIVE), worldName, location, members[displayName,userId,platform,avatarName], logPath}` `StateFlow`. **Log access mirrors the reference companion (`docs/vrc-nexus-teardown.md` §4.7) — this was a REAL bug the first attempt got wrong**: (1) VRChat's Quest package is **`com.vrchat.VRChatAndroid`** (legacy `com.vrchat.oculus.quest`), NOT the guessed `com.vrchat.mobile.*`; (2) candidate dirs also include the SHARED `/sdcard/Documents/Logs`, `/sdcard/Documents/VRChat`, `/sdcard/VRChat/Logs`, `/sdcard/VRChat`; (3) direct File read (needs `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`) is tried first, then a **SAF folder grant** (`ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE`, persisted via `takePersistableUriPermission`, re-resolved from `persistedUriPermissions`, listed/read via `DocumentsContract`+`ContentResolver` with offset-skip tailing) — the SAF route is what works when `Android/data/<vrcpkg>` is File-API-blocked (Android 11+/most Horizon OS). It tails the newest `output_log*.txt`/`player.log`/`vrchat*.log` by byte offset (replays a new/rotated file from 0; folds only up to the last complete line). **`VrcLogParser` also parses `Joining or Creating Room: <name>`** for the world name (in addition to `Entering Room:`). **CRITICAL user-facing requirement: VRChat must have Logging = FULL** (Settings → Debug) or `OnPlayerJoined`/`Joining` lines are never written — the panel's NO_LOG state spells this out. `InstanceRosterPanel` (`com.vrca.ui.screen`, its own file, observes the flow, starts the manager on headset) renders the four states via `AccessState`: NEEDS_PERMISSION/NO_LOG show a "Grant file access" button (only when All-files is missing) + a "Choose log folder" SAF button + (NO_LOG) the Logging=FULL checklist; IDLE/LIVE show the not-in-a-world hint or live member rows (initial-circle avatar + name + platform chip PC/Quest/iOS). **Roster ordering + friends**: members are sorted **friends-first** (from the local `FriendsCacheStore` keys, 15s TTL — no API cost) then by join time; a friend's name renders **yellow** (0xFFFFD54F). Platform enrichment walks this SAME order as a **single-flight** pass so chips fill in top-to-bottom. **No disk persistence / no storage buildup**: the reader writes NOTHING per-user to disk (only the SAF tree URI pref); `platformCache`/`enrichAttempts`/`enrichInFlight` are in-memory and **cleared on leaving an instance** so RAM stays bounded to the current instance. **The BASE All-files permission ALSO has a headset-only row in Settings → Permissions** (next to Overlay/Battery, `InstanceRosterManager.allFilesAccessIntent`); the rest of the setup (folder pick, Logging=FULL) lives only on the Home panel. **`MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` is in the HEADSET manifest ONLY** (public/admin never start the reader). **Per-user platform lookup**: `VrchatAuthManager.fetchUserInfo(ctx, userId)` (`GET /users/{id}`, PUBLIC endpoint so non-friends resolve) returns `VrcUserInfo(displayName, platform, trustRank, status, statusDescription, location)`; `prettyPlatform(raw)` maps `standalonewindows→PC`, `android→Quest`, `ios→iOS`, `web→Web`, blank/offline→"". **Still deferred**: the roster→Firestore writer + any Horizon-Store submission manifest entries. Boot/onboarding screens aren't monitor-framed yet (M1.5 polish). See `docs/account-system-plan.md` §2 for the device-role/source-of-truth model.
- **Device hash per build variant**: `MainActivity.ensureDeviceHash()` derives the `users/{deviceHash}` doc id from `computeBaseDeviceHash()` (`sha256("v2:$androidId:$signingCert")`, with a stored random fallback for blank ANDROID_ID). The **admin build** namespaces this as `sha256("admin:" + base)` so it resolves to a DISTINCT doc from the public build on the same phone — without this, both installs share one ANDROID_ID + signing key → one doc, so their presets/content overwrote each other AND the admin user-list self-filter (`it.id != myDeviceHash` in `AdminScreen`) hid the public install. With the distinct admin hash: (1) the two installs no longer sync (the admin build's moderation listener watches its own empty doc, not the public one); (2) the public install now appears in the admin Users tab / Dashboard so directed releases can be tested against it. The admin build never writes a user doc — `performSelfSync()`, `applyRemoteContentBeforeSync()`, and `applyRemoteConfig()` all early-return on `IS_ADMIN_BUILD`, so no orphan doc is created under the admin hash AND the admin's chatbox presets/messages live purely in local DataStore. (This gating was missing before: the admin build was reading its own `users/{adminHash}` doc back into DataStore on every launch via `applyRemoteContentBeforeSync`/`applyRemoteConfig`, which overwrote fresh local presets with a stale earlier snapshot — the "admin reverts to a past synced state on reopen" bug. With the three gates in place, DataStore is the sole source of truth for the admin build's content.) The admin hash is force-normalised on launch (overwrites any previously-cached public-equal hash). Public-only users are unaffected — their seed/hash is unchanged.
- CI workflow: `.github/workflows/android.yml`
- Firebase config: `google-services.json`
- Firestore rules: `firestore.rules` (uses `isOwner()` via `config/app.ownerUid`, NOT `sign_in_provider`). **Hardening (Phase 7)**: `selfMutableKeys()` includes `lastActiveAt` (hourly heartbeat write); `ownerOnlyUserKeys()` explicitly includes `killSignal` (defensive — self-update already can't touch it since it isn't in `selfMutableKeys()`). `announcements` read is `isOwner() || (signedIn() && resource.data.active == true)` — drafts (active==false) are owner-only and unauthenticated scraping is blocked; both client listeners already filter `active==true` so list queries pass per-doc. `users/{deviceHash}/live/{doc}` subcollection is readable by the owner or the device-doc owner, writable by the device-doc owner or admin. `bannedIdentifiers` `get` stays `signedIn()` (the client ban check reads its own vrchatId, unverifiable in rules) but `list` is owner-only so the collection can't be enumerated.

## Build Commands
```bash
# Debug build (public)
./gradlew assembleDebug

# Release build
./gradlew assembleRelease

# Admin build
./gradlew assembleAdminDebug

# Headset (Meta Quest) build
./gradlew assembleHeadsetAppDebug

# Run tests
./gradlew test

# Run instrumented tests
./gradlew connectedAndroidTest

# Full build + test
./gradlew build
```

### Web-session build environment + testing policy
- **Android SDK in Claude Code on the web**: the remote container starts WITHOUT the Android SDK, so `./gradlew` can't compile until it's installed. A committed **SessionStart hook** (`.claude/hooks/session-start.sh`, registered in `.claude/settings.json`) installs it automatically at session start (cmdline-tools + `platform-tools`, `platforms;android-34`, `build-tools;34.0.0`), accepts licenses, and points Gradle at it (`local.properties` `sdk.dir` + `ANDROID_HOME`). It is remote-only (`CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE`), idempotent (skips when present, ~15s cold), and installs to `$HOME/android-sdk`. `.gitignore` tracks only `.claude/settings.json` + `.claude/hooks/` (personal `.claude` files stay ignored). **Always `./gradlew compilePublicAppDebugKotlin` (or `assemblePublicAppDebug`) before pushing** — this is what catches API/signature nits (e.g. `HorizontalDivider` vs `Divider`, `MemoryCache.maxSizeBytes` taking `Int`) that would otherwise only fail in CI.
- **No permanent unit tests (user preference)**: do NOT add lasting test files to the repo — the user considers them unneeded clutter for logic that rarely changes. Instead, when adding a new feature or when the user asks to verify already-implemented logic, write a **throwaway** test/harness (e.g. temporarily under `app/src/test`), run it via `./gradlew testPublicAppDebugUnitTest` to confirm the pure-logic behavior, then **delete it before committing**. This covers only Android-independent logic (ad detection, `TitleCleaner`, bio diff, `followSeriesKey`/recurring collapse, timezone resolution, notification dedup/fingerprints); UI, notifications firing, and the VRChat/Discord/OSC/Firestore integrations can only be verified on the user's real device.

## Architecture & Stack
- Language: Kotlin
- UI: Jetpack Compose
- Backend: Firebase Firestore
- Auth/device management: Firestore `bannedDevices` collection
- NowPlaying: pause detection logic (check for edge cases when modifying)
- Admin UI: Users tab, Releases tab, tab bar navigation
- APK distribution: GitHub Releases. `githubUploadAsset()` and `githubCreateRelease()` in `ReleasesTab.kt` use OkHttp. **`githubUploadAsset` uploads to the canonical `upload_url` template returned by `githubCreateRelease`** (e.g. `https://uploads.github.com/repos/OWNER/REPO/releases/ID/assets{?name,label}`) — it strips the `{?name,label}` suffix and appends `?name=<encoded>`. It does NOT rebuild the URL from `BuildConfig.GITHUB_OWNER`/`GITHUB_REPO`. This matters because if those are a renamed/old repo, `uploads.github.com` responds with a redirect *early* (before the streamed APK body finishes), which closes the okio sink mid-`writeTo()` and surfaces as `IllegalStateException: closed` on every retry. `githubCreateRelease` already follows the moved-repo redirect during create, so its response `upload_url` is canonical — using it sends the binary straight to the right host with no redirect. The owner/repo for create-release come from `RELEASE_OWNER`/`RELEASE_REPO` GitHub Actions secrets (CI overwrites `keystore.properties` from them at build time; the committed `keystore.properties` is only used for local builds). `githubUploadAsset` still uses `followRedirects(false)` + a manual `Location` redirect loop (fresh `buildBody()` per hop, repeatable file stream) as a fallback. Both functions use `retryOnConnectionFailure(true)` and retry up to 5 times with exponential backoff. Error messages include the exception class name and cause chain for diagnostics.

### VRChat Integration
- **VrchatAuthManager**: Singleton handling VRChat API auth (Basic auth + 2FA), cookie storage via EncryptedSharedPreferences, presence fetching, and friends list retrieval. Also saves credentials to EncryptedSharedPreferences for auto-relogin when sessions expire. Credentials are saved BEFORE the login HTTP request so they survive app kills during response processing. **Basic-auth credentials are URI-encoded (`encodeUriComponent`, an `encodeURIComponent`-equivalent) BEFORE base64** — VRChat's backend URL-decodes them, so a password containing `@ + # : & %` etc. fails with 401 if sent raw. This was a real "permanent 2FA bypass broke" cause: the raw-bytes login 401'd → `clearCredentials()` wiped the saved creds → auto-relogin died → the next launch forced a full manual login *including* the 2FA prompt. **The 401 handler now only calls `clearCredentials()` when the error message actually indicates bad credentials** (`invalid`/`incorrect`/`credential`/`password`/`username`); a transient/session 401 (e.g. IP-invalidated session — VRChat binds the `auth` cookie to the client IP, which changes constantly on mobile) keeps the saved creds so auto-relogin can recover instead of forcing manual 2FA. On re-login after auth-cookie expiry, the Basic-auth request sends ONLY the persisted `twoFactorAuth` cookie via `getTwoFaOnlyCookieHeader()` — never the expired `auth=` cookie, which can cause VRChat to reject the request as a session conflict. VRChat recognizes the trusted device and skips the 2FA prompt. **The `twoFactorAuth` cookie is ALWAYS sent regardless of age — there is NO client-side expiry drop.** The web client keeps a remembered device trusted effectively indefinitely (rolling it forward on use), so any hard client-side cutoff (the old `TWO_FA_COOKIE_MAX_AGE_MS` 30-day guard, now removed) only ever HURTS — it forced an avoidable 2FA prompt that VRChat itself would not have asked for. Sending a stale cookie is harmless: worst case VRChat ignores it and returns `requiresTwoFactorAuth`, which is exactly what dropping it would have produced — so the SERVER is the sole authority on trusted-device validity. `KEY_2FA_COOKIE_STORED_AT` is kept for diagnostics only. On successful Basic-auth login, the auth cookie + user info are written via a direct prefs edit; **if VRChat re-issued a `twoFactorAuth` cookie in that login's `Set-Cookie`, it is captured and its 30-day clock RESET so the trusted window keeps extending**; if it did NOT (the common bypass-login case), the existing 2FA cookie and its original stored-at timestamp are preserved untouched (without this preservation, the 2FA cookie would get wiped on every successful relogin and the SECOND auth-cookie expiry would always force manual 2FA). **Trusted-device cookie roll-forward (`captureRolledCookies`)**: VRChat's web client never re-prompts because the server rolls the `twoFactorAuth` cookie forward on EVERY use — the browser's cookie never reaches its ~30-day Max-Age. The app previously captured that rotation ONLY on the explicit login/verify paths and discarded the `Set-Cookie` on every other authenticated call (`fetchPresence`, `validateSession`, `refreshProfilePic`, …), so the app's stored trusted-device cookie aged out and ~30 days after the last login VRChat demanded a fresh 2FA code even though the website would not. **`captureRolledCookies(context, rawCookies)` now persists any refreshed `auth`/`twoFactorAuth` cookie (resetting its stored-at clock) after every authenticated success** — wired into `fetchPresence` (runs every 10s while watched / on the slow loop, so the cookie stays continuously fresh), `validateSession`, and `refreshProfilePic`. It is strictly additive (only overwrites when a non-blank refreshed cookie is present), so it keeps the app's trusted-device window as fresh as the browser's and stops the spurious "keeps asking for 2FA" re-prompts. (A user whose stored cookie already expired under the old behaviour will be asked once more, then stays trusted.) **`captureRolledCookies` is now wired into EVERY authenticated REST call, not just three** — `fetchFriends`, `fetchPendingNotifications`/`V2`, `fetchUserGroups`, `fetchGroupName`/`Announcement`/`Posts`/`CalendarEvents`, `verifyStillFriend`, the `fetchPresence` `/users/{id}` + `/instances/{location}` sub-calls, and the post-2FA `/auth/user` fetch all roll the cookie forward. This matters for a NORMAL (unwatched) user: `fetchPresence` only hits the VRChat API while an admin is watching (the unwatched slow loop is local-only), so previously the sole roll-forward point for most users was the 6-hour `validateSession`; now the every-connect REST sweep keeps the `twoFactorAuth` cookie continuously rolled forward like the browser's cookie jar. **CRITICAL double-prefix fix**: `extractCookieValue()` already returns the full `name=value` pair (e.g. `twoFactorAuth=VALUE`), but the old `verify2FA()` re-wrapped it as `"twoFactorAuth=$it"` → stored the MALFORMED `twoFactorAuth=twoFactorAuth=VALUE`. VRChat rejected that as an invalid trusted-device cookie, so EVERY VRChat global logout forced a fresh manual 2FA prompt even though the website (whose cookie jar stored the correct value) would not. This was the real "trusted device never sticks" cause. `verify2FA()` now stores `extractCookieValue`'s return verbatim. A read-side self-heal (`normalizeCookie`/`readCookie`, used by `getCookieHeader`/`getTwoFaOnlyCookieHeader`) collapses any historically-stored double prefix in place (persisting the corrected value, so the WebSocket and all paths get the clean cookie) — existing users are migrated automatically on first read. `getPrefs()` has a recovery fallback: if EncryptedSharedPreferences init fails (MasterKey corruption), it deletes the corrupted prefs file and retries. `diagnoseAuthState()` logs the presence/age of all auth-related preferences for debugging. `VrchatPipelineService` runs a 6-hour `startAuthRefreshLoop()` that — because the 12-day age threshold rarely fires before the cookie actually dies — also **actively validates the session each cycle (`validateSession()` → `GET /auth`) and `autoRelogin()`s when it's dead regardless of cookie age**. `scheduleReconnect()` likewise re-validates and re-logins after repeated WebSocket failures (`reconnectAttempt >= 2`) instead of spinning on a present-but-dead cookie — `isLoggedIn()` only checks the cookie is PRESENT, not valid. Also provides REST helpers for offline notification backfill: `fetchPendingNotifications()`, `fetchPendingNotificationsV2()`, `fetchUserGroups()`, `fetchGroupAnnouncement(groupId)`. Trust rank extraction (`extractTrustRankFromTags`) reads the highest `system_trust_*` tag from VRChat's tags array. All REST `get()` calls set `useCaches = false` + `Cache-Control: no-cache` so Android's HttpURLConnection never serves a stale cached response — this fixes the instance player count (`/instances/$location`) showing minutes-old values in both the app and Discord RPC. **Instance headcount source (`extractInstanceUserCount`)**: VRChat's `/instances/{loc}` object exposes THREE candidate counts that disagree by a few users. **`userCount` is the field that matches the in-game client's instance panel** (what the player and everyone in the instance actually see in-headset). `n_users` AND the per-platform breakdown (`platforms`: `standalonewindows` + `android` + `ios`, which sums to the **website / VRCX** number) AGREE with each other but run a few HIGH — they count users mid-join / in-transit / timing-out that the in-game client has already dropped. The helper **prefers `userCount`**, falling back to `n_users` then the platforms-sum (iterating ALL keys so new platforms are future-proof) only when `userCount` is absent. (History: this thrashed before it was settled empirically. Original code preferred `n_users`; the user compared against the *website* and found it "wrong", so it was switched to the platforms-sum; a later screenshot showed the platforms-sum over-counting the *in-game* client (21 vs 18) so it was switched to prefer `n_users` — but `n_users` ALSO over-counted in-game (47 vs 36). A temporary in-app debug overlay logging all three fields finally proved `userCount` == the in-game panel (36) while `n_users`/`platformsSum` both read 47. Ironically `userCount` had been sitting in the code as the never-reached final fallback the entire time.) Both `fetchInstanceCount` and `fetchPresence`'s `/instances/{location}` parse go through this helper. Queue size is deliberately NOT added (irrelevant for normal instances).
- **VrchatPipelineService**: Foreground service with OkHttp WebSocket to `wss://pipeline.vrchat.cloud`. Handles real-time events (friend online/offline/active/location, friend-update, unfriend, invites, group events) and manages friends cache. Writes VRChat presence to Firestore **only while an admin is watching** this user (gated by `AdminWatchState.isWatched`); when unwatched, in-app presence still updates locally via WebSocket events but no traffic hits Firestore. The `syncPresenceToFirestore()` function gates the Firestore WRITE behind `AdminWatchState.isWatched`. When watched, presence polls every 10s. A slow 10s loop runs when unwatched with `forceLocalUpdate=true` to keep local presence state current (it does NOT write Firestore when unwatched, but it DOES call `fetchPresence` — the in-app VRChat tab + Discord RPC read the resulting `VrchatPipelineState.presence`; an earlier note claiming "no VRChat API calls when unwatched" was wrong). **Instant instance player count (`fetchInstanceCount`)**: the in-app/RPC player count (`n_users`) used to lag *minutes* behind the VRChat website. Root cause: the count is the 3rd of `fetchPresence`'s sequential calls (`/auth/user` → `/users/{id}` → `/instances/{location}`), so it only refreshed when the WHOLE chain landed — minutes apart on mobile under cookie IP-invalidation / rate limits — whereas the website hits ONLY `/instances/{loc}` per refresh (1 call, instant). Fix: `VrchatAuthManager.fetchInstanceCount(location)` does that single lightweight call (live `platforms`-sum count via `extractInstanceUserCount` / `capacity`). It is fired (a) **immediately on every self `user-location` world-hop** in `applySelfLocationEvent` (the WS location event carries no count, so the count previously sat at 0/stale until the next full chain — now it's fresh the instant you change instances), and (b) as a **null-fallback inside `syncPresenceToFirestore`** when `fetchPresence` returns null (the heavy chain failed) — it patches just the count on the last-known location so the count tracks reality instead of freezing. (When the cookie is *fully* IP-invalidated even this single call fails; that's the session-recovery path, separate.) **Light presence fallback (`VrchatAuthManager.fetchSelfPresenceLight`)**: when the heavy `fetchPresence` chain returns null, `syncPresenceToFirestore` first tries ONE `GET /users/{id}` call to recover the true location/state (no `/auth/user`, no `/instances` — one request, far less likely to be throttled than three) and flows the result into the SAME `VrchatPipelineState.presence` + watched Firestore write path. When the location is unchanged it merges the last-known `worldName`/instance counts; a world hop leaves `worldName` briefly blank until the next heavy chain refills it. This is what stops a genuinely in-game user (whose heavy chain is failing on a partial 429) freezing as "not in a world" on the admin panel / "not in VRChat" on their RPC. If even this single call fails (cookie fully dead), it falls through to the count-only patch as before. **Reactive self-presence from the WebSocket (`applySelfLocationEvent` / `applySelfUserUpdate`)**: the user's OWN `user-location` (world-hop) and `user-update` (status) events now patch `VrchatPipelineState.presence` DIRECTLY from the event payload — no REST call. This fixes "the Discord RPC keeps counting but doesn't match my VRChat state": self presence used to refresh only via the periodic `fetchPresence()` REST poll, which returns null whenever the auth cookie is IP-invalidated (constant on mobile/backgrounded), so `presence` froze while the RPC (whose online timer is independent) kept pushing a stale world. The `user-location` event was previously a no-op for self. Now patching it emits on `presenceFlow` → the RPC's `collectLatest` pushes the real world instantly even when REST is dead. When an admin is watching, `pushSelfPresenceToFirestoreIfWatched()` also writes the patched `vrchatLocation`/`vrchatWorld`/`vrchatState` straight to Firestore (no REST round-trip), so the admin directory's VRChat location updates instantly and resiliently — this fixes "the user's VRChat location not updating on the admin menu". The periodic watched 10s `syncPresenceToFirestore()` REST poll still runs as the authoritative full-field refresh (now more reliable thanks to the cookie roll-forward above). **No periodic `lastSeenAt` heartbeat lives here anymore** — liveness is a single hourly write owned by `VrcaViewModel.startHourlyHeartbeat()` (see "Hourly liveness model" below); the only periodic Firestore write this service makes is the 10s watched-user presence sync (which also refreshes `lastActiveAt`). Connect/disconnect transitions fire connection-status notifications (gated by `notif_connection` pref).
- **Friends cache**: Persisted **locally** in SharedPreferences (`vrca_friends_cache` / `friends_json`) via `FriendsCacheStore`. No Firestore involvement — friends data is only used by the user's own app for unfriend notifications and is meaningless to admins. Old `savedFriendIds`/`savedFriendNames` fields in user docs are removed via `FieldValue.delete()` on every offline write so legacy data doesn't linger.
- **Live bio/name/rank notifications (`handleFriendUpdate`) — the PRIMARY instant path**: The pipeline `friend-update` WebSocket event **DOES** carry `bio` / `displayName` / `tags` (verified on-device via a temporary debug log that dumped raw payload keys — every event showed `bio="..."`). So `handleFriendUpdate` fires bio/name/rank changes **instantly** for online friends, with no polling. (An earlier note here wrongly claimed VRChat doesn't send `bio` live and that the old "instant while active" was incidental — both were wrong; the live event has always carried it.) It gates on `user.has("bio")` so partial location/status payloads (which omit the field) don't false-fire.
- **Pipeline message serialization (`pipelineDispatcher`) — the cache-race fix**: `friendsCache` is a plain non-thread-safe `mutableMapOf`, but `onMessage` used to `serviceScope.launch { handlePipelineMessage(text) }` on the **multi-threaded** IO dispatcher — so two events touching the same friend ran CONCURRENTLY, one's cache write clobbering the other's and a diff reading a stale `prev`, **silently dropping a notification**. Symptoms: a fast back-to-back bio edit firing once or not at all, and a quick **friend→unfriend** where the `friend-delete` read the cache before `friend-add` had written the entry (`?: return` drop). **Fix:** a process-lifetime single-threaded `pipelineDispatcher` (`Executors.newSingleThreadExecutor().asCoroutineDispatcher()`, closed in `onDestroy`). `onMessage` now launches on it (`serviceScope.launch(pipelineDispatcher)`), so all WS handlers run serialized in VRChat's send order — each handler's read-modify-write is atomic (the cache write precedes the first suspension point), and `friend-add` is processed before the `friend-delete` that followed it. The two nested cache-touching coroutines (`friend-offline` cooldown handler, the connect second-fetch diff) and the REST refresh diff also run on `pipelineDispatcher`. The REST diff re-reads the **latest** cache entry before writing (`val latest = friendsCache[userId] ?: prev`) so a concurrent WS presence update isn't reverted.
- **Periodic friends-profile refresh (`startFriendsProfileRefreshLoop`/`refreshFriendsProfilesAndDiff`) — the BACKUP path**: the live WS path above covers online friends instantly; this loop exists only for the one case the WS can't deliver — a friend editing their profile from the **website or phone while their VRChat status is "offline"** (VRChat sends no `friend-update` for offline friends, so they appear ONLY in the `offline=true` friends-list pass). It re-fetches the FULL friends list (`fetchFriends(onlineOnly=false)`, both `offline=false`+`offline=true` passes) and runs the same name/bio/rank diff. Cadence **`FRIENDS_PROFILE_FG_MS` = 30s while on-screen**, **`FRIENDS_PROFILE_REFRESH_MS` = 60s when backgrounded** (foreground from `VrchatPipelineState.appForeground`). **Cadence was dialed back from 12s/60s to 30s/60s**: each sweep is TWO 2-pass REST fetches and every authenticated call rolls the shared, IP-bound `auth` cookie the live pipeline WebSocket ALSO uses as its connect token — so an over-tight 12s foreground cadence churned that cookie / hit 429s and made the WS (and the Discord RPC that reads its presence) disconnect far more often. 30s still catches an offline-friend edit within ~30s. Since notifications fire regardless of foreground state, the slower background cadence only delays the friends-LIST display refresh (which the user isn't looking at), never the notification itself. **Foreground-reactive sleep**: the inter-sweep wait is sliced into 2s chunks and breaks early when `appForeground` flips false→true, so reopening the app runs a fast sweep within ~2s instead of waiting out the full 60s background sleep (the screenshot bug: the loop was stuck on a 180s sleep after reopen). (`fetchFriends` 429-handles by waiting 5s + retrying, so the cadence self-throttles if VRChat rate-limits.) The diff **MERGES** profile fields onto cache entries and preserves the live presence fields (`location`/`worldName`/`status`) the WS events own WHILE ONLINE — EXCEPT it **reconciles the OFFLINE direction from REST**: this sweep includes the `offline=true` pass, so a friend listed with `status="offline"` here IS offline; if the cache still shows them online a `friend-offline` WS event was MISSED (socket throttled while backgrounded), so the entry is reconciled to offline (silently, no late notification). This stops the friends-online count drifting too HIGH mid-session without needing a reopen (previously only a full reload on reconnect/reopen fixed a missed offline). Only the offline direction is reconciled; a friend's live world/status while ONLINE stays WS-owned (the WS is more real-time than this up-to-60s-stale fetch). Skipped during warmup / when not logged in / empty cache. Dedup is shared with the live + reconnect paths via the cache (whichever updates the cache first makes the other see no diff → no double-fire) and the `bio_$userId`/`name_$userId`/`rank_$userId` group keys. `VrchatAuthManager.fetchFriends(context, onlineOnly=false)` still has the `onlineOnly` param (only the `offline=false` pass when true) but this loop always passes `false`.
- **Unfriend notification logic**: Two sources cooperate via a `notifiedUnfriendIds` dedup set. (1) Real-time `friend-delete` from the WebSocket fires immediately for unfriends during an active session — but is **skipped if the userId isn't in the cache** (queued event for an offline unfriend that loadFriendsCache already removed). (2) Offline diff uses **two-fetch confirmation** to eliminate API pagination flaps: after the first API fetch on connect, removed IDs are captured into `candidateRemovals` but NOT notified. After the second fetch (60s later), only IDs still missing in BOTH fetches (`candidateRemovals ∩ stillMissing`) advance to verification. **Final verification gate**: both paths call `VrchatAuthManager.verifyStillFriend(userId)` which hits `GET /users/{id}` and reads the `isFriend` field. If true → suppress notification and re-add to cache (false positive); if false or 404 → fire notification; if null (network error / 429 / expired session) → fall back to firing the notification so we don't silently drop real unfriends. The dedup set ensures at most one notification per userId per session. The notification text is neutral — "Friend removed" / "$name is no longer on your friends list" — since the app can't know who initiated the removal. **Last-friend (1→0) fix (`fetchFriends` returns `List<VrcFriend>?`)**: unfriending your ONLY friend never notified because the diff couldn't tell a genuine empty list from a failed fetch — `fetchFriends` returned `emptyList()` for BOTH, so the guards `if (fresh.isEmpty()) return` / `if (friendsCache.isEmpty()) return` swallowed the 1→0 removal. `fetchFriends` now returns **null on a HARD failure** (no cookie, or not a single page ever returned 200 — session dead / rate-limited-out) and a **(possibly empty) list on success** (a valid 200 with `[]` = genuinely 0 friends). `loadFriendsCache()` returns a `Boolean` and, on a null fetch, **preserves the existing cache instead of wiping it** (a failed fetch used to blank the cache → the next diff would read every friend as removed; now the preserved cache makes `candidateRemovals` empty on failure). `diffFriendsCache` **no longer bails on an empty `friendsCache`** — an empty cache after a successful fetch is a real 0-friends state whose removal must fire; the per-candidate `verifyStillFriend` gate + the two-fetch confirmation + the `< 80%` partial-fetch guard (which the 1→0 case passes since `1*4/5==0`) are the false-positive protection. `refreshFriendsProfilesAndDiff` uses `fetchFriends(...) ?: return` (null = failed → skip; empty = 0 friends → loops zero times). Net: unfriending your last friend now fires, while a failed fetch still never false-flags removals.
- **Friend-add / friend-request dedup (repeatable per-person)**: two coupled fixes for "New friend fires 2×" and "unfriend→refriend doesn't notify". (1) **`friend-add` SHORT-WINDOW dedup (`recentFriendAddMs` + `FRIEND_ADD_DEDUP_MS` = 5 min, in-memory)**: the `friend-add` pipeline event carries no `dedupId`, and VRChat re-emits/replays it (notably on a WS reconnect), so a duplicate fired "New friend" twice ("New friend (2)"). The handler now swallows a repeat within the window. It is NOT permanent — a genuine later re-add still fires, and `friend-delete` clears the stamp so an unfriend→refriend inside the window still notifies. (2) **Friend-request `fr_<userId>` key is now CLEARED on resolution** — `seenNotifIds` is permanent by design (correct for one-shot notifications), but the friend-request dedup key is REPEATABLE per person (a new request can come after a decline or an unfriend/refriend cycle). It was never removed, so a re-request from someone whose `fr_` key was already present was suppressed forever. `clearSeenNotifId("fr_<userId>")` (removes + persists) is now called on **`friend-add`** (request accepted) and on **`friend-delete`** (both the real-time handler AND the offline `diffFriendsCache` path), so a future request from that person notifies again. (3) **Declined/rescinded requests (`reconcileResolvedFriendRequests`, 5-min poll)**: VRChat sends NO pipeline event when a request is declined or the sender rescinds it, so accept/unfriend clearing alone missed those. VRChat re-lists a pending request until it's resolved, so a person who still has a live `fr_` key but is NO LONGER in the current pending friend-request list (V1 + V2 fetch) AND isn't now a friend had their request resolved → clear `fr_<uid>`. Guarded by a **2-consecutive-absence strike** (`resolvedFrStrikes`, in-memory) so a partial/failed pending fetch can't spuriously clear a still-pending request (which would re-fire it — the duplicate bug); never acts on a failed fetch. Runs on the 5-min poll. **Known narrow window**: if the same person RE-requests within ~10 min (before the 2 strikes clear), the still-set `fr_` key suppresses it — a NEW pending request looks identical to the old one under per-person keying, so it's kept; only a resolved-then-quiet gap clears. The common decline-then-later-request case is covered.

- **Notification system**: Seven Android notification channels — `vrca_pipeline` (background service, MIN), `vrca_friends_activity` (online/offline/world/status/avatar/rank), `vrca_friend_requests` (incoming requests + new friends + messages), `vrca_friend_removals` (unfriends), `vrca_invites` (world + group invites), `vrca_groups` (announcements/queue/role/etc.), `vrca_connection` (pipeline disconnect + sign-in alerts + server alerts). Legacy `vrca_vrchat_events` is deleted on upgrade. The "VRChat connected / Monitoring VRChat events" notification is intentionally NOT fired — the persistent foreground service notification already conveys that state, and the duplicate notification was redundant. `fireConnectionNotification(true)` only cancels any leftover disconnect notification from a previous cycle. Notifications use `setGroup()` keys (`vrca_group_friends`, `vrca_group_social`, `vrca_group_invites`, `vrca_group_groups`) so multiple events stack in the shade with summary notifications; summary notifications use `GROUP_ALERT_CHILDREN` to prevent double notification sounds. Per-friend cooldowns (60s) on chatty events (location, status, avatar). Bio uses the grouped notification model instead of cooldowns — every bio change is captured as a separate event in the in-app alert group. **Bio change detection fires on edit, clear (text→empty), AND add (empty→text)** — the prior code required both old and new bio non-blank, so clearing a bio or adding one to an empty bio never fired. The live `handleFriendUpdate` path gates on `user.has("bio")` so partial location/status payloads (which omit the field) don't false-fire; empty sides render as `(empty)` in the diff. Notification titles reflect the action ("cleared their bio" / "added a bio" / "updated bio"). All 27 toggles live in `VrchatNotificationPrefs` keys + `UserPreferencesRepository` flows. The UI lives in `LegacyPagesAndSettingsPage.kt`'s `NotificationToggleSection` (public, called from `VrcaScreen.kt`). Each `ToggleRow` has an optional `description` parameter showing muted `bodySmall` text explaining what the toggle does. Organized into 5 collapsible sections with sub-section labels for visual grouping: Friend list (4 toggles), Friends activity (10 toggles, sub-grouped into Presence / Profile changes / Alerts), Invites (3 toggles: world invites / invite requests / group invites), Groups (6 toggles, sub-grouped into Updates / Management), App and connection (6 toggles, sub-grouped into App / VRChat). Each section is expandable/collapsible via `AnimatedVisibility` with fade+slide animation. The `SubSectionLabel` composable renders muted-color `labelMedium` text between toggle groups. The `KEY_NOTIF_INVITE_REQUEST` flow falls back to `KEY_NOTIF_INVITE` until the user explicitly toggles it, so existing users who had the combined invite preference enabled keep getting invite-request notifications.
  - **Offline notification backfill**: On every successful pipeline connect, `backfillOfflineNotifications()` runs a REST sweep across VRChat endpoints (`/auth/user/notifications`, `/auth/user/notifications/v2`, `/users/{id}/groups` + per-group `/groups/{id}/posts` and group events). The posts sweep is the sole announcement source — `fetchGroupAnnouncement()` (the single pinned announcement) was removed from both backfill and poll loop because the posts endpoint already returns the pinned announcement as one of its posts, and having both caused duplicate notifications (`ga_` vs `gp_` dedup IDs for the same content). **`fetchGroupPosts` parses VRChat's wrapped response** (`{"posts":[...],"total":N}`) as well as a bare array. **`fetchGroupCalendarEvents`** hits the correct VRChat group-calendar endpoint **`GET /calendar/{groupId}?n=N`** first (returns `{"hasNext":bool,"results":[...],"totalCount":N}`), with the legacy `/groups/{id}/events` and `/groups/{id}/calendar` paths kept only as 404 fallbacks. The prior code used `/groups/{id}/events`/`/scheduledEvents`/`/calendar` which all 404'd — so the calendar sweep always returned null and events created while the app was closed never surfaced (the only event source was notification-v2, which VRChat drops from the pending feed once marked seen server-side). Parses `results`, `events`, `calendarEvents`, `scheduledEvents` array keys or a bare array. `fireGroupCalendarEvent()` is the shared helper (backfill + poll loop) that fires each new event (dedup `${groupId}_event_$eventId`, seen-marker = `startsAt`) into the `event_$groupId` in-app alert group with URL `/home/group/$groupId/calendar/$eventId`. `seedBackfillBaseline()` seeds recent post IDs and calendar event IDs so the first run doesn't flood pre-existing items. A one-time migration (`posts_events_baseline_v2` DataStore flag) re-seeds posts/events into the existing seenMap for users who installed before `fetchGroupPosts` was fixed — `seedPostsAndEventsIntoExistingMap()` merges without overwriting. A SECOND one-time migration (`calendar_baseline_v3` flag) runs `seedAllCalendarEventsBaseline()` which seeds **ALL** current calendar events (past AND upcoming) into the seenMap + event fingerprint set without firing — necessary because the old calendar endpoint always 404'd, so no events were ever seeded; without this baseline the first working `GET /calendar/{groupId}` sweep would fire every pre-existing upcoming event at once. After this baseline only events created later surface as new. A THIRD one-time migration (`posts_events_baseline_v4` flag) re-runs `seedPostsAndEventsIntoExistingMap()` because the **group posts/calendar fetch window was RAISED** (posts `20 → 50`, events `→ 100` in both the backfill sweep and `seedPostsAndEventsIntoExistingMap`) so a group's many repeating-event occurrences can no longer crowd genuine posts/events out of the fetch. Raising the window exposed OLDER posts + past events that were never in the seen baseline (seeded at the smaller window) — the catch-up path then fired them as "new" (the tester-reported "spammed with a bunch of old announcements from ages ago, only on the new version" regression). The v4 re-seed covers the now-larger window once (all posts + PAST events only; genuinely upcoming events stay unseeded so they still surface once as intended), so the newly-visible older items don't fire. Runs in `backfillOfflineNotifications` right after the v2/v3 gates, before the posts/events sweep. It seeds ALL posts but **only PAST calendar events** (startsAt already elapsed); upcoming events are intentionally NOT seeded so the backfill sweep fires them once and users see events they missed while closed (per-event dedup stops re-fires). **Cross-dedup, two flavors**: (1) announcements/posts use a CONTENT fingerprint (`announcementContentFingerprint(groupId, title, text)`) added to `seenNotifIds` when fired from any path, checked by the other paths. **The fingerprint is BODY-based** (`ann_fp_<groupId>_<normalized-body.hashCode()>`, body = `text.ifBlank{title}` lowercased + whitespace-collapsed + first 120 chars). The title is intentionally EXCLUDED: the live notification-v2 path keys on the `message` field while the REST group-post path keys on the `text` field and they title the same post differently, so a title-inclusive key produced two different fingerprints for one announcement and the dedup missed. **The 5-min poll posts sweep now calls `isContentFingerprintSeen()` before firing** (it previously only checked its own per-post `notif_group_announcement_seen` timestamp map, never the fingerprint the live path set) — this was the cause of "an announcement fires once when the group posts it, then AGAIN a few minutes later" (the live WS path fired immediately; the poll re-fired because it didn't consult the live fingerprint). Now the poll records the seen timestamp and skips when the body fingerprint is already present. (2) **EVENTS use an ID-keyed fingerprint** (`eventFingerprint(groupId, eventId)` → `evt_fp_<groupId>_<eventId>`) shared across the WebSocket live handler, `processV2Notification`, and `fireGroupCalendarEvent` (the REST sweep). The content fingerprint was WRONG for events: distinct events frequently share a generic title ("Event starting") and the same 80-char text prefix, so a content key collided and suppressed all but the first event — the cause of "only one event shows even when the group made several". The eventId-keyed fingerprint dedups the SAME event across the three paths (all extract the same `cal_xxx` id) without colliding sibling events. Fires missed notifications through the same `fireEventNotification` flow with dedup IDs. **Staleness filters**: only *transient* notifications older than 24h are skipped via `parseVrcTimestampMs()` on the `created_at` field — V1 invites/requestInvite/votetokick/message, and V2 invite/queue/join-request/instance. **Catch-up types are exempt from the age cutoff** and fire regardless of age: V1 `friendRequest` (pending state until accepted/declined), V2 announcement/post/event/calendar and role/transfer, and group announcements/posts (backfill + poll loop). This is safe because the per-item dedup (`seenNotifIds` for V1/V2, the `notif_group_announcement_seen` per-group timestamp map for group REST) already guarantees each one fires exactly once. A user offline for a week still receives the friend requests, role changes, and group announcements/events they missed, while already-seen items never resurface. The first-run baseline guard (below) prevents a flood on fresh install. Announcements from the same group are grouped together via `alertGroupKey = "announcement_$groupId"`. **Group name resolution**: a `groupNameCache` (groupId → name) is populated whenever `fetchUserGroups()` runs; `resolveGroupName(groupId, sender)` looks up the cache first, falls back to the sanitized sender username (rejecting `null`/blank/`someone`), then `"a group"` — so notifications never render "Announcement from null". `resolveGroupNameAsync()` is the suspend variant used by live + backfill group announcement/event paths: when the groupId isn't cached and the sender name is missing, it fetches the group's real name once via `VrchatAuthManager.fetchGroupName(groupId)` (`GET /groups/{id}` → `name`) and caches it — this fixes group **events** rendering "Event from a group" because event payloads frequently omit `senderUsername`. **Event/announcement timestamps**: `fireEventNotification` accepts `eventTimestampMs`; announcement paths pass the parsed `created_at`/`createdAt` so the in-app alert shows when the announcement was actually posted. **Calendar EVENTS show when they BEGIN, not when the series was created (tester-found "events show a really old date like 65d ago").** The old code used `createdAt` for the event display timestamp — but for a REPEATING event `createdAt` is when the SERIES was created (often months ago) even though each occurrence is upcoming, so it read "65d ago". Fixes: (1) `formatRelativeTime` (`VrchatPage.kt`) is now **future-aware** — a future timestamp renders "in 1d" instead of clamping to "just now" (the negative-delta bug that originally forced the `createdAt` workaround); (2) the display timestamp is now the event's scheduled **`startsAt`** (falling back to `createdAt` only if absent); (3) the shared **`buildCalendarEventBody(desc,title,startsMs,endsMs,createdMs)`** helper builds the alert body as `description` + **`Starts: <date·time>` / `Ends: <date·time>` / `Posted: <date>`** lines (device-local via `formatEventDateTime`/`formatEventDate`), so the alert surfaces made-time AND begin/end together. All THREE event paths use the helper + `startsAt` display: `fireGroupCalendarEvent` (REST sweep, reads `startsAt`/`endsAt` off the calendar object), the live WS notification-v2 handler, and `processV2Notification` (both pull `startsAt`/`endsAt` from the payload top-level or nested `data`/`details`). The change marker (re-fire detection) still tracks `startsAt` then `createdAt` so an edited start time re-fires. **Periodic group polling**: `startGroupAnnouncementPollLoop()` polls every 5 minutes independently of the WebSocket — fetches recent posts via `fetchGroupPosts()` and calendar events, AND re-sweeps pending `notification-v2` (events/announcements/role changes) via `processV2Notification()`. Its first poll runs ~45s after start (not a full 5 min) so catch-up surfaces quickly on reopen. **This is the retry path** for the one-shot connect backfill: that backfill makes 50+ REST calls rapidly and can miss items on a transient 429/timeout or a cold-start race, with no built-in retry — the poll loop re-attempts the V2 sweep every cycle so nothing made while the app was closed is lost. `processV2Notification(obj)` is the shared per-notification handler used by both the connect backfill and this retry sweep; `seenNotifIds` dedup makes re-processing safe (each fires at most once). The V2 sweep is skipped until `notif_backfill_initialized` is set (first-run baseline). This catches announcements that the WebSocket missed or delayed. Notification-v2 handler explicitly matches `post` types as announcements and `event`/`calendar` types as group events, with proper URLs (`/posts` for announcements, group page for events). Per-group announcement timestamps are persisted in DataStore (`notif_group_announcement_seen`) to avoid re-firing on reconnect. `seenNotifIds` set (**UNBOUNDED — no cap, no eviction, deliberate**) deduplicates between REST backfill and websocket replay — **persisted to SharedPreferences as a JSON array** (`vrca_seen_notifs/ids_ordered`, `commit()` not `apply()`) so IDs survive service restarts and prevent repeat notifications. **The "friend requests resend on every app restart" bug lived here (tester-found)**: the set was capped (500, later 4000) with eviction, and it holds EVERY dedup key (V1/V2 ids + `ann_fp_`/`evt_fp_` fingerprints + per-person `fr_` keys) — months of use filled it permanently, so every new id evicted an old one. Worse, the legacy `putStringSet` persistence is a HashSet: **insertion order was LOST on reload, so post-restart eviction removed effectively RANDOM entries** — including fresh `fr_` keys. A pending friend request is RE-LISTED by VRChat on every backfill until accepted/declined, so an evicted `fr_` key = the same request re-fired on the next restart/reconnect (and each fire appended another event to the in-app "Friend request (N)" card). **The fix is NO eviction at all** (per the user: cap removed entirely): membership alone decides — once an id is in, that notification can structurally never refire. Growth is human-rate (~40-60 chars per real notification; even years of heavy use is a few MB, loaded once at service create). One-time migration from the legacy `ids` StringSet. `persistSeenNotifIds` uses `commit()` because the swipe shutdown hard-kills the process without waiting for QueuedWork — an async `apply()` could lose the write and re-fire that notification next launch. **`addContentFingerprintToSeen`/`addEventFingerprintToSeen` persist immediately on genuine add** (they're not always followed by a `fireEventNotification` persist — an unpersisted fingerprint lost to a kill re-fired the announcement/event after restart, same bug class). Do NOT re-introduce a cap/eviction here — any eviction of a pending-state key recreates the refire bug. The admin-announcements set (`vrca_seen_announcements/ids`) is likewise uncapped, and its first-snapshot seed re-covers everything active on every restart without firing. **Ordering matters**: `fireEventNotification` checks the per-type toggle (`enabled`) BEFORE adding the dedup id to `seenNotifIds`. Adding it first would permanently poison the id if `enabled` read false transiently (e.g. DataStore not fully loaded during the cold-start connect/backfill race) — the notification would be dropped AND marked seen, so it could never fire on any later path. This was a cause of group announcements/events inconsistently never appearing on app open. REST calls are paced with 250-300ms delays; groups capped at 50 to stay under VRChat rate limits. Group announcement notifications include the group name in the title ("Announcement from GROUP_NAME"). Event notifications use calendar URLs (`/home/group/$groupId/calendar/$eventId`) when available. The `groupId` is extracted from `relatedGroupId`, `groupId`, `data.groupId`, `details.groupId`, or `senderUserId` (if `grp_` prefix), and the `eventId` from `eventId`, `data.eventId`, `details.eventId`, or `id` (if `cal_` prefix). As a final fallback BOTH fall back to `findIdWithPrefix(payload, "grp_"/"cal_")` — a recursive scanner that walks the entire notification-v2 JSON (including nested `data`/`details` blobs and embedded URLs) for the first value with that prefix. This is what makes event notifications reliably link to the calendar URL (`/home/group/grp_xxx/calendar/cal_xxx`) instead of falling back to `/home/notifications`. The notification-v2 `when` block has an **`else` catch-all** that logs the unmatched `type` string at INFO and still fires a generic group notification (gated by `KEY_NOTIF_GROUP_EVENT`) — so no notification-v2 is ever silently dropped just because VRChat used an unexpected type string. All handlers (WebSocket, poll loop, backfill) share `seenNotifIds` for dedup — WebSocket events now pass `dedupId = notifId` to prevent duplicates between real-time and polling. Each endpoint is wrapped in try/catch — failures are logged and skipped for this connect cycle. Bio/name change detection also runs on reconnect by diffing the old `friendsCache` against fresh API data before the cache is overwritten.
  - **First-run baseline guard**: A `notif_backfill_initialized` DataStore boolean blocks the first-ever backfill from firing anything. On the first pipeline connect, `seedBackfillBaseline()` walks user groups and silently records recent post IDs and calendar event IDs into `notif_group_announcement_seen`, then pre-seeds `seenNotifIds` with all V1 and V2 notification IDs (without firing), then sets both `notif_backfill_initialized` and `posts_events_baseline_v2`. This prevents both historical group announcements and pending friend requests/invites from firing on first install. From the second connect onward, the regular backfill flow runs and fires only events that arrived after setup.
  - **Newly-joined-group silent baseline (`seedGroupBaselineSilently` + `__known` markers)**: the first-run baseline only covers the groups the user was in AT first launch. A group **joined later** had none of its pre-existing posts/events baselined, so the backfill + 5-min poll swept them all as "new" and fired old announcements/events as notifications on join (tester-reported). Fix: a persisted **known-groups set** stored as reserved keys INSIDE the `notif_group_announcement_seen` map — `__known_groups_init` (the set-initialized flag) + `${groupId}__known` per group. The FIRST sweep on this version records every current group as known WITHOUT seeding (so existing groups still fire genuinely-missed items on upgrade — nothing is swallowed), then any group whose `__known` marker is absent is one joined LATER, so `seedGroupBaselineSilently(groupId, seenMap, updatedMap)` records ALL its current posts + calendar events into the seen map (and their `ann_fp_`/`evt_fp_` fingerprints into `seenNotifIds`) WITHOUT firing, marks it known, and `continue`s (skips firing that group this cycle). Wired into BOTH `pollGroupAnnouncements` and `backfillOfflineNotifications` (shared guard). After a group is baselined once, only content created after joining surfaces. The reserved keys are safe: the seen map is only ever read via `.optString(specificKey)`, never iterated as `groupId_post_`/`groupId_event_`.
  - **Expandable notifications**: Status change, rename, and bio update notifications use `BigTextStyle`. Expanding shows before/after comparison text.
  - **Notification click actions**: Invite, group, and friend offline notifications open relevant VRChat web pages (`vrchat.com/home/notifications`, `/home/group/$groupId`, `/home/user/$userId`) instead of just opening the app. Group v2 notifications fall back to `vrchat.com/home/notifications` when `groupId` is missing from the payload.
  - **Notification-tap ACTIONS (`NotificationActionReceiver`)**: world-invite and invite-request notifications perform a VRChat REST call on tap instead of opening a web page. `fireEventNotification` gained `tapActionType`/`tapActionData` — when set, the tap `PendingIntent` is a `getBroadcast` to `NotificationActionReceiver` (registered in the manifest, `exported=false`) instead of `ACTION_VIEW`. The receiver runs the call on `goAsync()`, Toasts the result, and cancels the notification id on success. Two action types, shared by the in-app buttons via `NotificationActionReceiver.perform(ctx, type, data)`: **`invite_me`** (`data` = the invited instance `{worldId}:{instanceId}` location → `VrchatAuthManager.inviteSelfToInstance`, re-invites you so you can join) and **`invite_user`** (`data` = the requester's userId → `VrchatAuthManager.inviteUserToInstance(uid, location)` where `location` is read LIVE from `VrchatPipelineState.presence?.location` at tap time, inviting the requester to your current instance). `inviteUserToInstance` = `POST /invite/{userId}` with `{instanceId}`. **Both invite methods (and `inviteSelfToInstance`) return `VrchatAuthManager.InviteResult(ok, error)`** — on failure `error` carries VRChat's own reason parsed from the response body (`error.message`/`message`, quote-trimmed; falls back to "That instance has closed or no longer exists" on 404, "not accessible" on 403). The receiver/in-app Toast and the admin "Invite me" result surface this verbatim so the user is told WHY (instance closed/0-people/full or invite/invite+ not accessible) instead of a generic failure. World-invite extracts the location from the invite `details` (`worldId` if it contains `:`, else `worldId:instanceId`); if it can't parse a `wrld_` location it falls back to the old `home/notifications` web tap. The same action wiring is mirrored in the offline V1 backfill.
  - **In-app coverage (every type)**: friend request + new friend collapse into ONE per-person card (shared `friend_$userId` alertGroupKey); unfriend (`unfriend_$userId`), trust-rank (`rank_$userId`, text "X is now **a** Trusted User" — the article reads better), VRChat message (`msg_$senderUserId`, or `msg_$notifId` for System/no-sender messages), and ALL group notifications (keyed `group*_$baseId`). **Invite fusing (two distinct modes)**: **invite requests** fuse PER-PERSON (`invreq_$senderUserId`) as a **single-event** group (`alertSingleEvent=true` → only the latest is kept in-app, no "(N)" count) — you only need to know a person is asking, not every request. **World invites** fuse PER-INVITER (`worldinvite_$senderUserId`, like bios) as an **accumulating** group keeping the full history, with **same-instance dedup** (`alertDedupByActionData=true` → repeat invites to the SAME instance location collapse to one entry; different instances stay separate). Both `alertSingleEvent` and `alertDedupByActionData` are `fireEventNotification` params threaded into `InAppAlertState.addGroupedEvent`. The generic `group.*` catch-all always shows (no `message.length > 100` gate). **Filter chips**: `isGroupAlert()` routes `announcement_`/`event_`/`group*` to "Groups"; the rest to "Friends"; `bio_` to "Bio".
  - **In-app action symbols + instance picker (`VrchatPage.kt`)**: per-event full-width action buttons were REPLACED by **compact circular symbol buttons in the card header**, sitting next to the collapse (chevron) and dismiss (X) affordances and matching their style (`HeaderActionSymbol`, 34dp tinted `Surface`). Per group the header shows: an **invite symbol** (↪ `Login` for invite-me / ＋ `PersonAdd` for invite-them) when the group has invite actions, and **one** "Open in VRChat" symbol (⧉ `OpenInNew`) for the single url shared by all events. **Open dedup**: friend-request+new-friend and bio/name/rank history all point at the same profile url, so they show ONE header open symbol instead of repeating it per event; groups with DISTINCT per-event urls (separate group posts/events) keep per-event "Open in VRChat" buttons in the body via `showPerEventOpen` (`= sharedOpenUrl == null`). **Invite symbol tap behavior**: invite-request (single) → instant `inviteUserToInstance`; world-invite with ONE distinct instance → instant `inviteSelfToInstance`; world-invite with MULTIPLE distinct instances (same person invited you to several) → opens **`InstanceListDialog`** (the instance picker). `CompactAlertButton` (38dp full-width tonal) remains for the body per-event opens and the dialog rows. `AlertActionButtons` was deleted. `InAppAlertEvent` carries `actionType`/`actionData` (persisted in the alerts JSON).
  - **`InstanceListDialog` + 24h instance history (`VrchatPage.kt`, all LOCAL — zero Firestore)**: a reusable app-styled picker listing instances with world image (Coil via `VrchatImageLoader`, session-authed), live occupancy, and a joinability status chip, plus a per-row **Invite** button (`inviteSelfToInstance`). Instance info is fetched FRESH every time the dialog opens (`VrchatAuthManager.fetchInstanceInfo(loc)` → `InstanceInfo(worldName, worldImageUrl, players, capacity, status)`; `InstanceStatus` from the HTTP code: 200=OPEN, 404=CLOSED/dead, 403=INACCESSIBLE), so counts/closed status are never stale. Used by (a) the world-invite multi-instance picker above, and (b) the **Instance History** chip in the VRChat-tab identity header: `InstanceHistoryStore` (`com.vrca.vrchat`, SharedPreferences `vrca_instance_history`) records every instance the user enters (full location WITH the `~nonce` access token, captured from the self `user-location` event in `applySelfLocationEvent`) along with **join/left times** (`joinedMs`/`leftMs`; `leftMs==0` = still in it; `markCurrentLeft()` closes it out when leaving) so the user can re-invite themselves to anywhere they've been in the past 24h and cross-reference what they played and when. **"Still here" is derived from the LIVE `VrchatPipelineState.presence` (the SAME signal the Discord RPC reads), NOT from the stored `leftMs` flag**: the dialog labels the newest open session "Still here" only while `presence.location == entry.location` (keyed on the live location so it recomputes reactively) — the instant presence leaves that world (offline / traveling / hopped) the label drops, regardless of whether `markCurrentLeft` fired. This was the "Still here never changes when I get off VRChat" fix — the old code trusted only `leftMs`, which depended on a `markCurrentLeft` signal that didn't always land. `markCurrentLeft` is ALSO now broadened on the service side: both `applySelfLocationEvent` and `syncPresenceToFirestore` close the open session on ANY world→non-world transition (offline / traveling / blank, excluding the redacted "private" which means still-in-instance), not just `location=="offline"`, so the stored "Left HH:MM" time is accurate too. Entries left >24h ago self-prune on every read/write; an instance still occupied never prunes. The nonce lives ONLY on-device (never synced to Firestore — like the user's own cookie). All of this costs **zero Firestore reads/writes**; the only network cost is VRChat REST (instance-info on dialog open, invite on tap), on the user's own session. **Pre-stored thumbnail + type at ENTRY time (so a cold history open never looks bad)**: `Entry` carries `imageUrl` + `instanceType`, grabbed the MOMENT the user enters an instance — the single `GET /instances/{loc}` that already fires on every self world-hop in `applySelfLocationEvent` (for the occupancy count) now uses `VrchatAuthManager.fetchInstanceInfo` (the SAME one request + `extractInstanceUserCount` count logic, just a richer parse) instead of `fetchInstanceCount`, so that ONE already-made call ALSO reads the world thumbnail + API instance `type` and writes them onto the history entry via `InstanceHistoryStore.updateInfo(...)`, and caches the image bytes via `AlertImageStore.ensureCached`. **Zero extra network cost** — it's the same call returning more of the response (only patches presence count when `status==OPEN`; only stores non-blank image/type so a CLOSED/INACCESSIBLE reply never wipes a prior value). Opening Instance History later renders the image + type chip INSTANTLY from the stored entry (`InstanceTarget.imageUrl`/`instanceType`, the latter fed into `parseInstanceType` as `apiType` with the location-string derivation as fallback) instead of fetching everything on the spot; the picker still live-refreshes occupancy and re-persists the freshest world-name/image/type via `updateInfo`. `updateImage` is now a thin wrapper over `updateInfo`. When the entry ages out of the 24h window and is pruned, `AlertImageStore.gc()` sees its `imageUrl` reference vanish and deletes the cached file — so the image clears with the entry.
  - **Rich event/announcement alerts (`AlertEventBody` in `VrchatPage.kt` + `AlertRichMeta`/rich fields on `InAppAlertEvent`)**: group-event and announcement alert cards render structured content instead of a text blob. Events show: a full-width **banner at the image's OWN intrinsic ratio** (`ContentScale.FillWidth` + wrapped height — creators upload arbitrary ratios, so ANY fixed-ratio Crop box cut edges; tester confirmed cropping twice, first at 21:9 then at 1200:630 — do NOT reintroduce a fixed-ratio crop). **Overlays ride the banner, identity-header style**: **language chips top-right in a right-aligned STACK** (`LanguageChip`; a horizontal row overflowed on long native sign names; native names via `vrcLanguageNames`/`nativeLanguageName()` — "日本語, English, 日本手話" EXACTLY as VRChat's site renders them; **tapping a chip shows the device-language translation (`deviceLanguageName()` — "Japanese Sign Language" on an English phone, VRChat's in-client localization style) in place for 5s then reverts** (`LanguageChip` is CONTROLLED — `AlertEventBody` hoists a single `translatedLang` + `langTapTick`, so only ONE chip is translated at a time and tapping another instantly reverts the previous even mid-window; re-tap restarts the 5s; sign languages translate to English names since Locale can't resolve them); the earlier `Locale.displayLanguage` localized names to the DEVICE language so "English" showed as 英語 on a Japanese-locale phone, and sign tags were wrongly shortened to "JSL" — VRChat's real names are `jsl`→日本手話, `ase`→American Sign Language, `kvk`→한국수어 etc.; map first, then native-Locale resolution, then uppercased code) and a **bottom scrim row** (black vertical-gradient) carrying the status/category/access chips (dark `solid` `EventMetaChip` variant) + **platform SYMBOLS** (`PlatformSymbols`: circular tinted BRAND glyphs matching VRChat's badges — Windows flag blue (`ic_platform_windows.xml`) / Android robot green (`Icons.Filled.Android`) / Apple mark light (`ic_platform_apple.xml`); a monitor/phone glyph read as wrong per user reference). **All chips (`EventMetaChip`/`LanguageChip`) are a FIXED 22dp height with centered single-line text** — CJK glyphs are taller than Latin, so padding-based chips rendered at different sizes and looked ragged in mixed-language rows. **Tappable chips (access chip's group link, language chips) use `Surface` + `Modifier.clickable`, NOT `Surface(onClick=...)`** — a clickable Surface enforces Material3's 48dp minimum interactive size, which padded each tappable chip to 48dp tall and blew a huge invisible vertical gap between the chip rows (tester-reported "big gap that looks weird"); `clickable` keeps the chip its true height. No banner → **TWO tight rows in a `Column(spacedBy(6.dp))`**: (1) a `FlowRow` with the status/category/access chips (wrapped in ONE inner `Row` so they're inseparable — as bare FlowRow children it split them, wrapping "Group" off on its own) + platform symbols on the same line; (2) a `FlowRow` of language chips directly below. Languages in their OWN row (not the same FlowRow as the chips) is what removed the big vertical gap the single-FlowRow wrap left. A thin `Divider` separates the meta block from the description. `richChips(solid)` is the single shared chip definition; `ExperimentalLayoutApi` for `FlowRow`. Chips: **status** ("Starts in 2h" primary / amber when <2h → "**Live now**" green while `startsAtMs ≤ now ≤ endsAtMs` → "**Ended**"; no `endsAt` = live for 4h after start, via `eventPhase()`), **category** (`prettyEventCategory`: `film_media` → "Film & Media", generic underscore→Title Case fallback), a **recurring indicator** (`InAppAlertEvent.recurring`, best-effort from `GroupAlertEnricher.extractRecurring`) — placed on the banner's empty **top-left** corner when there's a thumbnail, else appended to the muted meta line ("... · ↻ Repeats"); deliberately NOT a status-row chip (it crowded that row). **Recurring-flood collapse**: VRChat writes EVERY day of a repeating event as its own calendar event (a daily repeat = up to 50 alerts in one `event_$groupId` group). `collapseRecurring()` folds same-title events in a group down to the **nearest UPCOMING occurrence** (or most-recent past if none upcoming) at DISPLAY time in `AlertGroupCard` — so the card shows ONE, the "(N)" count is the collapsed count, and the header preview uses the SOONEST event (not the farthest, which read "in 48d"). It's retroactive (fixes cards that already flooded) and self-advancing (as an occurrence passes, the collapse picks the next from the stored occurrences). The Android notification was already one per group (`setOnlyAlertOnce` on the shared `alertGroupKey` id), **access chip** (Public green 0xFF4CAF50, Group purple 0xFFAB47BC) which **IS the tap target that opens the hosting group's web page** (per user feedback: the CHIP opens the group, NOT the card title — a title-click attempt was reverted). Below the meta line, a **"Hosted by <name>"** one-liner where the name is primary-tinted and clickable → the organizer's VRChat profile (`organizerId`/`organizerName`; `extractOrganizerId` probes owner/author/creator fields, name resolved once via `VrchatAuthManager.fetchUserDisplayName` and cached in the enricher). Below: description in `bodySmall`, a compact **one-line timing row** (Schedule icon + "Jul 5 · 3:05 PM → Jul 8 · 5:05 PM", `eventDateTime` has NO year), a **muted meta line** ("1 interested · Posted Jul 4, 2026" — `eventDate` keeps the year), NO relative-time footer on rich events (the chip + timing line already say when; plain alerts keep it), and **phase-appropriate actions**: UPCOMING → **Add to Calendar / Remove from Calendar** (`VrchatAuthManager.setCalendarEventFollowing` → `POST /calendar/{groupId}/{eventId}/follow` `{"isFollowing":bool}` — community-documented endpoint, failures surface VRChat's own error; success optimistically updates `following` via `InAppAlertState.enrichEvents`, AND schedules a **local reminder** (`EventReminderScheduler` — an inexact `AlarmManager.setAndAllowWhileIdle` alarm ~10 min before `startsAt`, no exact-alarm permission; `EventReminderReceiver` posts the "Event starting soon" notification; persisted to `vrca_event_reminders` prefs and re-armed on reboot via `BootReceiver.rescheduleAll`; Remove-from-Calendar cancels it). **Signed-up events (following==true) golden-pin to the top**: `VrchatStatusPage.inAppAlertSection` partitions the alert groups into a "★ Signed up Events" section (gold sub-header, `AlertGroupCard(signedUp=true)` → gold accent bar + gold border + faint gold container tint + a "★ Going" pill by the title) pinned above an "Other notifications" section; **Splitting is at the EVENT level, not the group, but COLLAPSED per SERIES**: a group can have some events the user signed up for and some they didn't, so the FOLLOWED events become cards in "Signed up Events" while the remaining unfollowed events stay grouped in Pinned (if the group is manually pinned) or Other. **Followed events are grouped by SERIES (normalized title) into ONE card per series** (`bySeries` map → `su_<groupId>_<seriesKey>`, the card's own `collapseRecurring` picks the nearest upcoming occurrence + shows the count) — a recurring event the user signed up for has ~50 occurrences each independently `following==true`, which without this collapse spawned ~50 identical "Going" cards (tester-found flood). Un-following an event drops it back into the remainder group; dismissing a remainder card uses `dismissEvents` (removes only its unfollowed events, so the followed ones shown separately survive). **SHORT-WINDOW per-series follow override (`followSeriesKey`/`recordFollowToggle`/`followOverride`, in `InAppAlertState`, in-memory)**: tapping Add/Remove records the user's decision keyed by SERIES (normalized title, or `id:<evId>` when titleless); the enricher's `following` read consults `followOverride(followSeriesKey(...))` FIRST, but the override lasts only `FOLLOW_OVERRIDE_MS` = **30s** — just long enough to cover VRChat's follow-propagation delay so an in-flight enrich can't flip a just-toggled event back. **After the window the SERVER's `userInterest.isFollowing` is authoritative**, so a follow/unfollow made on the **WEBSITE or in the VRChat CLIENT** is picked up by the 10s expanded poll / 60s tab loop. (The override was briefly made PERMANENT+persisted as a workaround for the old `extractEventFollowing` bug that reported EVERY event as followed — but permanence blocked external follow changes from ever showing, and with the follow field now read correctly (`userInterest.isFollowing`) there's no flip-back to guard against beyond the propagation window.) The optimistic UI update (`applyFollowToSeries`) covers every occurrence of the series at once, and a failed API call reverts both the override and the display. **Signed-up cards are NOT dismissable** — the per-card X is hidden and `InAppAlertState.dismissAll` preserves them (Remove-from-Calendar un-pins → the X returns), so a user can't accidentally delete an event they're going to. **Manual "📌 Pinned" section (universal, any alert type)**: below "★ Signed up Events" sits a **collapsible** "📌 Pinned (N)" section for groups the user manually pinned (`InAppAlertGroup.pinned`, persisted). A **tiny pin toggle** sits next to the "ago" row in EVERY card (both collapsed and expanded states) — filled gold when pinned, outline otherwise — **EXCEPT signed-up (Added-to-Calendar) cards, where the pin is hidden** (they auto-sort into "Going", so a manual pin fought the auto-sort; **Add-to-Calendar also auto-unpins** via `setPinned(false)`). `InAppAlertState.setPinned` caps manual pins at **`MAX_PINNED` = 10** (over-cap → a toast, no-op). **Per-event dismiss**: a multi-event card shows a small X per event (title row / "Change N" row) → `InAppAlertState.dismissEvent` removes just that event (a recurring series' X clears the whole series; emptying the group removes it). The AnimatedVisibility event list is wrapped in `key(displayEvents ids)` so a SECOND event fusing into a group actually repaints (the content lambda otherwise skips — same cycle-mute class). Pinned groups get the SAME gold treatment + a "📌 Pinned" pill, are NOT dismissable (X hidden + `dismissAll` preserves `pinned || following`), and un-pinning (tap the pin again) drops them back to "Other notifications". The Pinned section's collapse state is `PinnedSectionState.expanded` — the SAME process-scoped model as `AlertSectionState` (survives tab switches, resets to expanded on a cold process start). The three partitions (signed-up / pinned-not-signed-up / rest) are disjoint (signed-up wins) so a group renders once; keys stay the plain groupId so expand state survives partition moves. `following` is read from the calendar object via `extractEventFollowing` (probes `isFollowing`/`isInterested`/`isAttending`/... booleans + `userInterest`) so an event added IN-GAME shows as signed-up and its button reads "Remove from Calendar" instead of wrongly offering "Add" — best-effort pending confirmation of VRChat's exact field name); LIVE → **Join event** (fetches `VrchatAuthManager.fetchGroupInstances` = `GET /groups/{id}/instances` — VRChat doesn't link events to instances, so ALL the hosting group's open instances are listed in the SAME `InstanceListDialog` the multi-invite picker uses, with an `emptyText` explaining when the group has none open); ENDED → no action. Announcements get the banner (group-post `imageUrl`), **"Visit <host>" link buttons** for URLs in the body (`extractBodyUrls`, max 3), and **read-more** collapse past 320 chars. **Announcements from a group that currently has an OPEN instance also get a "See active instances" button** (same `InstanceListDialog`/`fetchGroupInstances` picker as a LIVE event's "Join event", shared `onJoinEvent`/`openJoinPicker`, dialog title neutralised to "Active instances · <group>").
