The button shows ONLY when the group has ≥1 open instance — `AlertEventBody` fires ONE `fetchGroupInstances` check on expand (this body only renders while the card is expanded, so it's one lightweight GET per opened announcement, not per row) and stores `announcementInstanceCount`. Per-event `remember` state is wrapped in `key(event.id)`. **The shared alert-card header title (`AlertGroupCard` `displayTitle`) is `maxLines = 2`** (was 1) — long notification titles ("Announcement from <long group name>", etc.) were ellipsis-cut on EVERY alert type since the header is shared; two lines before ellipsizing handles it.
  - **Event identity = `seriesId`, not title (`InAppAlertEvent.seriesId`); re-surface dismissed follows; "Removed" state**: three coupled fixes to event alerts. (1) **Stable series identity** — `InAppAlertEvent` gained `seriesId` (VRChat's per-series id; every occurrence of a repeat shares it, a one-off has its own), populated at the fire paths (`fireGroupCalendarEvent`/`fireDetailedCalendarEvent` via `AlertRichMeta.seriesId` = `GroupAlertEnricher.extractSeriesId`) and by the enricher, and persisted. The **display collapse** (`collapseRecurring`), the **signed-up `bySeries` partition**, and the **enricher match/transform** now key on `seriesId` FIRST (title only as a fallback for cards with no seriesId yet). This fixes "two DIFFERENT repeating events override/mix each other's thumbnail/languages/description/title and the card flip-flops between them" — title is not a unique identity (distinct series collided, edits changed it, the enricher's title clause cross-wrote one series' fields onto another). The thin v2 fire paths ALSO try to pull `seriesId` from the push (`content`/`data`/`details`, same as `startsAt`) so a series-carrying push groups correctly on first fire; when the trimmed payload omits it, seriesId is null and the targeted enrich sweep fills it within seconds. (2) **Re-surface a swiped followed event — POST-FIRE ONLY** — the signed-up section derives purely from `following==true` events in `_groups`, so an event dismissed from the in-app list (while `following` was still null, before enrichment confirmed it) vanished from "Going" (the enricher only UPDATES existing cards). When the enricher's server follow-read (`userInterest.isFollowing`, which reflects website/PC/headset subscribes) says `following==true` and `InAppAlertState.hasSeriesCard(...)` is false, it silently re-adds the card (`readdFollowedEventIfMissing`, no Android notification). **CRITICAL: this only runs on the POST-FIRE targeted sweep (`enrich(..., allowResurface=true)` from `scheduleTargetedGroupSweep`), NOT the routine periodic tab-loop/expanded sweeps (`allowResurface=false`, the default).** VRChat reports the user's OWN created events as `following==true` FOREVER, so a periodic re-surface resurrected every dismissed self-created event on every VRChat-tab open (tester-found "old dismissed events keep reappearing in Going"). Gating to the post-fire window keeps the genuine "you subscribed/created and swiped it away too fast" case while never fighting a deliberate dismissal of an old event. Remove-from-Calendar sets `following=false` so an intentional un-follow is also never fought; repeatable and per-series. (3) **Deletion → "Removed"; ENDED → pruned** — two cleanup mechanisms. **Ended prune** (`InAppAlertState.pruneEndedEvents(ctx, now)`, timestamp-only, no network): removes events whose end has passed (`eventEnded` = past `endsAtMs`, or >4h past `startsAtMs` with no end) so concluded events stop cluttering "Going"/the notifications area (a recurring series sheds past occurrences, keeps upcoming ones; `removed` red cards are KEPT; non-event alerts untouched). Run on VRChat-tab entry + every 60s in the tab loop. **Deletion → red "Removed" card** (`InAppAlertEvent.removed`, persisted): the enricher's `detectRemovedEvents` trusts the AUTHORITATIVE group calendar LIST. **`fetchGroupCalendarEvents` now returns `[]` (not null) for an emptied group** — the canonical `/calendar/{groupId}` endpoint's 200-with-`results:[]` is returned as an empty array instead of falling through to the legacy 404 endpoints and returning null, so "group has zero events" is finally distinguishable from "fetch failed" (null). Detection runs ONLY when the list is non-null (a null list = network error → skip, so a blip can't false-flag). Detection asks VRChat **DIRECTLY per event id** (`fetchCalendarEventResult` = `GET /calendar/{g}/{eventId}` → **404/410 = deleted**, 200 = alive) for every not-yet-removed, non-ended, signed-up-or-upcoming card — the PRIMARY signal, NOT gated on list-presence. Gating on list-absence (a first attempt) was the "second-deleted event never flags" bug: if VRChat's group calendar LIST lags and still shows the just-deleted event, that event was marked "present" and never even 404-checked. Going straight off the event's own 404 sidesteps the list entirely. Blip protection: a network/429/5xx reply is UNKNOWN and does NOT flag on its own; only when the direct check is inconclusive does it FALL BACK to the list — absent there for **`ABSENT_STRIKES_TO_REMOVE` = 2 consecutive sweeps** (`absentStrikes` in-memory counter) also flags, while a definitive 200 or list-presence resets the strike. The direct checks are bounded (≤10/sweep). List fetched at n=100 (fallback only). `markSeriesRemoved` sets `removed=true`+`following=false`. ENDED events are skipped (pruned, not flagged — the red state is for a cancelled event you might still attend). (`fetchGroupCalendarEvents` still returns `[]` not null for an emptied group so the list fallback can distinguish "zero events" from "fetch failed".) A removed event drops out of "Going", becomes its own **red** card (`rm_<groupId>_<series>`, `AlertGroupCard(removed=true)` → red accent bar/border/tint + "Removed" pill) in the normal area, body shows "This event was removed from VRChat" instead of Add/Join/Remove, and it's **dismissable**. **`removed` is STICKY** — the enricher deliberately does NOT clear it on a live-representative match (a title-fallback match could otherwise un-remove a deleted same-title sibling series); a genuinely recreated event gets a fresh id → new card. Won't be re-surfaced by (2) since `following` is cleared AND re-surface is post-fire-only.
  - **Repeats dialog — per-occurrence subscribe (`EventOccurrencesDialog` + `EventSeriesStore`)**: tapping the **"↻ Repeats"** indicator on a recurring event card (clickable when `recurring && seriesId != null && groupRefId != null`) opens a picker (styled like `InstanceListDialog`) listing **every occurrence** of the series with its start date/time + a per-occurrence **Subscribe/Going toggle** — VRChat tracks follow **per occurrence** (`cal_` id), so this matches the real model (`setCalendarEventFollowing(groupId, occurrenceId, following)`). **`EventSeriesStore` (`com.vrca.vrchat`, SharedPreferences `vrca_event_series`)** persists every occurrence per `groupId|seriesId` (`{id, startsAt, endsAt, following, deleted}`) until it ENDS (pruned) or is DELETED (flagged red "Removed", kept 24h, then pruned). **Cost model**: occurrence existence + timing + the **deletion diff** come FREE from the group calendar list the enricher already fetches (`reconcileOccurrenceStore` in `GroupAlertEnricher.enrichLocked` — groups the raw list by seriesId, reconciles the store, flags stored-but-absent occurrences deleted **only within the n=100 window** via `maxLiveStart` so a far-future occurrence isn't false-flagged). Only per-occurrence FOLLOW state costs a call (the list omits it), so `following` starts null and is filled **lazily**: the representative's follow is written free by the enricher (already fetched); the dialog fetches the rest **on open**, paced (300ms), nearest-first, capped 20, skipping any the user is actively toggling. In-app subscribes write the store instantly + fire the API optimistically (revert on failure). **Card aggregate "Going"**: a recurring card golden-pins when `ev.following == true` OR `EventSeriesStore.anySubscribed(...)` (subscribed to ≥1 live occurrence) — the partition's `isGoing()` in `VrchatStatusPage.inAppAlertSection` — so subscribing to any occurrence in the dialog lights the one series card in "Signed up Events". **Roll-forward when the shown occurrence ends (`pruneEndedEvents` → `advanceRecurringEvent`)**: the alert stores only ONE fired occurrence per series, and the ended-event prune ran BEFORE the enricher could re-point it, so a recurring card used to VANISH when its shown occurrence ended instead of switching to the next date. Now the prune, on finding a recurring event whose occurrence ended, re-points the card to the series' **next upcoming occurrence** from `EventSeriesStore.nextUpcoming(...)` (updates `eventRefId`/`startsAt`/`endsAt`/`timestamp`/`following`, preserving title/banner) instead of removing it; only a CONCLUDED series (no upcoming occurrence in the store) or a non-recurring event drops. The store is populated by the enricher over the card's lifetime, so by the time an occurrence ends it's virtually always present; if it somehow isn't, the fallback is the old behavior (drop) — no regression. **DELETION of the shown occurrence also rolls forward, not just ENDING** (`GroupAlertEnricher.removeOrAdvance`): when `detectRemovedEvents` confirms the card's occurrence is deleted (404), for a RECURRING series it marks that occurrence deleted in the store, then `InAppAlertState.advanceSeriesIfUpcoming` re-points the card to the next upcoming occurrence (clearing `removed`) — only a one-off, or a series with NO upcoming occurrence left, falls through to `markSeriesRemoved` (the red card). `reconcileOccurrenceStore` runs BEFORE `detectRemovedEvents` in `enrichLocked` so `nextUpcoming` already excludes the just-deleted occurrence. This also stops the card from collapsing on the delete (it stays in its section instead of moving to "Removed", so its `key` — and expand state — survive). **Dialog survives enrichment (`occurrencesFor` hoisted to `AlertGroupCard`)**: the Repeats dialog's open-state used to live inside `AlertEventBody`, which is wrapped in `key(event.id, event)` — so every enrichment tick that changed the event data disposed+recreated the body and slammed the dialog shut. It now lives in `AlertGroupCard` (card scope, keyed by the stable `groupId`) and `AlertEventBody` opens it via an `onShowOccurrences` callback, so an enrich sweep no longer closes it. A fully-deleted series shows a "removed from VRChat" notice. **Instance history reuse**: `InstanceListDialog` gained a `liveRefresh` param — the invite/join pickers keep the 15s occupancy re-fetch loop, but Instance History passes `liveRefresh=false` for a ONE-TIME grab (no continuous re-fetch), cutting its calls to a single pass.
  - **Thin-payload enrichment + live refresh (`GroupAlertEnricher`, `com.vrca.vrchat`)**: notification-v2 pushes carry far less than the calendar object (no banner, often no timing) AND an alert used to freeze at fire-time data (interested count never moved, event edits never appeared). `GroupAlertEnricher.enrich(ctx, groupId)` is the shared ONE-GROUP REST sweep (calendar events + posts, 2 calls, debounced 60s per group inside the enricher) that **merges fresh values into the fired alerts in place** via `InAppAlertState.enrichEvents` (events matched by `cal_` id, announcements by normalized-body == the content-fingerprint normalization): banner, start/end, created, interested count, category/platforms/access/**languages**, `following`, the event's REAL title (**strips VRChat's "New event by X:" v2 boilerplate via `cleanEventTitle`**, also applied at v2 fire time), display timestamp → `startsAt`, body → clean description. **Every post-creation-editable event field (title, start/end, category, description, visibility, thumbnail, platform, languages) refreshes through this merge** — fresh non-blank values overwrite; languages is the one field an edit can EMPTY, so it's keyed on `ev.has("languages")` (present-but-empty CLEARS the stale value instead of keeping it). **In-game follow detection + representative collapse**: the group calendar LIST omits the user's per-event follow state, so an event added to the calendar IN-GAME wasn't detected. The enricher collapses the fetched events to ONE representative per series (`collapseEventsByTitle`, nearest-upcoming — bounds the per-event work), then does a **single-event fetch** (`VrchatAuthManager.fetchCalendarEvent` = `GET /calendar/{groupId}/{eventId}`) which carries the follow state + richer organizer/recurrence, and applies `following` from it **UNLESS the user set a persistent in-app override** (`InAppAlertState.followOverride(followSeriesKey(...))`, checked first — a user's own Add/Remove always wins over the server, which reports the creator's own events as perpetually followed). A recurring representative's enrich MATCHES ALL stored occurrences of its series (by normalized title) so following/interested propagate to every one. **CONFIRMED VRChat calendar-event field names (from an on-device raw-JSON capture via the Settings→Debug diagnostics, `VrcaEventDiag`)**: follow state is the NESTED `userInterest.isFollowing` boolean (`extractEventFollowing` reads it — the old "`userInterest` present ⇒ following" test reported EVERY event as signed-up, since `userInterest` is always a non-null object); recurrence is `seriesId` (non-null) + `occurrenceKind=="occurrence"` (a one-off is `"single"`); interested count is `interestedUserCount`; banner is `imageUrl`. **Events have NO individual creator** — `ownerId` is the owning GROUP (`grp_`), not a person, so events show no "Hosted by" line (the group is already the access chip). **Posts/announcements DO carry `authorId` (a `usr_`)** — the announcement enrich path resolves it via `fetchUserDisplayName` (cached) and shows **"Posted by <name>"** (label is "Hosted by" for events / "Posted by" for announcements, keyed on `isRichEvent`). **`collapseEventsByTitle` keys on `seriesId ?: normalizedTitle`** (every occurrence of a repeat shares both), so a flooded recurring series collapses to ONE representative → ONE single-event fetch (fixes "repeat events take ages to load their info because the app grabs them all"). **It must NOT be keyed on `occurrenceKind`**: the calendar LIST endpoint reports that field unreliably (the confirmed value is only on the single-event fetch), and a brief attempt to give `occurrenceKind=="single"` list items their OWN bucket REGRESSED the collapse — every occurrence became unique, so the whole series flooded the fire path again AND the enricher went back to ~50 single-event fetches. The one-off-falsely-flagged-recurring concern lives in `extractRecurring` (the chip), not the collapse. Three callers: (1) `VrchatPipelineService.scheduleTargetedGroupSweep` (retries at 2.5s/8.5s/23s with `minIntervalMs=0` so a genuine new event enriches even before VRChat has indexed it, and a second event right after a first isn't debounced away); (2) **`VrchatStatusPage` runs a refresh loop while the tab is composed** (first pass 5s after entry, then every 60s, capped at 6 distinct `groupRefId`s from visible `event_grp_`/`announcement_grp_` alert groups) so interested counts and event edits UPDATE while the notification is on screen; (3) **while an event/announcement card stays EXPANDED it re-enriches every 10s** (`LaunchedEffect(expanded)` in `AlertGroupCard` LOOPS with `delay(10_000)` + `minIntervalMs = 9_000` — the ~9s per-group min lets each 10s tick through while the 55s tab loop mostly no-ops for that group; the effect is cancelled the instant it collapses, so the loop stops with it). This keeps the interested count / timing / banner / follow state / edits live on-screen (fixes "the interested counter is inconsistent with updating"). **Rate-limit guards, two layers**: the per-group debounce (atomic check+stamp via `compute`) caps each group to one sweep per window, AND a **global `sweepMutex` + 2.5s spacing** serializes sweeps ACROSS groups — spamming expand across cards from several different groups queues one 2-call sweep per 2.5s max instead of firing an unspaced burst (the 250ms intra-sweep pacing matches the backfill's). A multi-event card needs only ONE sweep: all its events share the group, and the single calendar response enriches each by its `cal_` id. **Repaint requirement: `AlertEventBody` is wrapped in `key(event.id, event)` — keyed on the FULL event data.** The card body sits inside `AnimatedVisibility`, which (proven on-device, same class as the cycle-mute saga) can skip repainting children whose params changed — keyed on id alone, enrichment updated the state but the expanded card never visually changed ("re-expanding doesn't update"). Do not reduce the key back to just the id. Enrichment NEVER fires a notification — display-state only. The rich fields ride `fireEventNotification(alertRich = AlertRichMeta(...))` from the REST calendar sweep (full meta at fire time, helpers now on `GroupAlertEnricher`) and the v2 paths. **Instant-detail live event fire (the "event notification shows a thin title-only card, then ~1 min later a SECOND full card appears in Signed up" fix)**: the live notification-v2 EVENT push used to fire a THIN card (no banner, no `following`) because a freshly-created event's push often carries no `cal_` id — so (a) the enricher couldn't match it and (b) the REST poll fired a separate full card that, being `following==true` for the creator, golden-pinned to Signed up while the thin one lingered. The v2 event branch now **fetches the full calendar object BEFORE firing** (`VrchatPipelineService.resolveFullCalendarEvent` → `fireDetailedCalendarEvent`) so its FIRST card is already detailed (banner/timing/interested/`following`, instantly pinning a self-created event to Signed up). `resolveFullCalendarEvent` prefers the single-event fetch (`fetchCalendarEvent` by `cal_` id — carries `userInterest.isFollowing`), else matches the group calendar by normalized title (nearest-upcoming occurrence for a recurring series) then upgrades to the single event, with one 1.2s retry for VRChat backend indexing lag; on total failure it falls back to the OLD thin fire + `scheduleTargetedGroupSweep` (the sweep then upgrades that thin card in place). This runs on `pipelineDispatcher` but its REST calls `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)`, so it yields the dispatcher thread (doesn't block other WS handlers) and touches no `friendsCache` across the suspension. **Cross-path DUPLICATE prevention is keyed on TITLE as well as the `cal_` id** (`InAppAlertState.hasEventFor(groupKey, eventId, normalizedTitle)`): both fire paths (the v2 push AND `fireGroupCalendarEvent`) skip + record the fingerprint when a card already exists for the event by id OR title, and the enricher ADOPTS a still-thin card by title (`eventRefId==null && title match`, transform sets its `eventRefId`) — so whichever path fires SECOND never spawns a duplicate even when only one of them knew the id. (Tradeoff: two genuinely-distinct undismissed events in the same group sharing an EXACT title collapse to one — rare, and it aligns with the recurring "one card per series" behavior.)
  - **`AlertImageStore` (`com.vrca.vrchat`, files at `filesDir/alert_images/<sha1(url)>.img`)**: dismissal-scoped persistent image cache fixing "thumbnails reload every time you open the menu". One file per unique image URL, downloaded ONCE (`ensureCached`, session cookie attached for vrchat hosts) when the notification fires / info first resolves; UI loads via `AlertImageStore.resolve(ctx, url) ?: url` (disk file first, Coil network fallback). **Lifecycle = references**: a file lives until NOTHING references its URL — alert dismissal (`InAppAlertState.dismiss`/`dismissAll`) and the instance-history 24h prune drop references, then `gc()` (also run at `load()`) deletes orphans. References tracked from: alert events' `imageUrl` (event/announcement banners; invite targets get theirs recorded via `setInviteTargetImage` when the picker first resolves info) + `InstanceHistoryStore.Entry.imageUrl` (recorded via `updateImage` at the same moment). `InstanceTarget.imageUrl` feeds the stored thumb into `InstanceRow` so history/invite rows render instantly while live info is in flight. Deliberately NO size cap (thumbnails are ~50-150 KB; even hundreds of undismissed alerts is single-digit MB).
  - **In-app alerts (grouped)**: Notifications create persistent in-app alert groups displayed as dismissible cards at the top of the VRChat tab. The notification section starts **collapsed** by default and is expandable via a "Notifications (N)" header. Groups are keyed by `userId + notificationType` (e.g., `bio_usr_xxx`, `name_usr_xxx`) or by notification category (e.g., `announcement_$groupId`, `event_$groupId`). When the same event type fires multiple times for the same user/group, events are appended to the existing group — never overwritten. `InAppAlertState` singleton manages `MutableStateFlow<List<InAppAlertGroup>>`. Each `InAppAlertGroup` contains a list of `InAppAlertEvent` objects (max 50 per group), with `firstSeenMs`, `lastUpdatedMs` timestamps, and optional `eventTitle` per event. The UI shows "Title (N)" when a group has multiple events. Expanding a group shows all events in chronological order, each with its own before/after diff (`wordDiff()` in `VrchatPage.kt`) and optional per-event title and relative timestamp. **The diff is LINE-level, not word-level.** Bios are usually multi-line lists; a word-level LCS matched common words ("the", "my", "get") across DIFFERENT lines and painted an inconsistent red/green/purple mess (the user reported this). Whole lines are far more unique, so the algorithm runs LCS over lines: each line is unchanged (neutral grey), removed (red), added (green), or **moved (purple `0xFFB388FF`)**. **Line move detection** matches a removed line's exact trimmed content against added lines — whole-line matching is unambiguous, so no blocklist is needed (the prior word-level move detection needed a blocklist to stop short words like "my"/"I" turning purple everywhere). **Single-line refinement**: each remaining real-removed line is paired with the most similar real-added line (token Jaccard > 0.3); paired lines get a word-level red/green diff (`lineWordDiff()`) so editing one word in a line highlights just that word instead of reddening the whole line. Single-line bios fall through this pairing and show word-level diffs too. Newlines are preserved so multi-line layout stays intact. Each rendered card is wrapped in `key(group.groupId)` so Compose tracks its expanded/collapsed state by group identity, not list position — without this, a new alert prepended to the list shifted every card's remembered state up one slot, so the card the user was reading collapsed and the new top card appeared expanded. Bio diff cards show bold **BEFORE** (red) and **AFTER** (green) headers above each half so users aren't confused about which side is the old/new bio. The collapse/expand chevron and the dismiss (X) button each sit in a tinted circular `Surface` (primary-tint for the chevron, error-tint for dismiss) so they stand out instead of blending into the card. Groups are capped at 20 (FIFO). Only 3 groups visible at once; "+N more" button reveals the rest. Dismissing removes the entire group, all its events, AND cancels the linked Android notification (using `groupId.hashCode()` as the stable notification ID). Backward-compatible: `addAlert()` wraps single events into single-event groups. Old flat alerts are auto-migrated to the group format on first load. `fireEventNotification` accepts `alertGroupKey` and `alertEventTitle` parameters — when provided, the event is added to the named group with its own title instead of creating a standalone alert. For grouped Android notifications, uses stable notification ID per `alertGroupKey.hashCode()` with `setOnlyAlertOnce(true)` so subsequent events update the notification silently (no re-alert). Bio change notifications no longer have a per-friend cooldown — the grouping model replaces cooldown-based spam prevention.
  - **Avatar change suppression**: Avatar change notifications are suppressed when the friend's location changed in the same `friend-update` payload OR when a `friend-location` event fired for the same friend within the last 15 seconds (`recentlyRelocated` map). This two-layer guard handles both same-payload location changes and the common race condition where `friend-location` updates the cache before `friend-update` arrives.
  - **Status change suppression during world hop**: `maybeFireStatusChange` is only called in `handleFriendUpdate` when the location did NOT change. VRChat sends status data redundantly with location changes, so firing both a "Friend moved" and "Friend changed presence" notification for the same event is noise.
  - **Warmup suppression**: For 30 seconds after pipeline connect (`WARMUP_MS`), all friend activity notifications (online, active, location, and handleFriendUpdate changes) are suppressed. This prevents the initial burst of WebSocket events from firing stale notifications for friends' existing state. Cache updates still happen during warmup so subsequent real changes are correctly diffed. Does NOT suppress friend-add, friend-delete, notification/notification-v2, or show-alert events.
  - **Gift notifications**: `gift`, `giftCredits`, and `giftBundle` notification types are handled in `handleNotificationEvent` and fire via `KEY_NOTIF_GIFT_RECEIVED`.
  - **Invite response notifications**: `requestInviteResponse` (someone accepted your invite request) and `inviteResponse` (someone accepted your invite) are handled and fire through the invite channel.
  - **Unknown event logging**: Unhandled pipeline event types are logged at DEBUG level for discovery. Known non-notification events (`user-location`, `see-notification`, `hide-notification`, `clear-notification`, `response-notification`, `content-refresh`) have explicit no-op branches.
  - **Reconnection notification fix**: `scheduleReconnect()` now calls `startPipeline()` (which handles session recovery and proper notification updates) when the user isn't logged in, instead of silently falling through with stale "Reconnecting in Xs..." text. Also updates to "Connecting..." before calling `connectWebSocket()`. If `connectWebSocket()` finds no cookie/auth token, it falls back to `startPipeline()` instead of silently returning.
  - **Reconnect dedup (`scheduleReconnect` `if (wsJob?.isActive == true) return`)**: a single socket failure fires BOTH `onFailure` AND `onClosed`, and the ~50s health check can also call `scheduleReconnect()` — each previously launched its OWN backoff coroutine, each opening a NEW WebSocket, so several overlapping pipeline sockets raced (every one of which can independently fail/close and spawn yet more), which itself reads as "the pipeline keeps disconnecting." The guard early-returns when a reconnect is already pending (mid-backoff) or in progress. `wsJob` completes quickly once `connectWebSocket()` hands the socket off (the socket lives independently), so a genuine later failure still schedules a fresh retry; the guard only suppresses the duplicate-trigger stack.
  - **Cookie write atomicity (`captureRolledCookies` `synchronized(cookieWriteLock)` + `commit()`)**: every authenticated REST response routes through `captureRolledCookies`, and the heavy `fetchPresence` chain + the friends sweep fire several CONCURRENTLY on `Dispatchers.IO`. Without a lock, two responses doing `edit()`/`apply()` at once race on the shared `auth`/`twoFactorAuth` store the pipeline WebSocket also reads as its connect token — a stale-IP cookie could win nondeterministically. The lock makes each read-decide-write atomic and ordered. Timestamp/roll-forward semantics are unchanged (every present cookie still refreshes its stored-at clock, which `shouldRefreshCookies` and the trusted-device window depend on) — only the concurrency was hardened.
  - **Persistent notification resilience**: A 10s loop re-posts the foreground notification if it gets swiped away. The notification channel uses `setSound(null, null)` and the builder uses `setSound(null)` + `setVibrate(null)` to ensure silence. The notification also refreshes automatically when Discord RPC status changes.
  - `friendsCache` is now `Map<String, FriendCacheEntry>` (was `Map<String, String>`) carrying display name, status, location, world name, avatar thumb, bio, trustRank — used to detect change events from `friend-update` and fire targeted notifications (renamed, changed status, changed avatar, updated bio, trust rank changed).
  - **App update notifications**: `startAppUpdateCheckLoop()` runs on service creation (public build only), checks `checkFirestoreRelease()` immediately and every 6 hours. Fires via `fireAppUpdateNotification()` with `KEY_NOTIF_APP_UPDATE` (defaults to enabled). Uses stable notification ID `NOTIF_ID_APP_UPDATE` so repeated checks update in place. Shows version name and release notes in expandable notification.
  - **Admin announcement notifications**: `attachAnnouncementsListener()` sets up a Firestore snapshot listener on `announcements` collection (active=true). First snapshot seeds `seenAnnouncementIds` without firing. Subsequent snapshots fire via `fireEventNotification` with `KEY_NOTIF_ANNOUNCEMENTS` for new doc IDs. Seen IDs persisted to SharedPreferences (`vrca_seen_announcements/ids`). **This service listener is the background-surviving one** (fires the NOTIFICATION when the app is off-screen / the user is on another tab). The public UI (`VrcaScreen.kt`) attaches a **FOREGROUND-scoped** `where active==true limit 60` snapshot listener (a `DisposableEffect` — attaches while the screen is composed, detaches `onDispose`) so the in-app announcements list updates LIVE while open and costs nothing while backgrounded (see "Live-while-open reads"). (History: this was briefly a one-shot `get()` on screen entry to cut a duplicate persistent listener, but the user wanted the in-app list to update live while open; the foreground listener's first callback is the same single read as that `get()`, then bills only when a push actually lands while you're looking — so it's the same baseline cost plus live-while-open, and still works when the user isn't logged into VRChat.)
  - **VRChat status page integration**: `startStatusPagePolling()` polls `https://status.vrchat.com/api/v2/summary.json` every 2 minutes. Parses Atlassian Statuspage format (indicator: none/minor/major/critical, components, incidents). Updates `VrchatPipelineState.statusPageState` for UI consumption. Fires ongoing notification on incidents via `KEY_NOTIF_VRCHAT_ALERT`, auto-dismisses after 20 minutes of all-clear. Tapping opens status.vrchat.com. `VrchatStatusBanner` composable shows on Home and VRChat tabs with color-coded warnings. Uses `ElevatedCard` with elevated shadow for the outer container. The banner is collapsible (header row with status dot, title, subtitle, and chevron toggle); its expand/collapse state lives in a shared observable `StatusBannerState.expanded` (`mutableStateOf`, process-scoped singleton) so collapsing it on one tab (e.g. Home) instantly collapses it on every other tab (e.g. VRChat) — previously each tab held its own local `remember` state and counted as a separate card. It defaults to expanded and resets to expanded on a cold process restart so a new outage re-surfaces. When collapsed, shows an "N affected" chip. Affected components are grouped under an "Affected systems" sub-heading inside a nested `Surface` card — each component row has a small status dot, the system name, and a colored status chip; rows are separated by thin dividers. Incidents are grouped under "Latest updates", each in its own nested `Surface` card with incident name and latest update text. A "View VRChat Status Page" button-style Surface link sits at the bottom.
- **VrchatPipelineState**: Shared in-memory singleton using `MutableStateFlow` for reactive cross-component state (connection status, presence data, VRChat status page). Compose UI observes via `collectAsState()`. `statusPageFlow` exposes `VrchatStatusPageData?` for the status banner.
- **DiscordRpcService**: Foreground service running a hidden WebView that loads `discord.com`. Discord's own JavaScript handles the gateway connection (intents, capabilities, X-Super-Properties, TLS, /science telemetry) — all traffic looks like a real Discord web session, making detection effectively impossible. After page load, a JS shim hooks `WebSocket.prototype.send` to capture the gateway WebSocket reference, then exposes `window.VRCA_setActivity(json)` / `window.VRCA_clearActivity()` which send OP 3 (Presence Update) directly on the captured gateway connection. The Flux dispatcher approach was abandoned after Discord fully obfuscated all internal module names. The native side pushes activity updates via `evaluateJavascript` whenever `VrchatPipelineState.presenceFlow` emits (1.5s debounce) or every 10s as keep-alive. World thumbnails are squash-proxied via `images.weserv.nl` (`fit=fill`); the default VRChat image (`DEFAULT_VRCHAT_IMAGE_URL`) skips squashing. Image URLs are resolved to `mp:external/...` format via Discord's `POST /api/v9/applications/{app_id}/external-assets` endpoint (called from within the WebView JS using the session token read from `localStorage.token` or captured from Discord's own fetch calls — the token never leaves the JS context and is never sent to native code). Resolved URLs are cached in `window._vrca_asset_cache`. The user token is never extracted or stored natively — `DiscordLoginWebView` just seeds the `CookieManager` session. Auto-starts when VRChat pipeline connects if `discord_rpc_enabled` and `discord_session_seeded` are both true.
  - **OP 3 interception**: The `WebSocket.prototype.send` hook intercepts all outgoing OP 3 messages from Discord's own code. When our activity is set (`window._vrca_activity`), it's injected into the `activities[]` array of every Discord-originated OP 3. This ensures our RPC persists through Discord's own status changes, idle detection, and gateway reconnects — Discord can't overwrite our activity because it's included in every outgoing presence update. The hook also captures the user's current status (`online`/`dnd`/`invisible`) from each OP 3 so our direct sends use the correct status rather than hardcoding `'online'`.
  - **Anti-idle**: On mobile, Discord auto-sends OP 3 with `status: 'idle'` when the user backgrounds the app. The hook tracks the user's "intended" status (last non-idle status they set). When Discord tries to send `status: 'idle'` but the intended status is `'online'`, the hook overrides it back to `'online'` with `since: 0`. This keeps the user's Discord status online while VRC-A's foreground service is running. DND and invisible are respected (never overridden).
  - **Safety system (7 features):**
    1. **Risk gating UI**: First-time enable shows a consent dialog with checkbox acknowledgment + 4-second delay before the Continue button activates. Consent persisted in `discord_risk_accepted` DataStore key — only shown once. Bullet list explicitly warns that disconnecting may cause Discord to invalidate sessions on other devices, logging the user out everywhere.
    2. **Session state monitoring + gateway-health recovery**: A 30s background loop (`startSessionMonitor`) reads `window.VRCA_gatewayHealth()` via `evaluateJavascript` instead of just `readyState === 1`. The old raw-readyState check showed a **false "connected"** when the gateway socket stayed OPEN but the session was dead/zombied (e.g. a Discord outage black-holing presence) — the user saw "Discord presence connected" while their RPC was missing from their profile, and had to manually sign out/in. `VRCA_gatewayHealth()` classifies: `no_gateway` (shim lost the ref), `connecting` (socket opening → wait), `dead` (readyState closing/closed), `bad` (Discord sent OP 7 Reconnect / OP 9 Invalid-Session, or the socket fired `close`), `zombie` (open but no heartbeat ACK for >2.5× the `heartbeat_interval`), `alive`. **Reload thresholds are now per-class (NOT a flat "reload after 2 checks for everything", which turned transient blips into frequent visible RPC drops)** — a reload (`onSessionExpired()`) drops the RPC for its reload+re-inject window, so it only fires when the signal is real: `dead` (readyState CLOSING/CLOSED — compression-independent, reliable) reloads after `DEAD_RELOAD_THRESHOLD = 2` (~60s, one RESUME-grace cycle); `zombie`/`bad` are derived from heartbeat-ACK / OP7-9 tracking that needs READABLE inbound frames, which Discord's `zlib-stream` compression makes unparseable → they can FALSE-positive, so they require `DEGRADED_RELOAD_THRESHOLD = 4` (~2 min) before reloading — and a socket that's genuinely closing reports `dead` and takes the faster path anyway. Each non-matching state resets the others' counters (`deadHealthChecks`/`degradedHealthChecks`/`noGatewayChecks`). `onSessionExpired()` **reloads the WebView**, which re-establishes a fresh gateway and **auto-re-authenticates from the persistent `localStorage` token + `CookieManager` cookie (no password)** — doing automatically what the manual sign-out/in did. **`no_gateway` also escalates to a reload**: the first misses re-inject the shim (cheap — recaptures the gateway if Discord's JS still has a live one); after `NO_GATEWAY_RELOAD_THRESHOLD = 3` consecutive still-`no_gateway` checks, or if shim injection EXHAUSTS its retries still reporting `no_gateway` (the `injectShim` give-up branch — `lastShimResult.contains("no_gateway")`), it escalates to `onSessionExpired()` instead of the terminal **FAILED** state. This fixes the user-reported dead end where the RPC silently dropped, the app showed "Discord setup failed: no_gateway(hook=true)", and re-injecting/restarting couldn't recover it — only a manual Discord sign-out/in (which reloads the page → Discord's JS opens a fresh gateway → the hook recaptures it via `onPageFinished`) brought it back. Re-running the module finder on the SAME page can never recapture a gateway Discord's own JS hasn't reopened, so a page reload is the only real fix; `onSessionExpired()`'s `MAX_SESSION_RECOVERIES` cap still bounds it (after the cap → "sign in again"). The shim (`WS_HOOK_JS`) tracks health via `_vrca_attachGatewayListeners(ws)` — a passive `addEventListener('message'/'open'/'close')` on each captured gateway socket (it only OBSERVES + re-pushes OUR activity; it never alters Discord's own frames, so the "real web session" anti-detection property holds). Heartbeat-ACK/OP-7/9/READY tracking needs **readable (uncompressed JSON) inbound** — when Discord uses `zlib-stream` compression those frames are binary and skipped, so `zombie`/`bad` degrade gracefully to `alive`; the **compression-independent** recoveries still work: a new gateway socket's `open` event re-pushes our activity at +3s/+8s (so a Discord-initiated reconnect re-lands the RPC even when frames can't be parsed), and `dead` (readyState) still forces a reload. **Known limit**: if the socket stays fully healthy (heartbeats ACKing) but Discord's presence BACKEND is down, there is no client-side signal — undetectable, resolves when Discord recovers. WebViewClient still detects login-page redirects (both `onPageFinished` and `shouldOverrideUrlLoading`) for true session expiry.
    2b. **Offline parking — no false "connected" while there's no network (`parkOffline` + validated-network callback)**: with **genuinely no network** (e.g. Wi-Fi off and no mobile data), the WebView still serves `discord.com` from its **HTTP cache**, so a page-load + shim-inject `MODULE_FINDER` returns `"ok"` and the old code declared the RPC **CONNECTED** even though the gateway WebSocket can never open — then a 10s presence push / 30s health check found the dead gateway, reloaded, the cached page loaded again, and the status **flickered CONNECTED → RECONNECTING every ~10s** (the user-reported "it shows reconnected to the RPC even though internet is off, then back to reconnecting, repeat"). **Fix: never declare CONNECTED or churn reloads while offline — sit parked in "Waiting for network" until validated connectivity returns.** `parkOffline()` (called from the session-monitor top-of-loop offline check, `loadWebView`'s offline guard, the `injectShim` success path's `!hasNetwork()` guard, `onSessionExpired`'s offline branch, and `onReceivedError`) sets `awaitingNetwork=true` + `shimReady=false`, stops presence pushes, resets all health/freeze counters, and shows "Waiting for network" — `shimReady=false` makes every health probe / `pushActivity` a no-op so nothing can re-declare CONNECTED. **`hasNetwork()` now keys on `NET_CAPABILITY_VALIDATED`** (Android actually probed real internet reachability), NOT `NET_CAPABILITY_INTERNET` (which only means the interface *claims* internet — true for a connecting/captive network). **The network callback uses `onCapabilitiesChanged` gated on `NET_CAPABILITY_VALIDATED`** (recover only once internet is confirmed) instead of `onAvailable` (fires the instant any interface registers, before validation → would reload into the cached page and false-connect), plus `onLost` to park immediately. So a real reconnect (validated) drives one clean `loadWebView()` recovery; a flapping/connecting network never triggers the doomed cached-page reload.
    3. **Failure detection + response**: Non-silent — failures update `DiscordRpcState.status` (shared `MutableStateFlow`) and the notification. Session expiry shows a red "Sign in again" button in the UI. Module finder failure shows "needs update" status. UI status messages are user-friendly ("Setting up Discord connection...", "Reconnecting to Discord...", "Discord connection setup failed") rather than technical injection terminology.
    4. **Auto recovery**: On session expiry, up to 3 auto-recovery attempts with linear backoff (2s, 4s, 6s) reload the WebView page. On dispatcher loss, shim re-injection with exponential backoff (3s, 6s, 9s, 12s, 15s). On consecutive push failures (5), shim re-injection. All counters reset on successful reconnection.
    5. **Per-user failure isolation**: All state is service-scoped — failures only pause the Discord RPC service, never affect VRChat pipeline, Firestore sync, or any other system.
    6. **User feedback/transparency**: `DiscordRpcState` singleton exposes `statusFlow` (enum: IDLE/CONNECTING/CONNECTED/RECONNECTING/SESSION_EXPIRED/FAILED) and `failureMessageFlow`. UI shows a colored status dot + label (green=Connected, yellow=Connecting/Reconnecting, red=Expired/Failed) with detail message.
    7. **Safety guards**: Max 5 shim retries with backoff, max 3 session recovery attempts, max 5 consecutive push failures before re-inject, 1.5s minimum interval between JS evaluations (double-guarded: both delay and timestamp check), all counters capped.
  - **RPC counter persistence**: `onlineStartEpochMs` (the Discord activity `timestamps.start`, always milliseconds) is persisted to SharedPreferences (`rpcPrefs / online_start_epoch`). **The grace-window restore is keyed on a `last_online_seen` heartbeat** (refreshed in `buildActivityJson()` on every confirmed-online tick), NOT on `offline_at`: when the user returns online and `onlineStartEpochMs` is 0, the saved start epoch is restored only if `now - last_online_seen < ONLINE_GRACE_MS`. **`ONLINE_GRACE_MS` is 20 minutes** (applies to BOTH the in-VRChat and not-in-VRChat counters via `resolveElapsedStart`). It is deliberately >15 min so it **spans the `PipelineWatchdogWorker`'s ~15-min recovery gap**: after an OEM process kill the watchdog re-arms the services on its next ~15-min cycle, and the counter must still be inside the grace window at that moment for the timer to resume instead of resetting. Because `last_online_seen` freezes the moment the app stops tracking (process death, swipe), a longer untracked gap correctly resets the counter to a fresh start instead of bridging into a bogus multi-hour timer. **Critically, `onStartCommand` sets `onlineStartEpochMs = 0L` (it does NOT preload the persisted epoch)** so `buildActivityJson()`'s grace check always runs — preloading the stale epoch directly was the bug that shipped a day-old start to Discord as a "25 hours online" timer after a normal cold start (the grace block only runs when the in-memory value is 0). After 20 minutes untracked, a fresh counter starts. **Not-in-VRChat counter flicker guard (`OFFLINE_CLEAR_CONFIRM_MS` = 3 min)**: the two counters were asymmetric — the in-VRChat counter has grace carry AND its prefs are never cleared (so it's stable during active play), but the not-in-VRChat counter's persisted start (`offline_start_epoch`/`offline_last_seen`) was WIPED on the FIRST tick where `isOnline` read true, so a single spurious `isOnline=true` (a stale REST presence read / racy WS `user-update`/`user-location` event on a flaky connection) permanently reset it and the next offline tick restarted from 0 — the "not-in-VRChat RPC timer randomly restarts on connection blips" bug. Fix: the offline prefs are cleared only once the online session is CONFIRMED sustained (`nowMs - onlineStartEpochMs >= OFFLINE_CLEAR_CONFIRM_MS`). A transient flicker resolves a FRESH `online_start_epoch ≈ now` (the prior session went stale > grace ago), so the clear is skipped and the offline counter carries on across the blip within its own grace window; a genuine sustained visit still clears it so leaving VRChat restarts the offline clock as intended.
- **DiscordRpcState**: Shared in-memory singleton using `MutableStateFlow` for Discord RPC status reporting. Compose UI in the settings page observes `statusFlow` and `failureMessageFlow` via `collectAsState()` to show real-time connection status.
- **DiscordLoginWebView**: WebView-based Discord login flow. User logs into discord.com; login completion uses a 3-layer verification: (1) URL must contain `discord.com/channels` OR `discord.com/app` (Discord may redirect to either after login), (2) 2-second dwell timer ensures the URL is stable (cancelled if page navigates away), (3) JS authentication probe checks `#app-mount` has rendered children AND no login form (`input[type="password"]`, `input[type="email"]`) is visible — uses stable DOM structure rather than webpack chunk variable names which Discord may rename. The probe retries up to 4 times at 1.5s intervals when it returns false, since Discord's SPA can take a few seconds to hydrate after the auto-redirect from `/login` for users with valid cached cookies. All retry runnables are tracked and cancelled in `onPageStarted` so a navigation away cannot trigger a stale `onLoginComplete`. Only after all 3 layers pass does `onLoginComplete` fire and `discord_session_seeded` is set.
- **IpField**: Multi-slot IP field component with 3 named slots (Home/Hotspot/Other). Uses local state tracking (not async DataStore) for immediate slot switching without cross-contamination. Supports per-slot editing and auto-migration from legacy single IP key. The Apply button and the slot-1 migration LaunchedEffect call `repo.saveIpSlotAddress(slot, address)` rather than `saveIpSlot(slot, name, address)` — only the auto-save debounce in the expanded slot editor is allowed to write the slot name. This prevents the placeholder defaults from `collectAsState(initial = "Home"|"Hotspot"|"Other")` from overwriting the user's saved name when DataStore hasn't yet emitted its real value. **The quick-edit `editBuffer` reflects ONLY the selected slot's saved address — it must NOT fall back to `uiState.ipAddress`** (the last-applied RUNTIME ip, which belongs to whatever slot was last equipped). On a tab re-entry `currentSlot` briefly defaults to 1 while the slot's DataStore value is still the placeholder `""`; the old fallback would then fill the field with a *different* slot's ip, and an Apply wrote that into the wrong slot (the "slot 2 overrides slot 1" bug). Worst case now the field is briefly blank (Apply is blocked on blank) and self-corrects once DataStore emits. **The "slot contents clone to slot 1 on tab re-entry" bug** (slot 2's IP overwriting a *populated* slot 1 after editing slot 1 → switching to slot 2 → leaving the tab → returning → switching back to slot 1) was caused by the **expanded-editor auto-save debounce** (`LaunchedEffect(n1,a1)` etc.). That debounce runs unconditionally on every fresh composition (tabs render via `when(p)` in `VrcaScreen.kt`, so leaving/returning to Home DISPOSES and re-composes `IpField` — all `remember` resets). The per-slot buffers (`a1/a2/a3`) seed from `collectAsState`'s initial value then "follow" DataStore via the `LaunchedEffect(ipNAddress)` effects; a follow-vs-debounce race could persist a buffer that merely SYNCED from DataStore rather than one the user typed, overwriting a populated slot. **Fix: per-slot `dirty` flags** (`dirty1/dirty2/dirty3`) set true ONLY in the editor `TextField` `onValueChange` (real user typing); the follow effects deliberately never set them, and each debounce `return@LaunchedEffect`s unless its slot is dirty. So a buffer that only mirrored DataStore is never written back — cloning is structurally impossible regardless of composition/lifecycle races. (This is build-variant-independent: `IpField` is identical across public/admin and no Firestore sync path reads/writes the slot addresses; it presented as "public-only" purely from differing per-install slot state and from the public install running an older released APK that predated the fixes.) **Secondary hardening — the slot-1 legacy migration is a race-free ONE-SHOT** (`LaunchedEffect(Unit)` reading PERSISTED values via `.first()`, gated by the `ip_slot1_migrated` DataStore flag so it runs at most once and never re-fires on recomposition/tab re-entry). It only copies the legacy `IP` key into slot 1 when `activeIpSlot == 1 && ip1Address.isBlank()`; the old reactive `LaunchedEffect(ip1Address, uiState.ipAddress)` re-fired on every tab re-entry and, because `repo.ipAddress`/the legacy `IP` key tracks the ACTIVE slot, copied the equipped slot's IP into an EMPTY slot 1. The `slot == 1` guard guarantees a non-slot-1 address can never be written into slot 1.

### Firestore Schema (users/{deviceHash})
Key fields written by the app:
- `isOnlineInApp` (bool): Set to `true` by the app-open write, `false` by the swipe-away shutdown and `VrcaViewModel.onCleared` (GlobalScope fallback). **Swipe-away shutdown is centralised in `AppShutdown.onTaskSwiped(context)`** (`com.vrca.app.AppShutdown`), called from the `onTaskRemoved` of EVERY foreground service. This was a real bug: the kill used to live only in `VrchatPipelineService.onTaskRemoved`, but the pipeline isn't always running, and **`KeepAliveService` — which IS always running (started from `MainActivity.onCreate`) and holds a `PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK` — had no `onTaskRemoved` at all**, so after a swipe the wakelock-holding service kept the whole process alive and nothing ever killed it. Now an `AtomicBoolean` in `AppShutdown` makes the heavy work run exactly once no matter which service fires first: it stamps the `manual_kill_at` flag, stops the other services (`VrchatPipelineService`/Discord/KeepAlive/Overlay, releasing wakelocks AND removing the "Connected as X" persistent notification promptly — without stopping the pipeline service here its notification lingered for up to 5s, the offline-write await, before `killProcess`, which looked like the swiped app was still "running / connected as username"), then on a background thread **awaits the offline Firestore write (5s timeout) and hard-kills the process** (`Process.killProcess` + `exitProcess`). `KeepAliveService.onTaskRemoved` is the reliable entry point since it's always running. The await ensures the going-offline write has a chance to reach Firestore before the kill; if it times out, the admin-side staleness sweep still flips the user offline once `lastActiveAt` ages past the ~65-min window. (Previously it waited a fixed 10s before killing, leaving the app running in the background that whole time.) **Sticky-restart guard**: `VrchatPipelineService`, `DiscordRpcService`, and `KeepAliveService` all return `START_STICKY` (so a memory-pressure kill is recovered), which means Android would normally relaunch them with a null intent right after the swipe-kill — making the app look like it was never killed. To prevent this, `AppShutdown` stamps `manual_kill_at = now` (SharedPreferences `vrca_remote`, committed synchronously) before killing; each service's `onStartCommand` treats a **null intent** as a sticky restart and, if `AppShutdown.isManualKillFresh()` (within `MANUAL_KILL_WINDOW_MS` = 15s), calls `stopForeground` + `stopSelf`, returns `START_NOT_STICKY`, AND kills its own (freshly-resurrected) process after a 300ms delay — because `stopSelf` alone leaves the recreated process sitting idle as a cached background process (Samsung's "running apps" list still showed it). The delay lets `START_NOT_STICKY` register before the kill so Android does NOT recreate the service yet again. A legitimate reopen always passes a non-null intent (with `EXTRA_DEVICE_HASH`) so it bypasses the guard, and the 15s window expires harmlessly. (Note: `NowPlayingListenerService` is a `NotificationListenerService` the system binds independently; it can be rebound by the OS later when notification activity occurs, but that no longer keeps the process perpetually alive after swipe.) **Persistent swipe flag (`swiped_away`)**: the 15s `manual_kill_at` window is NOT enough on its own — the `PipelineWatchdogWorker` (WorkManager, ~15 min) survives the process kill and fires in a fresh process LONG after the window expired, sees the user still logged in, and resurrects the pipeline + keep-alive. That was the "I swiped it away but it starts running again after a while" bug. So `onTaskSwiped` ALSO sets a persistent `AppShutdown.KEY_SWIPED_AWAY=true` boolean (committed synchronously) and best-effort cancels the watchdog (`PipelineWatchdogWorker.cancel`). The watchdog `doWork()` and every service's null-intent sticky-restart guard now back off when `isManualKillFresh() || isSwipedAway()`. The flag is cleared ONLY by a real user reopen (`MainActivity.onCreate` → `AppShutdown.clearSwipedAway()`) or reboot (`BootReceiver` clears it then restarts services). An OS/OEM kill never runs `onTaskSwiped`, so the flag stays false there and the watchdog still recovers the process — **a deliberate swipe stays dead, an OEM kill comes back.** **Stale-notification hardening (the "Connected as X doesn't consistently disappear on swipe" fix)**: (1) the kill thread enforces a **minimum 1.2s shutdown floor** (`MIN_SHUTDOWN_DELAY_MS`) before `killProcess` — `stopOtherServices()` only QUEUES the stop requests from `onTaskRemoved` (main thread); the actual `onDestroy`/`ACTION_STOP` handling runs on the main looper after it returns, so a fast (or null-task) offline write let `killProcess` race the stops and all four still-foreground services died abruptly — exactly where OEMs (Samsung) leave the shared notification stale in the shade; (2) right before `killProcess` the thread calls **`AppShutdown.cancelPersistentNotification()`** (`nm.cancel(SHARED_NOTIF_ID = 1001)`, processed in system_server so it survives our death — ignored while a live FGS still owns the id, a cleanup sweep once they've stopped); (3) each sticky-restart guard's delayed kill thread (KeepAlive/Pipeline/Discord) also calls it, because `KeepAliveService.onCreate` re-posts the notification BEFORE the `onStartCommand` guard runs and the `stopForeground(STOP_FOREGROUND_REMOVE)` removal can race the 300ms-delayed kill.
- `lastActiveAt` (timestamp): **Canonical liveness field (hourly model).** Written on cold-open and then once per hour by `VrcaViewModel.startHourlyHeartbeat()` (in-process coroutine — a swiped/OS-killed app stops firing it, so it goes stale and is correctly counted offline; an AlarmManager wakeup is deliberately NOT used because it would resurrect a dead app and make it report online forever). Also refreshed every 10s while an admin is watching this user (real-time ~25s liveness for the watched user). Admin online = `lastActiveAt` within ~65 min (see "Admin online detection" — the admin ignores the `isOnlineInApp` flag entirely and keys on this timestamp's freshness, minus a newer `offlineAt` marker).
- `offlineAt` (timestamp): **Clean-shutdown marker.** Written (alongside `isOnlineInApp=false`) ONLY on a deliberate swipe-away by `AppShutdown.buildOfflineWriteTask` and `VrcaViewModel.onCleared`. The admin's `isUserOnline` forces a row offline the instant `offlineAt` is newer than `(lastActiveAt ?? lastSeenAt)`, giving instant swipe→offline; a live heartbeat advances `lastActiveAt` past it so it can never false-flag a running app. If the swipe write never lands, the ~65-min staleness window still flips the user offline. **Both offline writes deliberately do NOT bump `lastSeenAt`** — refreshing the liveness mirror on shutdown would let `offlineAt > (lastActiveAt ?? lastSeenAt)` TIE (both the same serverTimestamp) for any user whose `lastActiveAt` is somehow absent, keeping a swiped app falsely "online" until the 65-min decay; `offlineAt` is the sole shutdown marker. In `selfMutableKeys()`.
- `lastSeenAt` (timestamp): **Legacy mirror of `lastActiveAt`** — written with the same server timestamp on every liveness write so older admin builds that read `lastSeenAt` keep working (additive migration; nothing removed). New admin code should read `lastActiveAt`.
- `afkEnabled`, `cycleEnabled`, `spotifyEnabled`, `timeEnabled`: Feature toggles (admin-editable)
- `warned`, `banned`, `warnReason`, `banReason`: Moderation flags (read by public app via snapshot listener)
- `targetedUpdateUrl` / `targetedUpdateNotes` (strings): **Legacy** — kept for backwards compat with old app versions. New directed releases use `releases/{deviceHash}` instead (see below).
- **Directed releases via `releases/{deviceHash}`**: Admin writes a per-device release doc to `releases/{deviceHash}` using the same schema as the global `releases/latest` doc (versionCode, versionName, downloadUrl, requiredMinCode, notes, publishedAt, publishedByDevice). The client's `checkFirestoreRelease()` checks `releases/{deviceHash}` first, then falls back to `releases/latest`. **Real-time detection**: `VrcaApp` attaches a Firestore snapshot listener on `releases/{deviceHash}` that immediately shows the update dialog when the admin pushes a targeted release — no polling delay. **The whole update-check block (VM creation + release check + listeners + `UpdateDialog`) runs BEFORE the onboarding gate in `VrcaApp`**, so a forced update overlays the first-run tutorial too — a brand-new user installs the latest version before setting anything up (the app-scoped VM is therefore created during onboarding; its init is login-independent and this is safe). The download uses Android's `DownloadManager` for in-app APK download, then triggers the system package installer. **Browser fallback (`VrcaApp` `onDownload`)**: `DownloadManager` is disabled/unavailable on some OEM builds (reported on Samsung Galaxy — `getSystemService` returns null or `enqueue()` throws), which made the update popup silently do nothing. The `onDownload` handler now null-checks the service and wraps `enqueue()` in try/catch; on ANY failure it falls back to `startActivity(ACTION_VIEW, downloadUrl)` to open the APK link in the browser (surfacing "opened the update in your browser instead" via `downloadError`), and if even that fails it shows the raw URL for manual download. Old app versions that only know about `releases/latest` can still be reached via the global "Release to All" flow; once they update to a version with per-device support, directed releases work forever. **ALL releases are FORCED automatically (both global and directed)** — `requiredMinCode` is no longer consulted; any newer `versionCode` makes the client treat the update as required (`InAppUpdate.parseReleaseSnap` + the `VrcaApp` per-device listener + the legacy `targetedUpdateUrl` path all hardcode `forced = true`). The `UpdateDialog` is then a non-dismissable wall (`onDismissRequest` no-ops, no "Later" button). **Forced updates also hard-block OSC output**: `VrcaApp` calls `VrcaViewModel.applyForceUpdateGate(true)` when a forced update is pending, which sets `VrcaOsc.blocked = true` on both the remote and local OSC senders. `VrcaOsc.sendOscMessage` early-returns when `blocked`, so EVERY chatbox transmission (typing/input/realtime, from any sender loop) is dropped at that single chokepoint — the user can't keep driving the VRChat chatbox by backgrounding the app. The gate lives on the **app-scoped VM**, so it persists after Activity destruction; it's cleared only if the pending update resolves. **Caveat (cannot be backfilled)**: force-update logic must already be present in the installed APK. Versions predating `InAppUpdate.kt`/this gate never read `releases/*` or the forced flag, so no Firestore field can compel them — the only lever they honor is the moderation ban screen (repurposable as an "update required" wall via `banReason`, but that's a manual hack, not wired up). Force-update is forward-only by nature.
- `watcherActiveAt` (timestamp): **Admin-only write.** Refreshed every ~30s while an admin has this user's detail page open. User app reads it from snapshots and feeds into `AdminWatchState.updateFromTimestampMs` — if within 60s, live-mode loops start.
- `killSignal` (timestamp): **Admin-only write.** Written by admin kill switch button. User app reads it from moderation snapshot listener — if fresh (within 60s), kills the app process.
- VRChat presence fields (`vrchatUserId`, `vrchatDisplayName`, `vrchatState`, `vrchatLocation`, etc.): Written every 10s while watched (real-time), AND refreshed by the **hourly delta** (`captureStateForSync` reads `VrchatPipelineState.presence`, null-safe — keys omitted entirely when not logged into VRChat so stale doc values are never clobbered with blanks). So an unwatched user's directory row shows location at hourly freshness. **Instance player count freshness (`refreshInstanceCountBeforeSync`)**: `vrchatInstancePlayerCount`/`vrchatInstanceCapacity` ride the hourly delta but were going STALE for unwatched users (frozen at the last successful heavy `fetchPresence` chain — often `0/N` when the location was patched from a `user-location` WebSocket event, which carries no count, and no later `fetchPresence`/`fetchInstanceCount` succeeded). `startHourlyHeartbeat()` now calls `refreshInstanceCountBeforeSync()` immediately before each `performSelfSync()` — a single lightweight `GET /instances/{loc}` (`VrchatAuthManager.fetchInstanceCount`, the same call the watched fallback + website tab-refresh use) that patches the live count into `VrchatPipelineState.presence` so the hourly write carries an accurate number. **Zero extra Firestore cost**: the hourly write already fires unconditionally (liveness) and already carried these two fields — the count was just stale; this only adds ONE VRChat REST call per hour per online user (not a Firestore read/write).
- NowPlaying / preview fields (`nowPlayingTitle`, `nowPlayingArtist`, `combinedPreviewText`, `activePackage`): **Volatile** — these change constantly during music playback. Real-time (10s) only while watched via the watcher-gated live-sync loop; never written on raw NowPlaying state emissions (that caused a write storm with no admin present). **They also ride the hourly delta + any debounced edit write** — `captureStateForSync()` includes them, so they're swept up whenever a write fires anyway (Firestore bills per doc write, not per field — zero extra writes; the 30s edit debounce is still only SCHEDULED by genuine content edits, never by NowPlaying ticks). History: these fields were in `buildUserSnapshot` (with a comment claiming "included in every self-sync write") but were silently dropped when the delta-write model introduced `captureStateForSync()` without them — `buildUserSnapshot` only fires on first install / uid change, so unwatched users' previews froze at their last watch session ("hourly write doesn't update preview/presence" bug). `VOLATILE_SYNC_KEYS` excludes them from `captureContentForOfflineWrite` (the swipe write deliberately omits volatile preview; the service's offline write already carries final presence).
- `lastReportedTime`, `cycleTrimWarning`, `lastTimeUpdateAt`: Only written while watched (live-mode loop).

**Friends are no longer in Firestore** — `savedFriendIds`/`savedFriendNames` are deleted via `FieldValue.delete()` on offline write. They live in local SharedPreferences only.

**Volatile subdoc `users/{deviceHash}/live/state`** (Phase 2 infrastructure — rules deployed, write migration deferred): Firestore rules allow the subcollection `users/{deviceHash}/live/{doc}`. It was intended to hold high-frequency volatile data (nowPlaying, VRChat presence, preview text) to keep them off the main doc. After the Phase 3 read-model change the admin detail view polls the **main doc** every 10s (no admin-side collection listener at all), so volatile fields stay on the main doc for now — splitting them out would require the admin detail poll to read two docs for no current benefit. The rules remain in place as future-proofing; revisit only if the user's own self-doc listener firing on watched-user volatile writes (echo-suppressed today) becomes a measured cost.

### Ban-evasion capture (tri-directional)
`VrchatBanChecker` (`com.vrca.vrchat`) implements deferred ban-evasion detection across three identifier types. Identity index: `bannedIdentifiers/{identifier}` → `{ banId, type, active }`. Full record: `bannedRecords/{banId}` → `{ deviceHashes[], vrchatIds[], authUids[], displayNames[], evasionAttempts[], ... }`. **Phase 1** (on bootstrap, deviceHash + authUid known): checks `bannedIdentifiers/{deviceHash}` and `/{authUid}`; if active, defers the ban screen and stores `pendingBanId` so the new alt VRChat ID can be captured after login. **Phase 2** (after VRChat login): checks `bannedIdentifiers/{vrchatId}`; if the VRChat ID is banned, the current device/auth are appended (new-device evasion); if Phase 1 flagged the device, the new VRChat ID is captured. **`appendIdentifiersToRecord` writes atomically via a single `WriteBatch`** — the up-to-3 new `bannedIdentifiers` index entries, the `bannedRecords/{banId}` array unions (`deviceHashes`/`authUids`/`vrchatIds`/`displayNames`/`evasionAttempts`), and the `moderationEvents` audit doc all commit all-or-nothing. Before this, they were 5 sequential awaited writes; a mid-way process death left the record inconsistent (an index pointing at a banId whose record never listed it, or a captured identifier with no audit event). The moderationEvent uses an auto-id doc reference added to the batch via `batch.set(collection.document(), ...)`. The whole operation is wrapped in try/catch (logged, non-fatal).

### Public UI kit + Onboarding (UI revamp Phases 1-2, see docs/ui-revamp.md)
- **`PublicUiKit.kt`** (`ui/common/`): shared revamp components — `CompactSectionCard` (collapsible card whose collapsed header carries a live summary; expansion state in `rememberSaveable(title)`, or plain `remember` when `persistExpansion = false` — used by the Media tab cards that must re-open collapsed after tab navigation), `TogglePill` (icon+label+dot pill with long-press jump-to-edit via `detectTapGestures`), `StatusDot`/`KitStatusChip` (KitTone Neutral/Success/Warning/Error mapped to theme + Slime status colors), `LabeledRow`, `TutorialImage` (ON-DEMAND instruction image loaded by 1-based `index` from `TutorialImageStore` — NOT bundled in the APK; shows a spinner while downloading, tap → full-screen pinch-zoom dialog via Coil `rememberAsyncImagePainter`), `TrustNote`, `KitSectionHeader`. Design rule: "collapsed = status, expanded = control"; visual north star is the login screens, NOT the admin panel.
- **`VrcaDialogs.kt`** (`ui/common/`, used by BOTH builds): the shared confirm/picker primitives so every "are you sure?" popup reads identically AND matches the app's Slime card identity. **All dialogs are now card-styled** (`Dialog { ElevatedCard }`), NOT stock M3 `AlertDialog` — the user explicitly rejected the AlertDialog look as not matching the revamp. **`VrcaCardDialog(onDismiss, dismissOnOutsideTap=true, content)`** is the shared shell (near-full-width `ElevatedCard`, `surface` container, `large` rounded corners, 6dp elevation, 20dp side padding) every popup is built on — same visual language as `UpdateDialog`/`InstanceListDialog`. Build NEW dialogs on it, not `AlertDialog`. **`VrcaConfirmDialog(title, body, confirmLabel, onConfirm, onDismiss, destructive=false, confirmEnabled=true, dismissLabel="Cancel")`** — card dialog with a header (a warning glyph in an error-tinted circle when `destructive`), bodyMedium onSurfaceVariant, and a bottom actions row (Cancel `TextButton` + a filled confirm `Button`, error-colored when destructive). EVERY confirm popup routes through it: public Dismiss-all + VRChat sign-out (`VrchatPage.kt`), Settings VRChat/Discord sign-out (`VrcaSettingsPage.kt`), admin **Kill App**, Log out VRChat/Discord (`UsersTab.kt`), Retract release (`ReleasesTab.kt`). The Discord **risk-consent** + **login WebView** dialogs (`VrcaSettingsPage.kt`) and the Automations **preset peek** dialog (`AutomationsPage.kt`) are also rebuilt on `VrcaCardDialog`. **`VrcaDialogCopy`** holds the CANONICAL device-owner sign-out copy. **`VrcaTimeZoneDialog(currentMode, use24h, onSelect, onDismiss)`** — the NEW searchable, DST-correct timezone picker (replaced the old `VrcaSingleSelectDialog` UTC-offset grid): a search field + a "Device timezone" row + a scrollable list of zone rows labeled **"Paris 3:42 PM (UTC+2)"** with the offset computed LIVE (`TimeZones.kt`), so it follows daylight savings. `VrcaSingleSelectDialog` + `SelectPill` remain (SelectPill still drives the Cycle speed chip row).
- **`TimeZones.kt`** (`ui/common/`): timezone helpers shared by the VM + the picker. `resolveTimeZone(mode)` maps the stored `timeMode` string to a `java.time.ZoneId` — backward compatible with the LEGACY `"UTC+N"`/`"UTC-N"`/`"Device"`/`"UTC"` formats AND new IANA ids (e.g. `"Europe/Paris"`). New selections store IANA ids (DST-correct); old `"UTC+5"` values keep working. `timeZoneLabel(id, use24h)` builds the "City h:mm a (UTC±X)" label; `zoneOffsetLabel(zone)` is the live offset; `allTimeZoneIds` (cached, all real IANA zones sorted by live offset) + `commonTimeZoneIds` (curated major cities shown before search). `VrcaViewModel.currentTimeString()` now resolves via `resolveTimeZone(timeMode)`. **Country-name search (all countries)**: `zoneCountryName(id)` maps a zone to its country display name via ICU's region table (`android.icu.util.TimeZone.getRegion(id)` → ISO code → `Locale("", code).displayCountry`, cached) — so searching "japan" finds `Asia/Tokyo`, "france" finds `Europe/Paris`, etc., for EVERY country with no hand-maintained list (minSdk 26, ICU available). `countrySynonyms` adds colloquial aliases ("usa"/"uk"/"holland"/"uae"/…). The picker filter (`VrcaDialogs.kt` `VrcaTimeZoneDialog`) matches city, region, country name, zone id, and offset label.
- **Chatbox sub-lines (multi-row Pinned / Cycle — `app/SubLineCodec.kt`)**: the Pinned message and each Cycle line can hold up to **3 sub-lines**, each rendering as its own VRChat chatbox ROW. A sub-line is `ChatboxSubLine(text, hidden)`; the list order is the row order; a `hidden` sub-line stays in the editor (and syncs) but is NOT sent. **The hide/order/hidden-text state rides the EXISTING synced string fields** (`afkMessage` for Pinned, each `\n`-delimited slide of `cycleLinesText` for Cycle) via `SubLineCodec` — **ZERO new Firestore fields, zero extra reads/writes** — so the admin, decoding the same format, sees + can moderate every row (hidden included). **Encoding**: a lone VISIBLE sub-line encodes as PLAIN text (no markers → byte-for-byte backward compatible with old readers and the single-row case); otherwise `SENTINEL() + sub0 + SEP() + sub1 + …` where a hidden sub-line's text is prefixed `HIDDEN()`. An encoded slide never contains `\n`, so `cycleLinesText`'s slide-level `\n` split / cross-device parsing are unaffected. `sanitize()` strips those control chars (and the reserved ` `) from user input. **`renderVisible(stored)`** (visible, non-blank rows joined by real `\n`) is the ONLY thing sent to VRChat — hidden rows cost nothing against the 144 budget; `visibleRowCount()` feeds the 9-line budget (`limitWithPriority` now counts ROWS, `text.count('\n')+1`, not blocks). **Admin readable round-trip** (`toAdminText`/`fromAdminText`, cycle variants `toAdminCycleText`/`fromAdminCycleText`): the admin text boxes show rows joined by `" ⏎ "` with hidden rows tagged `"⊘"` and re-encode on save, so the raw control-char form never appears in admin UI. **Phase 1 shipped = Pinned**: `VrcaViewModel.pinnedSubLines()`/`setPinnedSubLineText`/`setPinnedSubLineHidden`/`addPinnedSubLine`/`removePinnedSubLine`/`movePinnedSubLine` all re-encode into `afkMessage` via `updateAfkText` (so presets ride along for free — `afkPreset1-3` store the encoded value; sync writes the RAW `afkPresetTexts[i]`, NOT `getAfkPresetPreview` which returns a rendered preview). The Pinned editor (`AutomationsPage.kt`) is a reusable **`SubLineEditor`/`SubLineRow`**: the **MAIN line (row 0) shows normally and its number badge / chevron expands** to reveal up to 2 **small, indented, dimmer nested sub-line rows** (they must look like sub-lines, not standalone lines — per user feedback) each with its own hide (eye) / char meter / overflow (move up/down + delete), plus an indented "Add row" (≤3) and the **"up to 3 chatbox lines" caption that only shows while expanded** (the old always-visible "Message rows" header was removed). The main row's number badge = expand (NOT hide — the main line has no eye; hiding is a sub-line concept); collapsed it shows a "+N" chip when sub-lines exist. Expand state is a page-scoped `pinnedExpanded` `remember` (sub-lines hidden until the line is selected). The combined total `CharBudgetMeter` renders only when `visibleRowCount > 1` (a single row's per-row meter already IS the total). `pinnedSubs` is snapshotted in PAGE scope + sub-rows `key(i, hidden)`-wrapped (same AnimatedVisibility-skip fix as the cycle mute rows). `buildCombinedText` sends `resolveTokens(renderVisible(afkMessage))`. **Phase 2 shipped = Cycle**: each cycle slide is now sub-line encoded in `cycleLines[i]` (same codec). `activeCycleLines()` returns `SubLineCodec.renderVisible(cycleLines[i])` per active slide; `cycleActiveRawIndex`/preset previews/`hasVisible` all decode. VM ops `cycleSlideSubLines`/`setCycleSubLineText`/`setCycleSubLineHidden`/`addCycleSubLine`/`removeCycleSubLine`/`moveCycleSubLine` re-encode into `cycleLines[slide]` via `persistCycleLinesPreserve` (presets + sync ride along). The editor reuses `CycleLineRow` as the MAIN line (sub-line 0) with its **number badge repurposed from mute → EXPAND** (mute moved to the eye, which cycle already had; overflow still moves/duplicates/deletes the whole SLIDE) + a trailing chevron/"+N"; expanding renders the compact `SubLineRow`s for sub-lines 1.. + Add row + a per-slide total. `cycleLineFields` is now keyed `"slide:sub"`, seeded from decoded sub-lines; `cycleSubs` (decoded per slide) is snapshotted in PAGE scope for the AnimatedVisibility-skip fix, and per-slide expand state lives in a `cycleExpanded` map.
- **Drag-and-drop editor (`AutomationsPage.kt`, scope = reorder within a list → promote/demote within a section → cross-section Pinned↔Cycle; cross-preset dragging deliberately OUT of scope).** Universal model: **UI-only until drop** — the picked item is STABLE for the whole gesture, offsets follow the finger via `graphicsLayer` (deferred reads = no per-frame recomposition), and the VM mutation fires ONCE on release, so any interrupted drag (invalid drop / tab-away / crash) reverts for free. Grab point is always the **number badge** (long-press; tap does nothing — expand is the chevron, hide is the eye — so grabbing can't toggle anything). Dragged rows are **opaque** (`lerp(surfaceVariant, primary, …)`); a **bright insertion bar** (`drawBehind`, page-scope `dropBarColor`) shows the drop position (replaced the old gap-shift). Drop targets use **absolute-position math** (dragged centre vs other rows' midpoints) for consistent placement near ends/between rows.
  - **Increment 1 — reorder within a list**: **cycle main lines** (`cycleDragIndex`/`cycleDragOffsetY`/`cycleRowHeights`, `cycleDropTarget`) and **sub-lines within a block** (shared `subDragKey`/`subDragIndex`/`subDragOffsetY`/`subRowHeights`, `subDropTarget`, `subDragHandle`/`subRowMod` — used by both the pinned `SubLineEditor` and each cycle slide's inline sub-rows). A dragged cycle line **auto-collapses its sub-lines** while held (`draggingIdx != idx` gate — cycleExpanded untouched so they reopen on drop and the gesture isn't disposed by a key change). **Auto-scroll**: `PageContainer` takes a hoisted `scrollState` + `onViewport`; near a viewport edge (`edgePx`=96dp) a `LaunchedEffect` loops `pageScroll.scrollBy` and folds the scrolled distance into `cycleDragOffsetY`. Dropped well outside a list → reverts. **CRITICAL gesture-survival rule (the "can't drag pinned rows / sub-lines" bug): the drag-active flag must NOT be part of a row's `key()`.** Sub-rows previously wrapped in `key(..., activeDragIndex == i)` / `key(..., subActive)` were DISPOSED+recreated the instant a drag started (the flag flips true on `onDragStart`), which cancelled the in-progress `detectDragGesturesAfterLongPress` — so only cycle MAIN lines (whose `key(idx, lineEn, expanded, subs.size)` carries no drag flag) could be dragged. Fix: keys are `key(i, sub.hidden)` / `key(idx, s, sub.hidden)` (no drag term); the `dragActive` opaque styling still updates because it's derived from the page-scope `subDragKeySnap`/`subDragIndexSnap` snapshots read INSIDE the card content lambda (forces an in-place recompose, key stable → gesture survives), and the float/drop-bar are deferred `graphicsLayer`/`drawBehind` reads of the LIVE `subDragKey` (reactive regardless of the lambda). **Post-drop expand reset**: `cycleExpanded` (+ `pinnedExpanded`) is keyed by INDEX, so a reorder/demote/cross-move shifts indices and would strand the expanded state on a DIFFERENT line (the "expanded line shows collapsed until placed again, and a random other line looks expanded" report). The cycle-line `onDragEnd` tracks `structuralMove` and clears `cycleExpanded` + collapses pinned after any actual move, so expand state is never stranded.
  - **Increment 2 — promote/demote within a section (Cycle)**: **demote** = drag a cycle line onto ANOTHER line's centre (`cycleDemoteHover`, middle 30–70% band) → `vm.demoteCycleLineInto` nests its rows as sub-lines of the target (green "fits" / red "won't fit" tint via `canDemoteCycleInto` + `demoteTargetValid`/`dragInvalid`; the insertion bar hides in demote mode). **Promote** = drag a cycle sub-line OUT of its block (`subDragHandle` `onOutside`) → `vm.promoteCycleSubLine` makes it its own new line.
  - **Increment 1.5 — unified auto-scroll + data-integrity guards (device-feedback round)**: (a) **Auto-scroll now runs for EVERY drag, not just cycle main lines** — the single `LaunchedEffect(cycleDragIndex, subDragKey)` loop runs while `cycleDragIndex != null || subDragKey != null` and folds the scrolled distance into whichever offset is live (`cycleDragOffsetY` or `subDragOffsetY`); `subDragHandle.onDrag` sets the shared `cycleAutoDir` from the finger vs viewport edges. Fixes "dragging Pinned / sub-lines doesn't scroll the screen".
