A swipe/uninstall/reinstall leaves a stale lock → denied on the new device until one of those frees it (deliberate friction, per the user). `applyCrossDeviceSync` no longer touches the lock (content-pull only). `firestore.rules`: `accounts` `delete` is now `isOwner() || signedIn()` (a device frees its own lock on sign-out; same bounded threat as create/update — needs the high-entropy `usr_` id, only ever frees the account for its real owner).

**Admin remote-logout (VRChat + Discord, the deny escape hatch)**: UsersTab detail has **"Log out VRChat"** and **"Log out Discord"** buttons (account-wide via `AccountModeration.applyAccountWide`). VRChat: writes `logoutVrchatAt = serverTimestamp()` to every device doc on the account AND **deletes `accounts/{vrchatUserId}`** (frees the lock so the user can sign in on a new device — works even if the old device is offline). Discord: writes `logoutDiscordAt`. The user's moderation listener reads both (fresh within 60s, once per timestamp via `lastVrchatLogoutMs`/`lastDiscordLogoutMs`): `logoutVrchatAt` → `handleAdminVrchatLogout()` (`VrchatAuthManager.logout` clears the session INCLUDING saved credentials so auto-relogin can't revive it → `loggedOutSignal` → OSC gate + `releaseAccountLock` + pipeline `ACTION_STOP`); `logoutDiscordAt` → `disconnectDiscordLocally()`. Both keys are in `firestore.rules` `ownerOnlyUserKeys()` (admin-only write).

**Admin watcher heartbeat**: When an admin selects a user in the admin panel's detail view, a `LaunchedEffect` writes `watcherActiveAt = serverTimestamp()` to that user's doc every 30 seconds. When the admin navigates away or selects a different user, the coroutine is cancelled automatically. The user app reads `watcherActiveAt` from its snapshot listener and feeds it into `AdminWatchState.updateFromTimestampMs()` — if within 60s, live-mode starts.

**Echo suppression** (per-field `lastSyncedValues` map): Every field (toggle or content) compares its incoming snapshot value against `lastSyncedValues` — what we last knew was on the Firestore doc. If `remote == lastSyncedValues`, the snapshot is an echo (from self-sync, watcher heartbeat, or live-mode write) and we skip. If `remote != lastSyncedValues`, an admin actually changed it and we apply. Comparing against current local state would break this: during admin watch, the heartbeat fires snapshots with the OLD field values (Firestore hasn't received the user's pending local toggle yet), so a `remote != local` test would revert whatever the user just toggled. Content fields fall back to current local state when no baseline exists yet. Both branches update `lastSyncedValues` immediately on every snapshot (whether applied or skipped), so subsequent self-sync echoes are correctly suppressed. The map is also bulk-updated after each successful `performSelfSync` write via `captureStateForSync()`. The first snapshot seeds the baseline via `seedLastSyncedFromSnapshot`. **`lastSyncedValues` is persisted to SharedPreferences** (`vrca_remote/last_synced_values_json`) as a JSON object after each successful sync so the delta writer has a baseline across cold starts — without this, every app restart would fall back to a full snapshot write.

**Admin read model (Phase 3, admin-side only)**: The admin panel uses NO live collection listeners — those re-read on every user-doc change across the whole base, the dominant admin-side Firestore cost.
- **Directory** (`AdminScreen.kt` + `UsersDirectoryCache.kt`): **locally cached, zero-read on open.** The full user list is persisted to the admin device (`UsersDirectoryCache`, SharedPreferences `vrca_admin_directory`, JSON with timestamps as epoch-millis). Opening Dashboard/Users renders the cached list **instantly with 0 reads**; online/offline is recomputed locally from each row's cached `lastActiveAt`/`offlineAt` (`isUserOnline`), so cached rows decay to offline naturally without traffic. A full `get(Source.SERVER)` pull (limit `sharedLiveLimit`, default raised **500 → 2000** so the whole base loads — `+500` can raise further) happens ONLY when there's nothing cached (first-ever load) or the admin explicitly refreshed / paged (gated by `lastFetchedKey = "$sharedLiveLimit:$refreshTick"`). **Incremental liveness refresh (reads scale with ACTIVITY, not user count)**: because the cache freezes between full fetches, the online dots would go stale on a long-lived admin process. So when the directory is shown with a *populated* cache older than `DIRECTORY_REFRESH_TTL_MS` (10 min), an **incremental** query re-reads only users whose `lastActiveAt` advanced since the last refresh: `whereGreaterThan("lastActiveAt", cursor).orderBy("lastActiveAt", DESCENDING).limit(sharedLiveLimit)`. The cursor is `lastDirectoryRefreshMs - REFRESH_SKEW_MARGIN_MS` (5 min, to absorb client/server clock skew), or `now - ONLINE_STALENESS_WINDOW_MS` on a session's first refresh (so it reads exactly the currently-online set). **Idle/offline users have a stale `lastActiveAt` that doesn't match the inequality, so they cost nothing** — their cached rows decay to offline locally for free. As users settle offline the read count DROPS toward ~1 (an empty query's minimum) instead of staying at the full cached count; the only users read are those who came online or whose **hourly heartbeat just kicked in** (which advances `lastActiveAt` past the cursor, so the next refresh re-reads them and the merge REPLACES their stale cached row). **A swipe-away does NOT advance `lastActiveAt`** — it only stamps `offlineAt` (and deliberately does NOT bump `lastSeenAt`) — so the `lastActiveAt` query MISSES a swiped user once their `lastActiveAt` drifts older than the cursor, leaving their `offlineAt` stuck out of the cache and the row falsely "online" until the ~65-min staleness decay. To fix this the incremental refresh runs a **SECOND query — `whereGreaterThan("offlineAt", cursor).orderBy("offlineAt", DESCENDING)`** — and merges its docs (de-duped by id via `associateBy`) with the `lastActiveAt` query, so a recent swipe's `offlineAt` reaches the cache and the row flips offline on the next refresh. Costs ~1 extra read per swipe (Firestore's per-query minimum when none), so it stays bounded by activity. The merge (`LinkedHashMap` over cached + fresh) also ADDS newly-active users not yet in the cache. This NEVER scans the whole collection. Both queries are factored into a shared local suspend `incrementalDirectoryRefresh()`. **Three triggers feed it: (1) directory tab entry / cold launch (the directory `LaunchedEffect`); (2) app foreground-return — a `LifecycleEventObserver` bumps `resumeTick` on `ON_RESUME`, folded into the effect keys (but deliberately NOT into `lastFetchedKey`, so a resume never forces a full scan); (3) a LIVE periodic loop — a separate `LaunchedEffect(needsUsers)` re-runs `incrementalDirectoryRefresh()` every `DIRECTORY_LIVE_REFRESH_MS` (30s) WHILE the Dashboard/Users tab is composed.** Trigger (3) is the fix for "a swiped user never shows offline until the ~65-min mark": the TTL-gated triggers (1)+(2) only fire on tab-entry / resume, so an admin who just SITS on the directory never re-read — the swiped user's `offlineAt` landed in Firestore but never reached `sharedUsers`, and the only thing that flipped the row was the local `nowMs` ticker decaying `lastActiveAt` past 65 min. The 30s live loop re-reads the `offlineAt` query so a swipe surfaces within one cycle; it skips a cycle if a full/TTL refresh just ran (`now - lastDirectoryRefreshMs >= DIRECTORY_LIVE_REFRESH_MS`, so it never double-reads) and auto-cancels when leaving the directory tab (`needsUsers=false`). **The live loop is ALSO gated on `isForeground`** (the `LifecycleEventObserver` now tracks `ON_RESUME`→true / `ON_PAUSE`→false): Compose keeps a composed `LaunchedEffect` alive when the admin app is merely backgrounded, so without this gate an admin app **left open on the Dashboard/Users tab in the background polled Firestore every 30s indefinitely** — the dominant "reads keep climbing even though no admin is watching anyone" cost (it has nothing to do with watching a specific user; just sitting on the directory tab, even backgrounded/screen-off, drove it). With the gate the loop pauses the moment the app backgrounds and resumes on `ON_RESUME` (which also bumps `resumeTick` → a TTL-gated refresh re-freshens the directory). The TTL clock (`lastDirectoryRefreshMs`, a process-lifetime `AtomicLong`) is stamped on SUCCESS only (a full fetch also stamps it) so a failure or a resume-cancel retries on the next entry/resume; plain tab switches / brief resumes within the TTL cost **0 reads**. A `Refresh` icon button (Users header) and the Dashboard refresh button bump `refreshTick` — with a populated cache this runs the cheap **incremental** refresh, NOT a full pull; **`+500` (limit change) IS a genuine full pull** (`limitChanged` check on `lastFetchedKey`'s limit segment — the incremental path can only merge recently-active docs, it can never extend the loaded base). An open user's 10s detail poll also patches that one row live (`onUpdateUserRow`) regardless of the TTL. Sorted by `lastActiveAt ?? lastSeenAt` desc. **Count-mismatch reconcile (the "new user is counted on the Dashboard but missing from the directory" fix)**: a doc with NEITHER `lastActiveAt` NOR `offlineAt` — a brand-new user still on an app version predating the hourly liveness model, which only ever writes `lastSeenAt` — can **never** match either incremental `whereGreaterThan` query (Firestore inequality filters exclude docs missing the field entirely), so it would never enter the cache no matter how often the admin refreshed; before this fix only wiping the admin app's data (empty-cache full fetch) would surface it. The Dashboard's already-paid total `count()` aggregation exposes the divergence: after the count lands, if `totalUsersCount != sharedUsers.size` (cache populated, first load not in flight), `fullDirectoryFetch()` (the extracted full-pull helper, shared with the first-ever load) runs ONCE and replaces the cache — missing users appear, deleted ghosts drop. Throttled per count VALUE (`lastReconciledTotalCount`, process-lifetime `AtomicInteger`): if a reconcile didn't resolve the mismatch (a permanent skew between the count's `adminBuild==false` filter and the directory's self-filter), re-fetching won't either, so it waits for the count to actually change. **Legacy `lastSeenAt` sweep**: once such a legacy-version user IS in the cache, their liveness would still decay (their heartbeat advances only `lastSeenAt`, invisible to the two incremental queries) — so `incrementalDirectoryRefresh(includeLegacySweep)` runs a third `whereGreaterThan("lastSeenAt", cursor)` query, gated on the cache actually containing a row with `lastActiveAt == null && lastSeenAt != null` (self-extinguishing once stragglers update or vanish) and passed `false` from the 30s live loop (current-version users mirror `lastSeenAt` on every liveness write, so the sweep re-reads ~the whole recently-active set — ~2x reads per run — acceptable at tab-entry/TTL/manual granularity, not every 30s).
- **Aggregate stats** (`AdminScreen.kt` + `DashboardTab.kt`): `count()` server aggregations (total/warned/banned, ban-evasion) run **once on tab entry and on manual refresh** (keyed on `refreshTick`) — NOT polled on a timer. The old 60s poll burned reads for no benefit at that cadence. 1 read per 1000 docs counted.
- **Selected-user detail** (`UsersTab.kt`): the ONLY live read — a 10s `get(Source.SERVER)` poll loop scoped to the single open user, keyed on `selectedDocId` **AND `isForeground`**. Backing out (`selectedDocId → null`) cancels the coroutine so reads stop INSTANTLY. **Foreground-gated**: a `LifecycleEventObserver` in `UsersTab` flips `isForeground` on `ON_RESUME`/`ON_PAUSE`, and the poll's key includes it (with an early `return` when `!isForeground`), so leaving a user's detail page open and **backgrounding the admin app no longer keeps reading every 10s** — this was the dominant *"reads keep climbing even though no admin is browsing"* cost (the directory 30s loop was already foreground-gated; this 10s detail poll was NOT, the one loop that escaped the gate). The last loaded detail stays on screen while backgrounded and the poll resumes (re-fetching immediately) on `ON_RESUME`. **Live instance count**: the detail's `📍 world (N/cap)` count is fetched **live from the admin's OWN VRChat session** (`VrchatAuthManager.fetchInstanceCount` on the user's `vrchatLocation`, polled every 10s while the detail is open), overriding the Firestore-synced `vrchatInstancePlayerCount` so the admin sees an instant count independent of the user's sync chain; falls back to the synced value when the instance isn't visible to the admin / not logged in. **Self-invite ("Invite me to this instance")**: a footer button (gated on `VrchatAuthManager.isLoggedIn`) calls `VrchatAuthManager.inviteSelfToInstance(ctx, vrchatLocation)` → `POST /invite/myself/to/{location}` (the website "Invite Me" behavior) sending an invite to the ADMIN'S OWN VRChat account for the user's current instance — works for invite-only/friends+/group; the user is NOT notified. Reads the freshest location from `selectedDetail` (UserRow doesn't carry `vrchatLocation`); disabled when not a joinable `wrld_` instance. **Invite/invite+ nonce preservation (the fix that made self-invite work for those two instance types)**: the self-invite needs the `~nonce(...)` access token embedded in the synced `vrchatLocation`. `/users/{id}` REDACTS invite/invite+ locations to `"private"` (dropping the nonce) even for the user's OWN account — friends/friends+ are NOT redacted, which is why those self-invites already worked but invite/invite+ failed with "not accessible". Two guards now keep the full joinable location: (1) `fetchPresence` no longer lets `/users/{id}`'s `"private"` DOWNGRADE a full `wrld_` location from `/auth/user` (a real `wrld_` location or an `"offline"` departure still overrides, so stale-presence correction is unaffected); (2) `syncPresenceToFirestore` keeps the full nonce-bearing location captured by the WebSocket self `user-location` event when the REST poll returns `"private"` (`"private"` means still-in-instance, so this can't resurrect a left instance — a real departure reports `"offline"`). The user's OWN `invite_me` (from a world-invite notification) was always fine because the invite payload carries the full nonce. **Invite-only fallback — the USER's app invites the admin (`SelfInviteCoordinator`, `SelfInviteStore`)**: `inviteSelfToInstance` (the admin inviting THEMSELVES) is rejected for **invite-only** instances the admin has no standing in (the `~nonce` fix only covers invite/invite+). So the button tries `inviteSelfToInstance` first (covers public/friends/friends+/group/invite+) and, on failure, calls `SelfInviteCoordinator.runAdminSide`, which flips who invites: the **USER** (who IS in the instance, so may invite anyone) invites the admin. Signaling is a Firestore handshake on `users/{deviceHash}` — admin writes owner-only `selfInviteReqId`/`selfInviteAdminId`/`selfInviteLocation`/`selfInviteAt`; the user's moderation listener (`handleSelfInviteSignal`, deduped by the signal ms via `SelfInviteStore.lastHandledSignalMs`) runs `runUserSide`, which (1) tries a **direct** `inviteUserToInstance(adminId, location)`, and (2) only if VRChat rejects it, runs a hidden **friend → invite → unfriend dance**: user sends a friend request to the admin, writes back `selfInviteStatus="need_friend"` (self-mutable echo of the reqId), the admin (polling the doc) sends its own request so VRChat **auto-befriends on the mutual request**, the user re-invites once friends, then **force-unfriends 10s later no matter what**. **Safety invariants**: a counterpart is only ever unfriended if it was NOT a friend before the dance (checked via `getFriendStatus`; the fallback only runs when the direct invite failed, which already implies not-friends — a real friendship is never severed); the unfriend obligation is persisted to `SelfInviteStore.pendingUnfriend` **before** friending, so a connection dip / app death still cleans up on the next pipeline connect (`SelfInviteCoordinator.drainPendingUnfriend`, wired into `onOpen`, runs on both builds). **No indicators on either side**: `SelfInviteStore.isFriendNotifSuppressed(id)` (true for the global owner VRChat id OR any id in the local pending-unfriend set) gates the shared `VrchatPipelineService` friend-add / friend-delete / friend-request / offline-unfriend-diff handlers — cache/dedup bookkeeping still runs, only the notification is hidden. The **user** suppresses via the owner id (set from `config/app.ownerVrchatId` — written by the admin build in `runAdminSide` — AND from the signal's `adminId`) + the admin in its pending set during the dance; the **admin** suppresses the user's id (added to its pending set at signal time, before the user's request can arrive). **Only friend-list notifications are gated** — bio/name/rank/online/etc. about the owner still fire. **Suppression covers ALL friend firing paths** — the live `friend-add`/`friend-delete`/`friendRequest` handlers, the offline unfriend-diff, AND the REST **backfill** friendRequest (that last one was a hole: the live path suppresses by returning BEFORE recording the `fr_` dedup id, so the backfill re-fired it on the next connect). **The unfriend-event race**: the friend-delete WebSocket event arrives AFTER the unfriend REST call returns, so the cleanup keeps the counterpart in the suppression set a `SUPPRESS_GRACE_MS` (8s) window PAST the unfriend before `removePendingUnfriend` — otherwise "removed from friends list" slipped through on the admin side (the user side is permanently covered by `ownerVrchatId`). **Invite speed**: `runUserSide` **retries the invite directly** (every 1s) instead of polling `friendStatus` — the invite succeeds the instant they're friends server-side, sidestepping VRChat's `friendStatus` eventual-consistency lag (that lag was a ~10s stall before the invite fired); the admin's mutual-request poll is 1s. **The retry delay RAMPS (1s→3s, `INVITE_RETRY_MAX_STEP_MS`)** so the normal case still fires in ~2-3 tries but a never-forms failure can't hammer VRChat at 1/s (a full self-invite is ~5-8 VRChat REST calls; the status poll is Firestore, not VRChat). **The unfriend cleanup is DETACHED** (`scheduleCleanup`, a process-lifetime `CoroutineScope`) so `runAdminSide` returns the "invite sent" result the INSTANT the user's app reports it — the cleanup's 10s force-unfriend + 8s grace used to run BEFORE the return, so the admin UI sat on the "processing" spinner for ~18s after the invite had already landed (the reported "UI keeps acting like it's processing" bug). The persisted pending-unfriend backup + drain-on-connect still cleans up if the process dies before the detached coroutine runs. VRChat friend REST added to `VrchatAuthManager`: `getFriendStatus` (`GET /user/{id}/friendStatus`), `sendFriendRequest` (`POST /user/{id}/friendRequest`, mutual auto-friend), `cancelFriendRequest` (`DELETE …/friendRequest`), `unfriendUser` (`DELETE /auth/user/friends/{id}`). `firestore.rules`: signal keys in `ownerOnlyUserKeys()`, status keys in `selfMutableKeys()`. The loop shows a spinner on first load then refreshes silently. 10s matches the cadence at which the watched user app refreshes its volatile fields. **Watched fast-offline (`WATCHED_STALE_WINDOW_MS` = 25s, `WATCH_RAMP_MS` = 45s)**: a watched user's app writes a liveness timestamp every 10s — `lastSeenAt` via the ViewModel live loop (dependency-free, the RELIABLE heartbeat) and `lastActiveAt` via the presence loop (which can stall if VRChat's `fetchPresence` 401s on an IP-invalidated cookie) — so a heartbeat that stops advancing **while watched** means the app died/was swiped, far sooner than the 65-min unwatched window can prove. The detail poll tracks the **FRESHEST of `lastActiveAt`/`lastSeenAt`** (`maxOf`, so a stalled `lastActiveAt` never false-flags a live user whose `lastSeenAt` is still advancing) and the wall-clock time it last advanced. **Loop-confirmed detection**: once the poll has SEEN the heartbeat advance at least once (`loopConfirmed`), a stopped heartbeat flips offline after just `WATCHED_STALE_WINDOW_MS` (≈2.5 missed ticks) — no need to wait the full ramp; before the loop is confirmed (its 10s loop hasn't started yet, or **the user was already DEAD when the admin opened them** — force-killed/swiped with no `offlineAt` landing, so there is no heartbeat to observe) it falls back to the `WATCH_RAMP_MS` (45s, reduced from 90s) grace so a live user isn't false-flagged mid-ramp. The 45s ramp is the fix for "opening an already-offline user doesn't flip them offline": `AdminRuntime.setSelectedUser` fires the `watcherActiveAt` write IMMEDIATELY on open, so a genuinely-LIVE user's first heartbeat reaches the poll within ~15-25s — 45s clears that chain with margin while flipping a dead user offline far faster than the old 90s. (If the ramp ever false-flags a live user, it SELF-HEALS the instant the heartbeat advances.) When stale, the poll **synthesizes `offlineAt = now` on the in-memory `UserDetail`** (zero Firestore write). This flips `isUserOnline` offline in the detail AND, via `onUpdateUserRow`, in the directory + cache — and is the ROBUST swipe→offline path because it does NOT depend on the swipe-time `offlineAt` write landing during the chaotic shutdown (that write is the instant-when-it-lands bonus). It self-heals: when the heartbeat advances again the poll stops synthesizing and carries the doc's real `offlineAt`. **For this to clear correctly, both the detail-view row merge and `onUpdateUserRow` treat the loaded detail's `offlineAt` as AUTHORITATIVE (no `?:` fallback to the directory row's value) — a null `offlineAt` from the poll must be able to clear a stale/synthetic marker** so a healed user returns online.
- The `watcherActiveAt` heartbeat (`AdminRuntime`) writes every 30s while a user is selected. No periodic writes from the user app outside the watched-user live loop.

**Initial-data gate** (`initialDataLoaded`): `performSelfSync` returns early until a loader coroutine has read every user-content DataStore field into ViewModel state. Prevents cold-start sync from writing empty defaults.

**First-snapshot skip** (`initialSnapshotProcessed`): The very first Firestore snapshot after listener attach is dropped — DataStore is the source of truth on cold start.

**Cross-device content sync (multi-phone, same VRChat account)**: When the same VRChat user logs in on two phones, the installs have DISTINCT `deviceHash`es → two separate `users/{deviceHash}` docs. To keep presets/messages/intervals consistent without merging the docs, `VrcaViewModel.applyCrossDeviceSync()` (public build only, launched from `init`) waits for the pipeline to surface the user's `vrchatUserId` (`VrchatPipelineState.presenceFlow.first { it != null }`, 120s timeout), then queries `users` for sibling docs `whereEqualTo("vrchatUserId", myVrchatId)`. It picks the sibling with the freshest `updatedAt`; if that is newer than this device's own `updatedAt`, it pulls the sibling's content into local DataStore + ViewModel state via the shared `applyContentFromSnapshot(snap)` helper (extracted from `applyRemoteContentBeforeSync()` — applies afkMessage, cycleIntervalSeconds, cycleLinesText, afkPreset1-3, cyclePreset1-5, spotifyPreset, each guarded by a value-changed check) and rebuilds the preview. So editing presets on phone A surfaces on phone B's next app open. The query keys on the already-written `vrchatUserId` field (a single-field index Firestore auto-provides). The content pull is read-only against siblings — no cross-device content WRITE — so the only authority over a device's own doc remains that device + the admin. **The query returns the device's OWN doc too (same `vrchatUserId`), so its `updatedAt` is read from the result set inline — no separate own-doc `get()`.** **Two throttles now**: the sibling QUERY runs at most once per 60s (`ACCOUNT_QUERY_THROTTLE_MS`, persisted `last_account_query_ms`) — short, because it ALSO drives the single-session claim and so must run on a genuine reopen (not be suppressed for 30 min) while still bounding OS-kill relaunch storms — and the expensive CONTENT pull keeps its own 30-min window (`CROSS_DEVICE_SYNC_THROTTLE_MS`, persisted `last_cross_device_sync_ms`). A genuine reopen after editing on the other phone is almost always >30 min from the last sync, so the content feature is intact.

**Single-session lock (multi-device, same VRChat account)**: only ONE device may use VRC-A per account — now a **HARD DENY** (see "Single-session HARD-DENY" above for the full model; the old take-over/"Use here" reclaim described here was REPLACED). The lock doc `accounts/{vrchatUserId}` = `{ activeDevice, activeSince }` is claimed on every login and denies any other device. `firestore.rules` for it: `get` signed-in, `list` owner-only, `create`/`update` `signedIn()` constrained to the two keys + valid deviceHash, `delete` `isOwner() || signedIn()` (lock release on sign-out / admin remote-logout). Admin build never participates (`!IS_ADMIN_BUILD`).

**Account-wide moderation fan-out (`AccountModeration.applyAccountWide`)**: since the directory collapses all of an account's devices into one row, moderating that row must hit EVERY device on the account (else a banned user just opens VRC-A on their other phone and dodges it). `AccountModeration.applyAccountWide(db, targetDocId, fields)` reads the target's `vrchatUserId`, finds every `users/{deviceHash}` doc sharing it, and writes `fields` (merge) to all of them — target included — in a single `WriteBatch`. A target with a blank `vrchatUserId` (never logged into VRChat) has no siblings, so it writes just the target (identical to the old per-device behavior). Wired into **warn / remove-warn / ban / unban** (`ModerationTab` — the `applyUidBan` branch only), the **kill switch** (`UsersTab` `killSignal` — only the currently-open device acts on a fresh signal), and `AdminViewModel.warnUser/banUser/clearPunishment`. **Device bans (`bannedDevices/{deviceHash}`) are intentionally NOT fanned out** — a device ban is by definition per-physical-device. **Admin directory dedup (`UsersTab.filteredUsers`)**: the directory now COLLAPSES rows that share the same `vrchatUserId` (the multi-phone duplicates) into one representative — `preferRow()` keeps the online row, then the most recently active. Rows with a blank `vrchatUserId` (never logged into VRChat) are never merged. Dedup happens at render time over the cached list (preserving sort order); the underlying separate device docs are untouched.

**Offline editing**: User-content edits land in DataStore immediately and survive process death. On reconnect, the next debounced write or app-close write pushes them to Firestore. Admin-content edits are preserved across user restarts via `applyRemoteContentBeforeSync()` (read-before-write on app-open). If both the user and admin edited the same field while the user was offline, the Firestore version (admin's) wins because it's applied before the app-open write.

**Admin edits to OFFLINE users (cold-open apply path + moderation-listener backstop — toggles AND content)**: There are now TWO cooperating paths that both apply admin edits made while the user was offline, both keyed on the PERSISTED baseline (`lastSyncedValues`, loaded from prefs in `init`):

1. **`applyRemoteContentBeforeSync()`** (runs once on cold-open BEFORE `performSelfSync`, wrapped in a 12s `withTimeoutOrNull`): (a) `ensureAnonAuth()`; (b) reads the user doc **from `Source.SERVER`** — NOT the default `get()`, which returns Firestore's LOCAL CACHE holding this device's OLD pre-edit values, so an admin edit made while the app was closed would be invisible (old-vs-old → nothing applied; this stale-cache read was a primary cause of "admin content/preset edits don't apply when the user reopens"); (c) if the doc exists it sets `remoteDocConfirmedExists=true` (see liveness-only gate below); (d) `applyContentFromSnapshot(snap)` + `applyOfflineToggleEdits(snap)` apply admin-edited content/toggles. **There is NO blanket snapshot-seed of the baseline** — the old `seedLastSyncedFromSnapshot` was DELETED because it POISONED the listener: it set the baseline to the server value for EVERY field (even ones the apply skipped), so (i) the moderation listener then saw `remote == baseline` and echo-SUPPRESSED un-applied admin edits, and (ii) combined with a stale local value it made the cold-open delta write the local value BACK, overwriting the admin's edit. Instead, `applyContentFromSnapshot`/`applyOfflineToggleEdits` update `lastSyncedValues[key]` **only for the specific field they actually apply**, leaving every other key on its prefs baseline.
2. **The moderation snapshot listener (`applyRemoteConfig`)** is the **backstop** and is now ALSO the path that catches the common case where the admin's offline edit arrives as the FIRST (and on a fresh device the ONLY) server snapshot. **The first-snapshot drop was REMOVED** (`initialSnapshotProcessed` deleted): dropping it silently lost every admin edit that arrived as the first snapshot — the core "nothing applies on reopen" bug. All snapshots now run through `applyRemoteConfig`'s baseline-vs-`lastSyncedValues` echo-suppression.

**Cold-open write is LIVENESS-ONLY for an existing doc (`remoteDocConfirmedExists`)**: this is the structural guarantee that the cold-open `performSelfSync` can NEVER clobber an admin offline edit. When the `Source.SERVER` read confirmed the doc exists, the cold-open delta SKIPS all content+toggle keys and writes only liveness (and, crucially, does NOT rebaseline `lastSyncedValues` to local afterward — that would drop the prefs baseline the listener needs and let a later write clobber a user's own offline edit). The admin's edits live on the server doc; the apply path + listener pull them into local; a later debounced/hourly write (non-coldOpen) pushes any genuine user offline edit up. The full-write branch now fires only when `isFirstSync && !remoteDocConfirmedExists` (true first install, doc absent) or `uidChanged` (rules require `uid`/`authUid` to match the new auth, so a delta omitting them is denied).

**Content vs toggles differ on the null-baseline fallback (the OS-kill revival safety):** `applyContentFromSnapshot` falls back to comparing against current LOCAL when a field has no persisted baseline (so admin content still wins on first sync). `applyOfflineToggleEdits` does NOT — it SKIPS on a null baseline. This is deliberate: `restoreFeatureSession()` runs first on an OS-kill revival and may have just resumed OSC sending with toggles ON; a null-baseline local fallback could flip a just-restored toggle off and STOP the revived user's OSC. So a toggle is only changed when a REAL baseline shows the admin genuinely changed it (`remote != baseline`) — admin DISABLE while OEM-killed is respected (sender stops), while an untouched toggle (`remote == baseline`) leaves the restored toggle and its OSC alone. With a real baseline the apply preserves the user's OWN offline content edits too: `remote == baseline` means the admin didn't touch it, so the user's local edit survives and the debounced write pushes it up; both edited → `remote != baseline` → admin wins (documented intent). (The earlier compare-vs-LOCAL design clobbered the user's offline edits whenever the cache read returned the stale server value.)

**Cold-open `performSelfSync` write rules (`TOGGLE_KEYS = afkEnabled/cycleEnabled/spotifyEnabled/spotifyDemoEnabled/timeEnabled`):** for an existing doc (`remoteDocConfirmedExists`) the cold-open delta is LIVENESS-ONLY — all content+toggle keys are skipped and the baseline is NOT rebaselined to local (see above), so it can't clobber. For a first-install cold open (doc absent) toggle keys are still skipped (they start OFF; writing OFF back would race an admin's enabled toggle) while content is written into the creating snapshot. The hourly/debounced (non-coldOpen) writes are full deltas and push genuine user edits. (True first-install with no doc: `applyRemoteContentBeforeSync` early-returns on `!snap.exists()` leaving `remoteDocConfirmedExists=false` and `lastSyncedValues` empty → `isFirstSync && !remoteDocConfirmedExists` full write creates the doc — toggles default OFF, nothing to apply.)

**Admin online detection (liveness-driven, NOT the `isOnlineInApp` flag)**: `isUserOnline(u, nowMs)` in `UsersTab.kt` is driven by **`lastActiveAt` freshness**, deliberately IGNORING the `isOnlineInApp` boolean. A user is online ⟺ `(lastActiveAt ?? lastSeenAt)` is within `ONLINE_STALENESS_WINDOW_MS` (~65 min) **AND** there is no clean-shutdown `offlineAt` marker newer than `lastActiveAt`. **Why the flag was dropped**: pre-app-scoped-VM public builds wrote `isOnlineInApp=false` from `VrcaViewModel.onCleared()` on EVERY Activity destruction (normal under memory pressure / "Don't keep activities") **without advancing `lastActiveAt`**, so a perfectly-live user showed offline with a fresh 28-min-old timestamp — the "Cherubic shows offline while her chatbox is clearly updating" bug. `lastActiveAt` is the ground truth because the hourly heartbeat (every 10s while watched) only fires while the process is alive. Under the hourly model an unwatched user writes `lastActiveAt` once per hour, so the window is ~65 min (5 min grace past the 60-min heartbeat); a watched user (10s loop) shows online within ~25s. **Instant-offline on swipe via `offlineAt`**: a clean swipe-away (`AppShutdown.buildOfflineWriteTask` + `VrcaViewModel.onCleared`) stamps `offlineAt = serverTimestamp()`. When `offlineAt > lastActiveAt` the row is forced offline INSTANTLY (no 65-min wait). This can never false-flag a live app — the next heartbeat advances `lastActiveAt` past `offlineAt` (self-healing, no clear needed). If the swipe-time write races the 5s kill timeout and never lands (the long-standing "swipe doesn't consistently go offline"), the staleness window still flips the user offline within ~65 min — instant when the write succeeds, eventually-consistent when it doesn't. VRChat session state is irrelevant to all of this: the liveness write uses Firebase anon auth + device hash and is fully decoupled from VRChat auth (a VRChat un-auth never blocks or triggers an online/offline write; VRChat presence fields are only additively merged when present). **The Dashboard counter uses the SAME `isUserOnline` predicate** (`DashboardTab.kt`) so the count and the directory rows stay consistent. The admin-side staleness sweep (`AdminRuntime`) still flips `isOnlineInApp=false` for genuinely-stale users — now only to keep the raw flag roughly accurate for any OLD admin builds that still read it directly; the current build never reads it for the online decision. `UserRow`/`UserDetail`/`parseUserRow`/`UsersDirectoryCache` all carry the new `offlineAt` timestamp; `firestore.rules` `selfMutableKeys()` includes `offlineAt`. The shared user-list state is `mutableStateOf<List<UserRow>>` (whole-value assignment) populated by the one-shot directory fetch.

### Settings page (`VrcaSettingsPage.kt`)
Grouped sections: **Chatbox display** (Invisible Chatbox Border toggle, description "Shrinks the chatbox background." + **"Time format" toggle** (label renamed from "24-hour time"; description "Change your time format to 12 or 24 hour mode." — `vm.time24h`/`setTime24hFlag` ← DataStore `time_format_24h`, default OFF = **12-hour with AM/PM** (`DateTimeFormatter "h:mm a"` with `Locale.US` for a stable uppercase marker) vs ON = `"HH:mm"`; consumed by `currentTimeString()` so it applies to the Time chatbox line, the preview, and `lastReportedTime`), **App** (version display + Battery Optimization request — promoted out of Permissions), **Permissions** (ALL permissions the app uses: Notifications (`ACTION_APP_NOTIFICATION_SETTINGS`), Notification Access, **Install updates** (`ACTION_MANAGE_UNKNOWN_APP_SOURCES` — in-app APK installs), Overlay), **About**, **Help** (collapsible FAQ rows via `HelpFaqRow`), **Debug** (collapsible). **The VRChat re-login takeover passes `onCancel` into `VrchatLoginScreen`** — Cancel renders INSIDE the login screen content (a `TextButton` above the disclaimer; the old top-bar Row placement clashed with the app bar). Same for the VRChat-tab takeover; onboarding passes no `onCancel` (hard gate, no cancel). Deliberately NO check-for-update button (a user could spam Firestore reads). Account/VRChat, Discord RPC, Notifications, and IP-slot sections were considered and rejected by the user.

### Update dialog browser fallback
The `UpdateDialog` (`VrcaApp.kt`) has a browser-download fallback TextButton ("Download failed? Tap here…") below the Download/Retry buttons that opens `info.downloadUrl` via `ACTION_VIEW` for devices where DownloadManager fails (storage perms, OEM download restrictions).

### Rich Content engine (`com.vrca.richcontent` — Phase 1 of the Updates/Announcements/Status revamp)
Shared block-based document system that will power the Update popup, Settings "What's New", and the in-app Announcements list (one model + renderer + media store, so a new block type lights up everywhere). Status popup is a separate re-skin, not authored content. **Phase 1 shipped = the engine only; NOT yet wired into any surface** (that's Phase 2 = updates, Phase 4 = announcements). Four files, zero new dependencies (public APK unchanged — Coil already present, `VideoView` will be built-in, parser is ours). **All four compile clean on both variants.**
- **`RichDoc.kt`** (pure Kotlin + `org.json` — the codebase's JSON lib): `RichDoc(v, blocks)` where `RichBlock` is a sealed class — `Heading(text, color?)`, `Text(text)`, `Bullets(items)`, `Image(url)`, `Video(url, poster?)`, `Callout(tone /* info|warn|success */, text)`, `Divider`. Serialised to a JSON string stored in ONE Firestore field (`bodyDoc`) on announcement/release docs → single doc read; media (images/videos) live on GitHub/jsDelivr, never Firestore. `toJson()`/`parseRichDoc(json)` round-trip (parse is tolerant: returns null on blank/garbage/empty, skips unknown block `t` types for forward-compat with newer admin builds). `mediaUrls()` collects every image/video/poster URL in order (feeds the media-store reference set). `toPlainText()` flattens for legacy fields / collapsed-card previews (strips inline markup). **BACK-COMPAT is load-bearing**: `legacyDoc(text)` parses an existing plain `notes`/`text` string into blocks using the SAME rules the old `parseTosBlocks` used (numbered `^\d+\.` headers, `*`/`-` bullets grouped into one `Bullets` block, blank-line paragraphs, literal `[label](url)` markdown-link collapse) so old announcements/releases render with their original structure. **`resolveRichDoc(bodyDoc, legacyText) = parseRichDoc(bodyDoc) ?: legacyDoc(legacyText)`** is the accessor every render site uses — prefer the rich doc, fall back to legacy. (The old update system therefore keeps working on old app versions AND old docs; a forced update can still show no notes exactly as today by leaving `bodyDoc`/`notes` blank.)
- **`InlineMarkup.kt`** (Compose ui-text): `buildInlineAnnotated(raw, linkColor): AnnotatedString` — supports `**bold**`, `*italic*`, `[c=#RGB/#RRGGBB/#AARRGGBB]colored[/c]` (a toggle/stack scanner), and **auto-linkifies bare `https://` URLs** into clickable underlined primary-tinted spans tagged `"URL"` (trailing punctuation trimmed off the link). `AnnotatedString.urlAt(offset)` reads the tag on tap. `parseHexColor(hex)` (3/6/8-digit → `Color`). `stripInlineMarkup(s)` for previews. Pure (colors passed in) so it's unit-testable. **No button/link block — a raw URL in text just becomes tappable.**
- **`RichDocRenderer.kt`** (Compose + Coil): `RichDocRenderer(doc, modifier, mediaScope)` renders blocks as a responsive vertical `Column` (spacedBy 12dp) — **never a freeform/absolute canvas** (breaks across screen sizes). Heading = `titleMedium` in `block.color ?: primary`; Text/bullets/callout via a private `RichText` (uses **`ClickableText`** + `urlAt` + `LocalUriHandler` — the pre-1.7 clickable-link path, because this project is on **Compose BOM 2024.01.00 / ui-text ~1.6**, where the modern `LinkAnnotation.Url`/`withLink`/`TextLinkStyles` API does NOT exist; ClickableText is deprecated in foundation 1.7 but still compiles as a warning); Callout = tinted `Surface` (`accent.copy(alpha=0.14f)` + a 4dp accent bar; info→primary / warn→amber 0xFFFFB300 / success→green 0xFF4CAF50); Divider = `Divider` (NOT `HorizontalDivider` — M3 1.1.2 in this BOM has only `Divider`, the modern name is unresolved — a real compile catch). **Image** = full-width `AsyncImage` at intrinsic ratio, **local-file-first via `RichMediaStore`** (`model = cachedFile ?: url` so it renders from disk once cached, Coil network fallback meanwhile) with tap → pinch-zoom fullscreen `Dialog` (mirrors `TutorialImage`). **Video renders an INERT poster+play-glyph placeholder this phase** — real tap-to-play WITH AUDIO (built-in `VideoView`, single active player) lands in Phase 5.
- **`RichMediaStore.kt`** (modeled on `AlertImageStore` + `TutorialImageStore`): on-demand media cache at `filesDir/rich_media/`, SHA-1(url) key, `.part`-then-rename, **two scopes in separate subdirs** — `ann/` (**persistent, admin-controlled**: `gcAnnouncements(referencedUrls)` deletes any `ann/` file not in the active-announcements URL set → admin remove/swap culls it from user storage immediately) and `upd/` (**ephemeral, view-scoped**: `clearUpdateMedia()` wipes it on update-popup / What's-New close; patch-note TEXT stays cached elsewhere, only heavy media is transient). `ensureCached(url, scope)` is scope-LOCAL (a URL used by both an announcement and an update keeps two copies so `clearUpdateMedia` can never delete an announcement's media); `resolve(url)` reads across both scopes. No auth cookie (media is public jsDelivr/github). **Phase-3 rule (not yet built): admin uploads use a UNIQUE filename per upload (`<id>_<epochMillis>.<ext>`), never overwriting — jsDelivr caches hard, so a new upload = new URL = live edits appear instantly, and the old URL falls out of the reference set → auto-culled.**

**Phase 2 shipped = Updates on the engine + Settings "What's New"** (`InAppUpdate.kt`, `VrcaApp.kt`, `WhatsNewStore.kt`, `VrcaSettingsPage.kt`; compiles clean both variants). `ReleaseInfo` gained a `bodyDoc: String = ""` field; `parseReleaseSnap` + BOTH real-time release listeners (`releases/{deviceHash}` + `releases/latest`) read `bodyDoc`. **`UpdateDialog` now renders `resolveRichDoc(info.bodyDoc, info.notes)` via `RichDocRenderer(mediaScope = UPDATE)`** inside the existing bounded-scroll Surface (cap raised 0.45→0.5 screen-height; Download stays pinned OUTSIDE the scroll; forced-update logic UNCHANGED — a forced update with blank `bodyDoc`+`notes` shows no notes exactly as before, so the **old update flow keeps working on old app versions and old docs**). A `DisposableEffect(doc)` at the top of `UpdateDialog` `ensureCachedAsync`es the doc's media into `upd/` while shown and `clearUpdateMedia()`s on dispose (ephemeral). **Settings → App → "What's New"** (`WhatsNewDialog`, `Icons.Filled.NewReleases`): shows the INSTALLED version's patch notes from `WhatsNewStore` (SharedPreferences `vrca_whatsnew`, keyed by versionCode) rendered via the same `RichDocRenderer`; media pulls on open, culls on close; friendly empty state for installs predating this feature. **Zero extra Firestore reads**: the `releases/latest` listener already holds the doc, so when `code == BuildConfig.VERSION_CODE` it caches that doc's `versionName`/`bodyDoc`/`notes` locally for offline What's New. No check-for-update button (spam-read hygiene). `parseTosBlocks`/`StyledBlocks` remain (still used by `TosGate`), just no longer by `UpdateDialog`.

**Phase 3 shipped = Admin builder (`RichDocEditor`) + GitHub image upload + rich Announcements AND Releases authoring** (`RichDocEditor.kt` new, `AnnouncementsTab.kt` rewritten, `ReleasesTab.kt` + `firestore.rules` edited; compiles clean both variants). **`RichDocEditor(blocks: SnapshotStateList<RichBlock>, githubPat)`** (`com.vrca.admin`) is the reusable block-list editor: per-block type-specific fields (heading text+hex color / text with `**bold** *italic* [c=#hex]` hint / bullets add-remove items / image URL + "Pick & upload" / video URL+poster / callout tone chips info·warn·success / divider), each block card has **move up / move down / duplicate / delete** controls (drag-and-drop is a deferred polish — reorder works via the arrows), an **Add block** horizontally-scrolling chip row, and a **live `RichDocRenderer` preview** (the same renderer users see). **Firestore cost = ZERO for the editor** (pure local state; media → GitHub). **`githubUploadImage(ctx, uri, pat)`** PUTs the picked image to the PUBLIC image-store repo (`Ashoska/VRC-A-Image-store`) via the GitHub **Contents API** with a **UNIQUE filename** (`rich/rich_<epochMillis>.<ext>`, never overwrite → jsDelivr cache-safe live edits) and returns the **jsDelivr** URL (`cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/Ashoska/VRC-A-Image-store@main/rich/…`); it reuses the release **`BuildConfig.GITHUB_PAT`** (the PAT needs Contents:write on the image-store repo — **on failure it throws GitHub's own error text so a token/permission problem is diagnosable on-device**). **`AnnouncementsTab`** now authors a `RichDoc` (Title stays a plain field; the old Body textarea is replaced by `RichDocEditor`): Publish/Update writes `title` + `bodyDoc` (JSON) + `body` (plaintext flatten via `toPlainText()` for old clients/notification) in **ONE write**, then **optimistically updates the local list (zero re-read)** — respecting the "one write, minimal reads" mandate. **Edit** loads an announcement's `bodyDoc` (via `resolveRichDoc`, from the already-read row → zero extra read) back into the editor; **Copy** loads it as a new draft; a "RICH" pill marks rich announcements. `AnnouncementRow` gained `bodyDoc`; the 50-doc refresh reads it (same query). Announcements `create/update` rule is `if isOwner()` (no key restriction) so **no rule change was needed** for announcements. **`ReleasesTab`** keeps its plain `editNotes` (GitHub release body + legacy `notes`) and ADDS a `RichDocEditor` (`releaseBlocks`); publish writes `bodyDoc` (JSON) + `notes` (= `editNotes` or the rich doc's plaintext fallback) — one write. **`firestore.rules`: `bodyDoc` added to the `releases` `create/update` `hasOnly([...])` allowlist** (announcements needed none). So the client's Phase-2 `resolveRichDoc(bodyDoc, notes)` now has an admin-authored source for BOTH updates and announcements. (Deferred: drag-and-drop reordering, and per-video transcode/upload which is Phase 5.)

**Phase 4 shipped = in-app Announcements surface** (`VrcaScreen.kt`, `HomePage.kt`, `AnnouncementSeenState.kt` new; compiles clean both variants). The Home "Announcements" `SectionCard` now renders each active announcement as a **collapsible `AnnouncementCard`** (status-banner style, **NOT dismissable** — they persist until the admin removes them): collapsed shows title + a one-line preview + a chevron (priority is NOT shown to end users — it only drives sort order + auto-expand); expanded renders the full **`resolveRichDoc(bodyDoc, body)` via `RichDocRenderer(mediaScope = ANNOUNCEMENT)`** (rich announcements; legacy plain-`body` announcements still render via the `legacyDoc` fallback). Sorted priority→recency; **priority > 0 auto-expands**. The old `.take(3)` cap is gone (collapsed cards are compact); `AnnouncementUi` gained `bodyDoc` (read in the existing foreground `where active==true limit 60` listener — same query, zero extra reads). **Unread model (`AnnouncementSeenState`, `com.vrca.ui.common`)**: a process-scoped observable `Set<String>` of seen ids persisted to SharedPreferences (`vrca_ann_seen`) — an announcement shows a **primary-color NEW dot** until its card is first expanded (`markSeen` on expand); `unseenCount(activeIds)` is available for a nav badge. **Media prefetch + cull**: a `LaunchedEffect(announcements)` in `VrcaScreen` collects every media URL across active announcements, `ensureCachedAsync`es them into the `ann/` scope, and calls `RichMediaStore.gcAnnouncements(referencedUrls)` — so an admin removing/swapping an announcement's media frees the user's storage on the next snapshot. **Zero Firestore cost** (the listener already reads these docs; media is on jsDelivr). The background `VrchatPipelineService.attachAnnouncementsListener` still owns the off-screen NOTIFICATION. (Deferred: a nav-icon unread count badge — the per-card NEW dot ships now.)

**Phase 5 shipped = Video (playback with audio + admin transcode/upload)** (`RichDocRenderer.kt`, `RichDocEditor.kt`, `app/build.gradle`, two flavor source sets; compiles clean both variants). **Public playback (ZERO new deps)**: the `Video` block now renders `RichVideo` — poster + play button; tapping plays inline **with audio** via the built-in `android.widget.VideoView` (`AndroidView`). **Single active player** via a process-scoped `ActiveVideoState.playingKey` token (each card holds a `remember { Any() }` token; starting one sets the key so every other RichVideo drops back to its poster and releases via `AndroidView(onRelease = { stopPlayback() })`). **Admin transcode is flavor-scoped so the PUBLIC APK gains 0 dependencies**: `transcodeVideoForUpload(ctx, uri): File?` is defined in BOTH flavor source sets — `app/src/publicApp/.../VideoTranscode.kt` is a no-op stub returning null, `app/src/adminApp/.../VideoTranscode.kt` is the real **Media3 Transformer** transcode (HEVC/H.265 + AAC + ~4 Mbps bitrate cap via `DefaultEncoderFactory`/`VideoEncoderSettings`, `@OptIn(UnstableApi)` so the marker doesn't leak to the shared caller). **`androidx.media3:media3-transformer/common/effect:1.3.1` are added via `adminAppImplementation`** (admin APK only; the flavor is `adminApp`). The shared `RichDocEditor` Video block gained a **"Pick & upload"** button: it launches a `video/*` picker → `transcodeVideoForUpload` (admin compresses; public stub returns null → raw fallback) → a **19 MB size guard** (jsDelivr ~20 MB cap; over → error telling the admin to shorten or paste a URL) → `githubUploadVideoBytes` (the image uploader refactored into a shared `uploadBytesToImageStore(bytes, ext, pat)` reused by image + video, unique `.mp4` filename, jsDelivr URL). Zero Firestore cost (media on GitHub). URL-paste for video/poster still works as a fallback. (Refinement deferred: resolution downscale via `Presentation` — bitrate cap is the real size lever.)

**Phase 6 shipped = VRChat status banner consistency pass** (`VrcaScreen.kt` `VrchatStatusBanner`; compiles clean both variants). The status banner was ALREADY built in the shared collapsible-card + chip language (`ElevatedCard`, `StatusBannerState.expanded` collapse, nested `Surface` sub-cards for "Affected systems" / "Latest updates", status pills) — it's effectively the north-star the other surfaces were brought up to, so Phase 6 is a light alignment, not a rebuild: the collapse chevron switched from `KeyboardArrowUp/Down` to **`ExpandLess/ExpandMore`** (matching the announcement + What's New cards) and the "View VRChat Status Page" link became a full-width Row with an **`OpenInNew`** icon (clear external-link affordance, consistent with how links read across the revamped surfaces). Data source unchanged (`status.vrchat.com` via `VrchatPipelineState.statusPageFlow` / `startStatusPagePolling`); no RichDoc (not admin-authored). **All three popups — Update, Announcements, VRChat status — now read as one card system.**

**Post-testing fixes (device feedback)**: (1) **Bold/italic now render** — `RichText` switched from the deprecated `ClickableText` (which wasn't applying `fontWeight`/`fontStyle` spans on-device) to `Text` + `detectTapGestures`/`getOffsetForPosition` for link taps; markup + links + colors all render. (2) **Quick inline color** — `buildInlineAnnotated` gained `/c#RRGGBB=word` (colors one whitespace-delimited word, no closing tag) alongside the verbose `[c=#hex]…[/c]`. (3) **Callout colors**: info `#3B82F6` blue / success `#22C55E` green / warn `#F4511E` red-orange (a warning reads reddish, not the old amber `#F59E0B` which looked too close to success/yellow), tint 0.18. (4) **Video delivery moved off jsDelivr to `raw.githubusercontent.com`** — jsDelivr returned "Invalid URL" for the uploaded `.mp4` even at ~8MB (it serves images fine but not the video); raw serves any type/size and the client caches locally anyway. `RichVideo` shows a buffering spinner + an error fallback while the (progressive) stream loads. (5) Admin editor: the Divider block renders a real `Divider` preview (not a text description); the video HEVC-compression caption removed (admin-only screen). (6) The Home `AnnouncementCard` container is now `surfaceVariant` (was the purple `secondaryContainer`) to match the app's card vibe. (7) **Upload-only editor** — all URL text fields removed from image/video blocks (button flips to "Replace …" when set); added an "Add poster image" upload for video blocks. (8) **GIF support** — `coil-gif` + a GIF-enabled Coil `ImageLoader` (`richImageLoader`, `ImageDecoderDecoder` API28+/`GifDecoder` below) used by `RichImage`, so an uploaded `.gif` animates (the image picker already accepts `image/gif`; no new block type). (9) **Rich notes on TARGETED releases** — the per-device "Push update to this user" flow (`UsersTab`) gained a `RichDocEditor` + writes `bodyDoc` to `releases/{deviceHash}` (the releases `bodyDoc` rule already covers `{docId}`). (10) **Orphaned-media cleanup** — `githubDeleteFileByUrl`/`githubDeleteMedia` (Contents API GET-sha-then-DELETE, no-op for non-our-repo URLs) are wired into announcement **delete** (removes all its media) and **edit** (removes media that was in the old version but not the new), so removed/replaced files don't pile up in the image-store repo. (11) **Instant video, no buffering** — `RichVideo` prefetches the clip into the local cache on render (`ensureCached`) and **plays the local file** (`Uri.fromFile`), so tapping play is instant even on bad internet; it only falls back to streaming (with a spinner) if the prefetch hadn't finished. (12) **Full orphan SWEEP** — `githubSweepOrphans(db, pat)` lists every `rich/` file and deletes any not referenced by an announcement or release (owner lists both — `releases` `list` rule changed to `isOwner()`); wired to a **"Clean up unused storage"** button in `AnnouncementsTab`. **Still open (optional)**: a **faststart** transcode (moov-at-front) — not needed now that video plays from the local cache; release-retract media cleanup.

**Post-testing fixes (round 2 — device feedback)**: (1) **Bold/italic now render via per-run PARAMETER-level styling** — a Samsung device silently DROPPED per-SPAN `fontWeight`/`fontStyle` (only span color rendered), so `RichText` was rewritten: `buildInlineRuns` produces a flat `List<InlineRun>` (bold/italic/color/url per run) and the renderer draws each run as its OWN `Text` with **parameter-level** `fontWeight`/`fontStyle`/`color` on `FontFamily.SansSerif` (a real bold/italic font), inside a `FlowRow`; `\n` splits into stacked rows. The old `buildInlineAnnotated`/`urlAt` AnnotatedString+SpanStyle path was removed. Links stay tappable (per-run `clickable`). Do NOT go back to SpanStyle weight/style — some system fonts ignore it. (2) **Streamed release-asset media upload** — media now uploads as raw-byte GitHub RELEASE ASSETS to an auto-managed `rich-media` release (`ensureMediaReleaseUploadUrl` caches the upload_url, reusing `githubCreateRelease`), replacing the base64 Contents API which inflated the file 33%, built a giant in-memory JSON, and did one blocking POST + a git commit per file — that's why uploads crawled and SATURATED the connection while APK pushes (the same streamed-asset path) were near-instant. Stored URLs are the release **download URLs** (`github.com/.../releases/download/rich-media/<file>`, CDN-backed; client caches locally then plays/loads from disk). `githubDeleteFileByUrl`/`githubSweepOrphans` now find + DELETE release assets by name/id. (3) **GIF is its OWN block** (`RichBlock.Gif`) — upload a `.gif` OR paste a direct GIF link (`media.giphy.com/….gif` etc.), rendered via the GIF-enabled `RichImage`. No Tenor/Giphy search picker: Google shut down the Tenor API (Jan 2026 deprecation, June 2026 shutdown, no new keys), Giphy went paid, and a picker would add a standing third-party dependency — so direct-link + upload only.

**Post-testing fixes (round 3 — device feedback, media playback)**: (1) **Video PLAYBACK is now Media3 ExoPlayer → TextureView, NOT `VideoView`** (`RichDocRenderer.kt` `RichVideo`, `app/build.gradle`). `VideoView` (and `PlayerView`) render into a `SurfaceView`, which punches a hole in the view hierarchy and z-orders BEHIND Compose content — so the video played as a **black box** in BOTH the admin editor preview AND the published announcement (same `RichVideo` either way, proving it was the player component, not the `AnimatedVisibility` context). The clip downloaded fine (`cached ~8 MB`) and re-encoding to H.264 didn't help, confirming a rendering issue. Fix: `androidx.media3:media3-exoplayer:1.3.1` added as a top-level **`implementation` (BOTH builds, ~1 MB to public APK)**; `RichVideo` creates an `ExoPlayer` (`remember(playUri)`, released via `DisposableEffect`) rendering into an `android.view.TextureView` (`exo.setVideoTextureView(tv)`) — a TextureView composites like a normal view (no surface hole). Aspect ratio tracked from `Player.Listener.onVideoSizeChanged` (`Modifier.aspectRatio`, coerced 0.5–2.5) so it isn't stretched; `STATE_ENDED` clears the single-active-player token; `onPlayerError` shows a clean "Couldn't play this video." **Did NOT add `media3-ui`/`PlayerView`** (also a SurfaceView + not cached offline). `@androidx.annotation.OptIn(UnstableApi)` on `RichVideo` (some Media3 markers). (2) **Admin transcode is H.264/AVC, not HEVC/H.265** (`app/src/adminApp/.../VideoTranscode.kt`, `setVideoMimeType(MimeTypes.VIDEO_H264)`) — HEVC decode isn't universal (a cached HEVC clip could download yet fail to decode), and at the same 4 Mbps bitrate cap file size is comparable (HEVC only buys quality-per-bit). (3) **`RichMediaStore.ensureCached` uses OkHttp** (`followRedirects`/`followSslRedirects`, 60s read / 120s call timeouts), not `HttpURLConnection` — GitHub release-download URLs 302-redirect cross-host to `objects.githubusercontent.com`, which `HttpURLConnection` didn't reliably follow, so videos never cached locally (images/GIFs masked it via Coil's own redirect-following URL fallback). (4) **GIFs animate inside the announcement card via a per-frame redraw** — a GIF (`RichImage(animate=true)`) runs a `withFrameMillis` ticker read INSIDE a `Modifier.drawWithContent { frameTick.intValue; drawContent() }`, forcing the draw phase to re-run each frame. Required because the announcement card wraps content in `AnimatedVisibility`, which stops forcing redraws once expanded, so Coil's `AnimatedImageDrawable` froze until an external redraw (touch/scroll) — the editor preview animated fine because it isn't inside an `AnimatedVisibility`. Ticker only runs while the GIF is composed (card expanded). Blank-URL image/video-poster blocks now early-return (no "Unable to create a fetcher" noise from unfilled editor blocks). (5) **Rich editor content + scroll survive the media picker** — `releaseBlocks` (`ReleasesTab`) and `targetedBlocks` (`UsersTab`) were plain `remember { mutableStateListOf() }`, so launching the image/video/gif file picker (which recreates the heavy admin Activity — the PROCESS survives, only the Activity is rebuilt, which is why the `AdminRuntime`-held APK pick survived but these didn't) reset them → the update-log content was LOST. **`rememberSaveable` was NOT sufficient**: on recreation `AdminScreen.sharedUsers` reset empty, so the selected-user DETAIL subtree (gated on the user being present) was DISPOSED for a frame past Compose's saved-state restoration window, dropping the saved blocks (and bouncing the admin to the top of the screen). **Fix = process-lifetime state in `AdminRuntime`** (same pattern as the APK pick): `AdminRuntime.editorBlocksFor(key)` / `editorNotesFor(key)` / `isEditorSeeded`+`markEditorSeeded` / `clearEditor(key)` hold the block list + plaintext notes + one-shot seeded flag keyed by editor id (`"release"` global, `"targeted:<docId>"` per user), cleared on publish. The picker `pendingImageIndex`/`pendingVideoIndex`/`pendingPosterIndex`/`pendingGifIndex` are `rememberSaveable` so the ActivityResult callback lands on the right block. **Scroll-to-top fix (two parts)**: (a) `AdminScreen.sharedUsers` is seeded SYNCHRONOUSLY from `UsersDirectoryCache.load(ctx)` (was `emptyList()`) so the directory is populated from frame one on recreation and the detail view is never disposed (zero extra Firestore reads — same on-disk cache the async loader already used; the full-fetch/incremental decision is unchanged); (b) both editors' scroll `Column`s use `rememberSaveable(saver = ScrollState.Saver)` (the detail one keyed by `docId`) so the position is restored. The detail 10s poll / watcher writes are untouched (still gated on `selectedDocId`+foreground, so leaving the detail still stops them). **Media UPLOADS also run on `AdminRuntime.scope`** (`AdminRuntime.uploadMediaBlock` + `uploadingTagsState`/`uploadErrorsState`, `RichDocEditor(editorKey=…)`): the upload previously ran on the editor's `rememberCoroutineScope`, which the picker's Activity recreation CANCELLED mid-upload → the block never got its URL ("I pick a file but it doesn't appear as added"). Now the upload (image/gif/poster + the video transcode+size-guard) runs on the process scope and writes the URL straight into the process-lifetime block list, so it completes across the recreation; progress/errors are read back from AdminRuntime StateFlows keyed `"<editorKey>#<index>"`. All THREE editors (releases `"release"`, targeted `"targeted:<docId>"`, announcements `"announcement"`) share this. **Update popup is FULL-SCREEN** (`UpdateDialog`, `VrcaApp.kt`): `Dialog(usePlatformDefaultWidth=false)` + a `fillMaxSize().padding(10.dp)` card; the rich patch-notes `Surface` takes `weight(1f)` (fills all space between header and the pinned Download button, was capped at 50% screen height), with an iconed `headlineSmall` header + version pill. **Retract clears the popup live**: retracting a release (normal or targeted) DELETES the `releases/*` doc; the user's `releases/latest` + `releases/{deviceHash}` listeners now, on `!snap.exists()`, drop the `UpdateDialog` (and lift the forced-OSC gate) when it's currently showing THAT source's version (tracked via `lastGlobalCode`/`lastTargetedCode`, zero extra reads) instead of leaving a retracted update on screen until restart. **Media caps + pairing (`RichDocRenderer`)**: every image/gif/video is height-capped at `MAX_MEDIA_HEIGHT` (380dp) with `ContentScale.Fit` (video via `aspectRatio(matchHeightConstraintsFirst=true)`) so a TALL clip is sized down + centered instead of filling the screen; a wide clip still fills width. **An image/gif block can hold a SECOND image** (`RichBlock.Image`/`Gif` gained an optional `url2`): the editor's image/gif block has an "Add second image/GIF (side by side)" button (uploads to slot 1 via `uploadMediaBlock(..., slot=1)` → sets `url2`); when `url2` is present the block renders the two images SIDE BY SIDE (`RichImageOrPair` → `Row` of two `weight(1f)` `RichImage`s), otherwise it's a single full-width image. A "Remove second image" button clears `url2`. (This replaced an earlier adjacency-based auto-pairing of consecutive image blocks.) Both editors now PRE-LOAD the existing content for EDITING: `ReleasesTab.loadCurrent()` seeds `editNotes`+`releaseBlocks` from the live `releases/latest` (`resolveRichDoc(bodyDoc, notes)`), and the targeted `LaunchedEffect(docId)` in `UsersTab` seeds `targetNotes`+`targetedBlocks` from `releases/{docId}` — each guarded (a `rememberSaveable` `editorSeeded` flag / `seededTargetDocId` per user) so the effect's re-run on the picker's Activity recreation NEVER re-seeds over in-progress edits, and `targetNotes` is now `rememberSaveable(docId)` too.

### Chatbox Rate Limit
VRChat's chatbox update limit is 0.5 seconds (`SEND_FLOOR_MS = 500L`). Music refresh is locked at 1 second (`MUSIC_REFRESH_SECONDS_LOCKED = 1`).

### Cycle sender reads LIVE lines (stale-snapshot fix)
`startCycle()`'s loop re-reads `cycleLines` (trimmed/filtered/take(10)) **every tick** — it must NOT iterate a list captured at launch. The old captured-snapshot loop caused: editing a cycle line mid-send showed the new text (preview + the music loop's null-override rebuilds read live state) but flashed the PRE-EDIT text at each rotation boundary (only the cycle tick was stale), until a Stop/Start re-captured the list. Empty-mid-send is handled (renders without a cycle line via `forceClearIfAllOff`, keeps looping so re-adding a line resumes); `cycleIndex` is clamped when lines shrink. **`cycleIndex` advances AFTER the interval delay, not right after the send** — during the wait it must keep pointing at the on-screen line so `currentCycleLinePreview()` (preview + other sender loops' null-override rebuilds) agrees with what the tick sent; the old post-send increment made the preview point one line ahead for the whole interval (invisible in steady state as a pure phase shift, visible as a disagreement the moment a line's content changed). Edits while sending surface at the next rotation tick by design (no per-keystroke OSC push — typing would spam partial strings to VRChat).

### Manual Send takeover + modes (`HomePage.kt` Manual Send card, `VrcaViewModel`)
Sending a manual message **pauses the automated chatbox (Pinned/Cycle/Music/Time) for 20s** (`MANUAL_HOLD_MS`, bumped from 10s) so people can read it. **On hold expiry the input field is CLEARED** (both the Instant-mode `scheduleManualRevert` and the Live-loop expiry branch set `messageText = ""`) so a fresh manual send starts empty instead of the stale text lingering ("retyping reshows old text"). **The field is kept in view while typing** via a `BringIntoViewRequester` (`manualBring`) on the `OutlinedTextField` + a `LaunchedEffect(messageText.text, focused)` that `bringIntoView()`s after a 60ms relayout delay — the live preview above the field grows as you type and would otherwise scroll the input line out of view (tester-reported). Below, "10s"/"the 10s hold" refer to this same now-20s window. **Revert respects the `oscSending` master gate (`revertToNormalChatbox`)**: `rebuildAndMaybeSendCombined(forceSend=true)` builds from the feature TOGGLES, not `oscSending`, so every normal caller guards it with `if (oscSending)` — but the manual-revert paths did NOT, so sending a manual message while NOT Started (oscSending=false) and letting the hold expire fired ONE automated send if any toggle was on ("using manual message when OSC isn't sending makes automation send when the timer finishes"). All revert/clear paths (`scheduleManualRevert` expiry, both Live-loop expiry branches, `clearManual`, and the `setManualLiveModeFlag`-off path) now go through `revertToNormalChatbox(local)`: when `oscSending` it resumes automation (`rebuildAndMaybeSendCombined`), when NOT it just `clearChatbox`es the one-off manual message and restores the preview to `buildCombinedText` — never transmitting automated content. Manual send itself still bypasses the gate (one-off always allowed) and starts no loops. The pause is enforced at the single automated chokepoint: `rebuildAndMaybeSendCombined` early-returns while `manualHoldActive()` (`now < manualHoldUntilMs`), setting the Home preview to the manual text. **It does NOT pause the sender loops themselves** — the cycle/music/keepalive `while` loops keep ticking (cycle keeps advancing `cycleIndex` + its timer in the background), only their OUTPUT is suppressed, so when the 10s expires the automation resumes from wherever it now is with no reset. A manual send is NEVER gated (it goes straight through `VrcaOsc.sendMessage`), so the user can send again mid-window and it re-arms the 10s. `scheduleManualRevert()` restores the normal chatbox (`rebuildAndMaybeSendCombined(forceClearIfAllOff=true)` + typing off) once the hold expires — this is what "goes back to normal" even when no automated loop is running (OSC off). Two Manual Send controls (compact segmented toggle + a Live-only toggle, DataStore `manual_live_mode`/`manual_scroll`, local-only): **Instant** (default — type + Send, one-shot, blocked when over the 144/142 budget via `manualCharBudget()`; the Send button disables + the field shows an error counter) and **Live** (the chatbox updates every 0.5s as you type via `startManualLiveLoop`, with a `/chatbox/typing` indicator; each change re-arms the 10s hold so the message stays up 10s after the LAST keystroke, then reverts). **The typing indicator is suppressed while the invisible/minimal-background chatbox is ON** — the combination glitches VRChat's in-game UI, so the `VrcaOsc.typing` setter gates on `minimalBackground` at the chokepoint (`effective = value && !minimalBackground`; a clear still passes through). Only manual-send paths set `typing`; the automated combined senders never do. **Scroll** (Live-only) shows the newest 4 VRChat chatbox lines (`MANUAL_SCROLL_LINES`) via `formatManualScroll(text, budget)`. **It WRAPS the text itself** (`wrapToVisualLines` — greedy word-wrap by estimated proportional width `MANUAL_SCROLL_WIDTH=26f`, a touch narrower than VRChat's ~30-unit wrap so VRChat never re-wraps our lines into a 5th; honors explicit `\n`, hard-splits an over-long unbroken word) then keeps the newest ≤4 wrapped lines within the char budget, joined with `\n` to lock the breaks — older lines scroll off the top. This fixed the reported bug where splitting only on `\n` treated a long unbroken message as ONE line and trimmed CHARACTERS off the front (`takeLast(budget)`) instead of dropping whole lines. `manualCharWidth` estimates glyph width (caps/wide glyphs wrap sooner). The over-budget counter is NOT error-colored in Live mode (Scroll handles overflow by design). **The in-app field RENDERS EXACTLY AS VRChat wraps it in Live+Scroll (WYSIWYG)** — `onMessageTextChange` (scroll branch) collapses the field to flowing text (`replace("\n"," ")`) and sets it to `formatManualScroll(...)` (the wrapped, newest-4-lines-within-budget string it also SENDS), cursor forced to the end. This REPLACED `trimManualToWindow` (deleted): the old approach kept the field as raw text and only trimmed off-window lines, but the in-app box is WIDER than VRChat's chatbox, so a message that wrapped to 3 lines in VRChat showed as 2 in the box — the field's lines didn't match the chatbox, and a line scrolling off made the field jump / typing feel off (tester-reported "not smooth to type in"). Now field == what's sent == what VRChat renders (same wrap, same 4 lines), so the visible lines always agree and the field never grows past the char budget (`formatManualScroll` windows the total ≤ budget). Newlines collapse to spaces while Scroll is on (a scrolling ticker is one flowing line in VRChat), so manual line breaks aren't kept in that mode. The **Clear** button is an `OutlinedButton` (was a bare `TextButton` that didn't sit right next to the filled Send); the Scroll toggle description is "Scroll to the 4 newest lines." A **Clear** button (`clearManual()`) drops the hold, clears the field + chatbox, and restores the normal chatbox. **The live loop re-pushes the CURRENT field text every 0.5s for the ENTIRE hold window — even while the user is paused — not only on change** (`startManualLiveLoop`): a genuine edit (re)arms the 10s hold and shows the `/chatbox/typing` dots (`typingOn` flag, dots drop when paused), while every tick still sends the current (scroll-formatted) text, so a keystroke that lands between ticks during fast typing is always reflected within 0.5s and the final bit is never stranded (tester-reported "the last bit doesn't send when typing fast"). Identical re-sends are cheap and keep the chatbox message fresh until the revert. It idles (breaks) only once the field is unchanged AND the 10s hold has elapsed (the revert restored the normal chatbox); a keystroke restarts it. The legacy `messengerUiState.isRealtimeMsg` path was replaced by `manualLiveMode` in `onMessageTextChange` (the overlay + legacy conversation surfaces now share the same Live/hold behaviour).

### Cycle resume across Start/Stop (no snap-back to line 1)
`startCycle()` used to hard-reset `cycleIndex = 0` and show position 0 on the first tick, so every Start/Stop (and toggle off/on) restarted the cycle at line 1. It now captures `val resumePos = cycleIndex` (preserved by `stopCycle`, and kept advancing during a manual-send takeover) and the first tick shows `resumePos.coerceIn(0, live.size-1)` — the cycle continues from where it left off. `recentCyclePicks` is still cleared on start (shuffle no-repeat window doesn't persist across stop, acceptable).

### Invisible Chatbox Border ("skinny bubble" trick)
A display preference (user-facing name "Invisible Chatbox Border"; UI = a toggle in Settings → "Chatbox display" PLUS an unlabeled eye `IconButton` on the Home preview card next to `SendStatusChip` — deliberately no text/explanation, the preview's reaction IS the explanation; the Home preview simulates the state: full `surfaceVariant` bubble when off, floating text over a tiny 44×16dp pill when on) that appends U+0003 (END OF TEXT) + U+001F (UNIT SEPARATOR) to every outgoing `/chatbox/input` payload — the same undocumented trick VRCOSC ("Use Minimal Background") and MagicChatbox (`BlankEgg`) use. The control chars corrupt VRChat's bubble text-bounds measurement, collapsing the background to its minimum pill while the text renders normally (full removal is impossible — the pill is the client's minimum background, rendered on other players' clients). Implementation: `VrcaOsc.minimalBackground` (`@Volatile`, like `blocked`) + `withMinimalBackground(text)` applied in `sendMessage`/`sendRealtimeMessage` — caps content at 142 then appends, so the suffix can never be trimmed by the 144 limit; blank text is left blank (a chatbox CLEAR must stay a truly empty payload or an empty mini-bubble would linger in-game). `buildCombinedText` shrinks the builder budget to `VRC_MAX_CHARS - MINIMAL_BG_RESERVED_CHARS` (142) when ON so the preview matches what fits; OFF returns the 2 chars to content. The suffix is added ONLY at the OSC chokepoint — never in `combinedPreviewText`, conversation history, or Firestore. State: `VrcaViewModel.minimalChatboxBg` + `setMinimalChatboxBgFlag()` (the `Flag` suffix avoids the JVM setter clash, same as the feature toggles), persisted via DataStore key `minimal_chatbox_bg` like the notification toggles (survives close/swipe/restart; NOT a sender toggle — independent of `oscSending`/`FeatureSessionStore`, not synced to Firestore, applies to manual conversation sends too). An init collector seeds it into both `VrcaOsc` instances on every VM creation (incl. headless revival). Risk note: undocumented VRChat quirk; if VRChat patches it, the chars render as nothing and the bubble is simply normal again.

### NowPlaying
- Ad/DJ detection: Spotify uses text-based matching (`com.spotify.music` only) to prevent false positives on regular songs, plus a player-agnostic `advertisement*` (title OR artist) check. **YouTube and YouTube Music need DIFFERENT ad signals** (`NowPlayingListenerService`) — on-device traces proved seekability is NOT a universal tell:
  - **YouTube VIDEO app (`com.google.android.youtube`) uses seekability**: it strips `ACTION_SEEK_TO` from `PlaybackState.getActions()` during pre-roll/mid-roll ads (you can't scrub an ad), so `seek=N` + finite duration (≤5 min) → ad, `seek=N` + zero/absent duration → live stream. A **buffering gate** (ignore non-seekable samples while `STATE_BUFFERING`/`STATE_CONNECTING` — a loading track is non-seekable but buffering, an ad PLAYS) + **entry hysteresis** (`YT_AD_ENTRY_STREAK` = 2 consecutive non-seekable PLAYING samples, `ytSeekNStreakByPackage`) de-noise the raw signal so a song switch can't flash "Ad".
  - **YouTube MUSIC (`com.google.android.apps.youtube.music`) uses a RELATIVE DURATION COLLAPSE, not seekability and not a fixed ceiling**: an on-device trace proved YT Music reports `seek=Y state=PLAYING` for BOTH songs AND ads (a song read `dur=165s`, an ad read `dur=15s`) — so `ACTION_SEEK_TO` is useless here (the long-standing "YT Music ads never detected / show the song's name" cause — the old code assumed `seek=N`=ad, but YT Music ads stay seekable). Each title's **established (longest-seen) duration** is tracked (`songEstablishedDurByPackage`, seeded on a title change, raised whenever the same title later reports longer). An ad is flagged by EITHER of two shapes, **both NOT gated on `playing`** (a PAUSED ad keeps `title=song` + the ad's short duration — verified: a paused ad still read `dur=15s` — so detection must survive a pause; gating on `playing` was the cause of "pausing mid-ad reverts to the song title"):
    - **(1) COLLAPSE** — the title was already established LONG (a real song played) and the SAME title (`lastSongTitleByPackage`) is now suddenly playing a duration collapsed `≥ YT_MUSIC_AD_COLLAPSE_MS` (20s) below its established length (under a `YT_MUSIC_AD_ABS_MAX_MS` 3-min sanity cap). This is the classic mid-roll ad reusing the current song's title, at any ad length (44s/55s/90s — no fixed ceiling, which an earlier fixed 45s ceiling got wrong by missing 55s+ ads).
    - **(2) SHORT-UNCONFIRMED** — a short duration (`1..YT_MUSIC_AD_MAX_MS`, **60s**) on a title we have NEVER seen longer than an ad (`establishedDur ≤ 60s`). This catches the **PRE-ROLL ad**, which on YT Music shows the **UPCOMING song's title at the ad's short length** (confirmed on-device: an ad displayed the real song's title at `dur=30s`). Because that title's only-ever-seen length is the ad's 30s, there is no long baseline to collapse from, so the collapse rule alone missed it — this was the root "**first ad of a pod is missed, second one works**" cause (the second ad reuses a title the real song already established long → caught by COLLAPSE). Once the real song plays and establishes a length `> 60s`, the title is "confirmed long" and only COLLAPSE applies to it. **Tradeoff: a genuinely short (<60s) real song would be read as an ad** — accepted because the user confirmed those short durations are ads showing the song title, not real short songs. (`YT_MUSIC_AD_MAX_MS` was lowered 75s→60s per the user.)
    The 2-sample entry hysteresis (`YT_AD_ENTRY_STREAK`) rejects a transient short-duration frame at song start.
    - **(iTunes ground-truth, additive)** — `ITunesDurationLookup` (`com.vrca.nowplaying`) hardens the above by fetching the song's REAL duration from the free, key-less **iTunes Search API** (`https://itunes.apple.com/search?term=<artist title>&entity=song&limit=1` → `results[0].trackTimeMillis`). On each YT Music title it `prefetch()`es (background single-thread executor, process-lifetime `ConcurrentHashMap` cache incl. misses-as-0, in-flight de-dup so the 500ms poll can't storm it); when a real duration is cached it **RAISES** `establishedDur` (and `songEstablishedDurByPackage`) for that title. This catches the **pre-roll ad on its FIRST appearance** even when its duration exceeds the 60s short-unconfirmed cap: iTunes proves the upcoming song is long, so the ad's short duration trips COLLAPSE immediately instead of waiting for the real song to establish a baseline. Strictly additive — a network failure / no match leaves the device-duration heuristics untouched.
