(Caveat: if iTunes matches a longer alternate version of a genuinely-short real song it could over-flag, but that's the same documented short-song tradeoff.)
  - **Shared de-noising for both** (these caused three reported bugs — a false "Ad" flash on song switch, the real song title leaking mid-ad, and the ad number randomly climbing 1→2→3): **(3) Window-locked title** — once an ad opens the special window, `specialWindowIsAdByPackage[pkg]=true` and the redacted `title="AD"` is FORCED for the WHOLE 2s window (YouTube `isSpecialWindowActive` branch), so a single per-cycle flap can no longer leak the real song title; `lastAdInfoByPackage` preserves the parsed "X of Y" across the locked cycles. **(4) Per-break counter reset** — `VrcaViewModel.adSegmentCount` resets to 0 when `isAdNow` goes true→false (ad break ended), so each break starts at "Ad 1" instead of climbing across the session; combined with the window lock keeping `isAdNow` continuously true through one ad, the count no longer increments mid-ad. **Detection appears ~1s after the ad starts (the 2-sample hysteresis), not instantly.**
  - **Live stream detection** (video app only): `NowPlayingSnapshot.isLive` flows through `VrcaViewModel.nowPlayingIsLive`; `buildNowPlayingLines()` shows `"● LIVE"` instead of a progress bar. Live streams are not persisted to disk (restoring "LIVE" on next launch would be nonsense). (The temporary `NowPlayingDebug` on-device trace + its Settings → Debug "YouTube ad signals" card were REMOVED once detection was confirmed stable.)
- **One-line title fitting (`TitleCleaner`, `com.vrca.nowplaying`)**: YouTube / YT Music (and sometimes Spotify) titles are long and padded with non-identifying cruft, and the old `buildNowPlayingLines()` did a blunt `safeTitle.take(maxLine-1) + "…"` that chopped mid-word and could destroy the song's identity. `TitleCleaner.fitOneLine(title, artist, maxLine=42)` now fits `"artist — title"` onto ONE chatbox line via a cheapest-loss→most-loss ladder, stopping the moment it fits: **(1)** strip Tier-1 cruft (FREE, zero identity loss) — `(Official Music Video)`/`(Lyrics)`/`(Visualizer)`/`(Remastered 2021)`/`(HD)`/`(4K)`, the YT Music `- Topic` artist suffix, trailing `| Label` promo segments, Spotify's trailing ` - <tag>` version suffixes, `#shorts` hashtags, and emoji/pictographs; **(2)** de-dup an artist embedded in the title (`"Artist - Song"` → `"Song"` since the artist is shown separately); **(3)** if still over, drop Tier-2 borderline tags (`feat.`/`ft.`/`deluxe`/`radio edit`/`explicit`/`extended`…); **(4)** if still over, drop the separate artist (the song name identifies it); **(5)** last resort — word-boundary truncate with `…`, preferring a clean cut unless it wastes >12 chars (then hard-cut to keep more identifying text). **Tier-3 keep-words are NEVER stripped** (a bracket group / ` - ` suffix containing one is left intact even if it also contains a cruft word): `remix`/`vip`/`bootleg`/`acoustic`/`live`/`unplugged`/`instrumental`/`demo`/`cover`/`sped up`/`slowed`/`nightcore`/`taylor's version`/possessive- & language-`version`/`from "Movie"`/`soundtrack` — these denote a DIFFERENT recording, so removing them would change which track it is. Cruft is matched only inside `(...)`/`[...]` groups or after a `|`/` - ` separator (bare title words are never stripped, so a song literally named "Video Games" survives). Applied to both the live-stream line and the main music line. All regexes JVM-validated. **"Fits one line" is a DUAL constraint** (`fitsOneLine`): ≤ 42 chars AND ≤ `VISUAL_LINE_UNITS` (28.5f — backed off from the ~30 empirical edge for estimator-noise margin) estimated visual width via `visualWidth()`/`charWidth()` — VRChat's chatbox wraps centered PROPORTIONAL text at ~30 average-character widths, so a 41-char caps-heavy title within the char budget still wrapped to a second rendered line ("Theodore Roosevelt Would LOVE…" wrapped at ~29 chars; "…the DUMBEST mfs on earth" at ~31 — the empirical calibration). Weights: **`…`=2.0 — the ellipsis is three dots on ONE char; counting it 1.0 made `truncate()` emit a "shortened to fit" title whose appended `…` itself pushed the line over, wrapping the whole last word in-game**, `m w M W`=1.4, uppercase/digits=1.2, narrow (`i j l t f r`, space, light punctuation)=0.6, CJK/fullwidth=2.0, else 1.0. The 42-char cap is kept so a skinny all-lowercase title can still use every char that genuinely fits. The last-resort `truncate` walks the prefix by width (ellipsis included) before the word-boundary cut. **User opt-out**: `TitleCleaner.rawOneLine(rawTitle, rawArtist, maxChars)` is the "filters off" path (Media → Now Playing "Filter Titles" toggle) — it does NONE of the above (no cruft strip, no artist-dedup, no one-line fit), just `"artist — title"` with a hard char cap; the gate lives in `buildNowPlayingLines()` (`if (musicCleanTitles) fitOneLine(...) else rawOneLine(..., VRC_MAX_CHARS)`).
- **Ads keep the progress bar**: `buildNowPlayingLines()`'s ad branch renders a bare `"Ad"` label PLUS a progress bar driven by the ad's OWN position/duration (forced playing) — the bar used to vanish during ads, leaving a bare label. No brand/title is shown, so nothing leaks. When the ad reports no duration (some YT Music audio ads) it falls back to just the label.
- **Ad label is a bare `"Ad"`**: ads render as just `"Ad"` (no index). The player's ad index ("Ad 1 of 1") was unreliable, so it was dropped per the user — the label is a constant `"Ad"`. (`parseAdIndex`/`nowPlayingAdInfo`/`adSegmentCount` still exist and are still set, but the label no longer consumes them.) **The ad-label block in `buildNowPlayingLines()` is gated on a dedicated `nowPlayingIsAd` flag (set from the collector's `isAdNow`), checked BEFORE `isSpotifyDj` with an early `return`.** This fixes "the ad never shows": a Spotify ad blanks the artist (`title="AD"`, `artist=""`), which made `isSpotifyDj` (`safeArtist.isBlank()`) evaluate TRUE — so the old `if (nowPlayingSpecialActive && !isSpotifyDj)` guard was false and the whole ad block was skipped, falling through to render a bare "AD" title + progress bar. An ad and a DJ segment both blank metadata; only the explicit `nowPlayingIsAd` flag distinguishes them, so the ad path must be checked first and independently of `isSpotifyDj`.
- Special window: Spotify/generic ads use a 10s window (cleared immediately once real metadata appears). **YouTube ads use a short 2s window** refreshed every 500ms by the continuous poll while seek=N holds; after the ad ends the window expires naturally in ≤2s. YouTube packages are EXEMPT from the eager `clearSpecialWindow` + `stopPoll` in the `isSpecialWindowActive` branch — their `onMetadataChanged` callback can race with `onPlaybackStateChanged`, reading a stale `controller.playbackState` (still seek=Y from the previous song) before the PlaybackState update propagates. That race falsely triggered "ad ended" → cleared the special window AND killed the continuous pause-detection poll, so (a) the ad indicator never appeared (showed the song name instead of "Ad N") and (b) the pause detector stopped receiving samples. The fix: for YouTube packages, the `isSpecialWindowActive` branch is a no-op — the poll keeps running, the window stays alive while the ad is ongoing (refreshed each seek=N cycle), and expires organically after the ad ends.
- **YouTube / YouTube Music ARE detected** (earlier notes claiming they can't be were wrong — they DO publish a MediaSession with title/artist/position). Two quirks are handled in `NowPlayingListenerService`: (1) **Pickup delay** — a freshly-started session used to surface only when the app posted its media notification (a few seconds late). An `MediaSessionManager.OnActiveSessionsChangedListener` (registered in `onListenerConnected`, unregistered in `onListenerDisconnected`) now wires the controller the instant the session goes active, so detection is near-immediate (future-proof for ALL media apps, not just YouTube). (2) **Progress kept moving while paused** — YouTube keeps reporting `STATE_PLAYING` + `playbackSpeed=1f` even when PAUSED, so the only reliable pause signal is that the raw `position` stops advancing across snapshots. That position-stall check lives in `NowPlayingState` and needs fresh samples every ~500ms, but YouTube's `MediaController` fires almost no callbacks while paused — so `ensureControllerForPackage` now starts a **continuous 500ms poll for YouTube packages** (`startPollForRealTrack`, exempt from the 10-min cap that the Spotify ad/DJ poll uses; it self-stops on controller change / teardown / session-destroy / notif-removed). The poll pushes a snapshot EVERY cycle for YouTube (other players still push only on real change / every 4s), so the stall detector reaches its 2-cycle threshold within ~1s of a pause → forces `isPlaying=false` → `effectiveIsPlaying` goes false → `buildNowPlayingLines` stops extrapolating and freezes the progress bar at the real snapshot. On resume the real position is being pushed every 500ms, so the bar continues from the true timestamp with no teleport.
- Motion-based play detection with YouTube-specific stall tracking. **YouTube Music play/pause is the INVERSE case and must NOT use the position-stall hack**: its raw `position` field is unreliable — it can ADVANCE while paused and FREEZE while playing — so the motion/stall detector inverted it (playing showed as paused, paused showed as playing, with the progress bar moving/freezing backwards). YT Music's reported `PlaybackState.state` IS honest, so it is trusted directly: `NowPlayingState.update()` sets `inferredIsPlaying = snapshot.isPlaying` for `com.google.android.apps.youtube.music` (skipping the stall block, which now applies ONLY to the video app `com.google.android.youtube`), and `computeDisplayedPlaying()` short-circuits `return nowPlayingReportedIsPlaying` for YT Music before any motion logic. The video app keeps the `STATE_PLAYING`+`speed=1f`-while-paused stall workaround unchanged.
- **Last-track persistence across revival (`NowPlayingState.attach`/persist)**: the snapshot lives only in process memory, so after an OS-initiated process kill + headless ViewModel revival (`KeepAliveService` → `ensureRuntimeViewModel`) a PAUSED track had no active MediaSession to re-push and the chatbox NowPlaying line just blanked out. `NowPlayingState` now persists the last *detected, non-special* track (title/artist/duration/position/package) to SharedPreferences (`vrca_nowplaying`) inside `update()` and `pauseIfActivePackage()`, and `VrcaApplication.onCreate()` calls `NowPlayingState.attach(context)` to re-seed `_state` from disk **as paused** (re-anchoring `positionUpdateTimeMs` to `now`, `speed=0`, `isPlaying=false`) before any ViewModel collects — so the previous track shows immediately instead of disappearing. `attach` is idempotent and only restores while the in-memory state is still blank (never clobbers a live one). Ads/DJ/special windows are never persisted (restoring "AD" would be nonsense). A genuine hard clear (`clearIfActivePackage`) removes the persisted entry so a real stop doesn't resurrect a stale track. Also fixes the plain cold-start case, not just headless revival.
- **Blank-metadata push keeps the last track (close-blanking fix)**: closing the media app (vs Spotify, which keeps its MediaSession alive in the background) tears its `MediaSession` down — YouTube/YouTube Music in particular fire `onMetadataChanged(null)` / the 500ms poll reads empty metadata right around teardown, pushing a `detected=false` (blank title+artist) snapshot. The old `NowPlayingState.update()` wrote that blank straight into `_state`, instantly wiping the preview AND the OSC chatbox line ("closing YouTube removes the text immediately; Spotify doesn't"). The disk persistence above did NOT rescue this — `attach` only re-seeds when in-memory state is blank at *process start*, but here the process is still alive. **`update()` now treats a blank push for an already-`detected` track as a PAUSE: it freezes the last known title/artist (`speed=0`, `isPlaying=false`, clears `specialActive`/`adInfo`) instead of clearing**, mirroring `pauseIfActivePackage`'s keep-last behavior — so YouTube freezes the last track on close exactly like Spotify. A genuine hard stop still goes through `clearIfActivePackage()` (the only remaining blank path). **YouTube ad detection remains a known limitation**: during a YouTube ad the MediaSession keeps the VIDEO's title but reports the AD's (short) duration/position, so the progress bar shrinks to the ad then jumps back. `classifySpecial()` only flags ads when the title/artist starts with `"advertisement"` (Spotify's behavior); YouTube exposes no reliable ad flag, so this is intentionally left as-is rather than adding a fragile duration-collapse heuristic that would false-positive on Shorts / short videos / chapters.
- Crystal progress bar (preset 3) uses filled diamonds (U+25C6) before the marker position
- **Progress bar time separator is plain `/`; the PAUSED bar drops one filler slot instead** (`pausedTrim` in `renderProgressBar` / `renderSoundwaveBar`). The real overflow culprit was the PAUSE marker: `⏸` (U+23F8) gets emoji presentation in VRChat's chatbox font (and Android's), rendering ~2-3× wider than the text-glyph playing dot `◉` — so the bar+time line wrapped when paused but not when playing. Rather than swapping the emoji, the bar sheds ONE identical filler slot while `dotIsPlaying == false` (hearts 8→7 inner, presets 2/3/5 10→9, soundwave 8→7); every slot is the same glyph so it just reads one char shorter, the marker index rescales automatically, and the slot returns on resume. If in-game testing shows paused still wrapping, the tuning knob is dropping one more slot. Separator-swap attempts that FAILED before this approach (don't retry them): ASCII `|` has wide punctuation side bearings (rendered `14:47 | 20:5` with gaps, wrapped); FRACTION SLASH `⁄` U+2044 triggers fraction composition (`41:³⁰⁄₅₁:08` super/subscript digits); dental click `ǀ` U+01C0 was fine but unnecessary once the real culprit (the pause emoji) was found. **YouTube video does NOT get extra title lines** — VRChat's chatbox renders centered proportional text at ~30 average chars width, so a wider char budget just keeps the artist prefix and wraps worse visually; the single-line `TitleCleaner.fitOneLine()` (42-char cap + 30-unit visual width) with artist-drop escalation is the correct behavior for long video titles.

### UI Naming
- The "AFK" feature is renamed to "Pinned" in all user-facing strings. Internal variable/key names (`afkEnabled`, `afkMessage`, etc.) remain unchanged for backwards compatibility with DataStore and Firestore.
- Default presets use "Away" instead of "AFK" (e.g., "Away - grabbing water").
- Notification icons use a dedicated `ic_notification.xml` drawable (95% scale, less padding than adaptive launcher icon) for better visibility in the notification shade. **ALL persistent foreground-service notifications** — `VrchatPipelineService.buildPersistentNotification`, `KeepAliveService`, `DiscordRpcService.buildNotif`, and `OverlayService` — use `ic_notif_sync.xml` (the Material "sync" circular-arrows glyph, matching the Automations tab icon). Since all four services share `NOTIF_ID = 1001`, any service that rebuilds its notification overwrites the others in the shade — so they must all use the same icon or the sync glyph would revert to the old icon when another service updates. Event alert notifications (friend activity, invites, groups, etc.) still use `ic_notification.xml`. **Duplicate-`ACTION_START` notification guard**: because the services share `NOTIF_ID = 1001`, a duplicate `ACTION_START` (app reopened after pressing Back, or a sticky restart) must NOT reset the shared persistent notification to a startup state. `VrchatPipelineService.onStartCommand` only posts `"Connecting..."` when NOT already connected; when already connected it re-asserts `startForeground` with `lastConnectedNotifText` — otherwise `onOpen` never fires again to restore "Connected as X" and the notification gets permanently stuck on "Connecting..." even though presence/RPC are live. Likewise `DiscordRpcService.onStartCommand` posts `"Discord RPC active"` (not `"Discord RPC starting..."`) when already running.

### Remote Config (Admin Edits)
The public app's moderation snapshot listener on `users/{deviceHash}` also picks up admin-editable fields (feature toggles, messages, intervals, presets) and applies them in real-time. Each incoming snapshot is filtered by:
1. `initialSnapshotProcessed` — drop the very first snapshot (DataStore wins cold start)
2. Per-field `lastSyncedValues` comparison — if the remote value matches what we last wrote, it's our own echo and is skipped; if it differs, an admin changed it and it's applied

After those filters, the snapshot's content fields (afkMessage, cycleLines, presets) are written to DataStore so existing flow collectors propagate them into ViewModel state. Toggles are set directly on the ViewModel (they don't persist — see below).

### Removed Features (do not re-add)
- **Divider system**: Previously allowed inserting text dividers between chatbox components. Fully removed from UI, ViewModel, DataStore, and message construction.
- **Preset naming**: Custom names for pinned/cycle presets. Removed from ViewModel, AdminScreen, DataStore, and Firestore sync. Presets now use generic "Preset 1/2/3..." labels.
- **Redundant action buttons**: Start/stop buttons in Cycle, pin/unpin + send once in Pinned, start/stop + test in Music — all removed since toggles handle activation.

### Toggle Persistence
Feature toggles (`afkEnabled`, `spotifyEnabled`, `cycleEnabled`, `timeEnabled`) survive Android process death (memory pressure) via `SavedStateHandle`, and survive Activity destruction because the runtime `VrcaViewModel` is now **app-scoped** (owned by `VrcaApplication`'s `ViewModelStore`, not the Activity's — see "Background Survival"). Content (messages, intervals, presets) DOES persist via DataStore as normal. Admin can still flip toggles in real-time via the Firestore snapshot listener, which writes directly to ViewModel state (and SavedStateHandle) without touching DataStore.

**OSC send gate (Start/Stop) — toggles configure WHAT, Start gates WHEN**: A master `VrcaViewModel.oscSending` boolean decides whether the enabled toggles actually transmit over OSC. **Toggling a feature no longer auto-starts sending** — flipping `afkEnabled`/`cycleEnabled`/`spotifyEnabled`/`timeEnabled` while `oscSending == false` only updates the local preview and persists the config; no OSC leaves the device and the VRChat chatbox is untouched (this lets the user set up their preview first). `startSending()` (Home "Start sending" button) flips `oscSending` true and launches the sender loops (`startAfkSender`/`startCycle`/`startNowPlayingSender` + `manageKeepaliveLoop`) for whatever toggles are on; each starter early-returns unless `oscSending && <its toggle>`, and every sender `while` loop also checks `oscSending` so the master gate hard-stops them. `stopSending()` (Home "Stop sending" button, replaces the old red **KILL**) flips `oscSending` false, stops all sender loops, and clears the VRChat chatbox **WITHOUT untoggling any feature** — so pressing Start again resumes exactly what was configured. While sending, toggling a feature applies live (starts/stops just that loop). `killStopAndClear()` still exists but is now reserved for the **ban path** (`VrcaScreen.kt` ban gate) — it additionally untoggles all four features. The Home UI shows a `SendStatusChip` (amber dot "Sending" / grey dot "Idle" — **deliberately NO uptime in the chip**: "Sending · 2h 14m" squeezed the headlineSmall title into wrapping "VRChat / Preview"; the Stop button carries the uptime instead) in the header as the at-a-glance OSC indicator, and the Quick Toggles header sub-label flips between "Press Start when ready" and "Edits show live" (plus a "hold a pill to edit" hint). **Home layout (`HomePage.kt`, Phase 4 of the UI revamp — see `docs/ui-revamp.md`)**: the original **"VRChat Preview"** `SectionCard` (title enlarged via `titleStyle = headlineSmall`) keeps its original visual identity — the centered tonal preview `Surface` above the faint avatar-silhouette `Canvas` (two redesign attempts were reverted per user preference; do NOT restyle it). **The bubble text sets an explicit `color = onSurface`** (expanded bubble + collapsed chip): `Surface(color = surfaceVariant)` switches `LocalContentColor` to the muted `onSurfaceVariant`, which dimmed the preview text — the user read the dimming as "the box is rendered on top of the text". In-game chatbox text is white, so the bright onSurface matches; the minimal-pill branches don't need it (their Text is a SIBLING of the pill, inheriting the card's content color). The container is a flexible `Column` with **no height cap and no `Arrangement.Center`** (120×120 silhouette) — first the old fixed-280dp overlay `Box` overflowed, then the `heightIn(max=420.dp)` + Center replacement STILL overflowed while sending (a max-height Column with Center arrangement lets oversized content escape BOTH edges of the card); the bubble text is capped at 9 lines so letting the card grow naturally is bounded and can never spill. A **character-budget counter** ("N / 144 characters", right-aligned `labelSmall` on the row under the preview that carries the eye toggle on its left; budget drops to 142 with the invisible border on; amber within 30 of the cap, red over) shows how much of VRChat's chatbox limit the current combined output uses. The card is **collapsible**: a chevron in the card actions (persisted via `UiPrefs.previewExpanded`) toggles between the full simulation and a compact "live chip" bubble (tap to re-expand) — the chip shows the FULL chatbox content (`maxLines = 9`, same cap as the expanded bubble; the original 3-line cap cut content off and read as broken). **The invisible-border eye toggle lives BELOW the preview, on the LEFT of the character-counter row — NOT in the header actions row.** With the eye in the header, the wider "Sending" chip squeezed the headlineSmall "VRChat Preview" title into clipping; removing the eye from that row frees enough width that the title renders whole at FULL size in both Idle and Sending states (per the user: the title must look identical, including size, in both states — never shrink it). **`SectionCard` titles keep an auto-shrink safety net** (`VrcaScreen.kt`: `onTextLayout` → fontSize × 0.92 relayout loop, floor 11sp, `softWrap = false`) whose trigger is **`isLineEllipsized(0) || didOverflowWidth`** — with `overflow = Ellipsis` the truncated layout "fits" so `didOverflowWidth` alone NEVER fires on-device (the original shrink hack was dead because of this); the actions Row spacing is 4dp. Manual Send's collapsed summary is just "Type a manual message". The card also carries the `SendStatusChip`, a **"Next cycle in Ns" ticker** (driven by `VrcaViewModel.nextCycleAtMs`, set by the cycle loop before each delay; a 1s `nowTickMs` ticker runs only while sending), the full-width Start/Stop button (Stop label carries the uptime), the cycle-trim warning, and the nested **Quick Toggles** `ElevatedCard`. **Quick Toggles render as a VERTICAL full-width `TogglePill` list** (PublicUiKit) in `vm.cardOrder` order — the 2×2 grid was reverted (pill labels got clipped); long-press jumps to the feature's edit page (Pinned/Cycle → Automations, Now Playing → Media, Time → its UTC dropdown); the Time pill label shows the mode ("Time · UTC+2"). **`TogglePill` uses `combinedClickable`, NOT a raw `pointerInput(checked, …)`** — the keyed detector was cancelled+relaunched on every state flip, so taps landing mid-restart were dropped or replayed a stale `!checked` (a no-op write → no recomposition → "the dot only updates when another toggle changes"); combinedClickable keeps lambdas fresh and adds ripple. The pill's state indicator is a single dot pinned to the **RIGHT edge** (labels vary in width; a dot trailing the text looked ragged). **No ON/OFF text next to the dot** — it was added once and rejected by the user as UI clutter; do not re-add. The Edit mode (Edit/Done in the Quick Toggles header) is **shared**: it switches the list to the legacy vertical arrow list (chatbox COMPONENT order, `vm.cardOrder` → OSC output order) AND overlays slim reorder strips on the page-level cards; while active, a **full-width "Done reordering" button is pinned at the top of the page** (the header Done can be off-screen while moving upper cards). **Home page-card order** (`Preview`/`Connection`/`ManualSend`) is a separate `UiPrefs.homeCardOrder` CSV pref (Reset resets both orders). **Connection and Manual Send are `CompactSectionCard`s** (collapsed = status): Connection's collapsed summary is TWO lines — the IP with the slot's device name underneath (`"$ip\n$slotName"`; the summary Text allows 2 lines) — with a live reachability chip (20s `InetAddress.isReachable` ping while composed — green "Reachable", amber "No reply" since OSC is UDP and many devices ignore pings, red "Not set"); expanding reveals the `IpField`. **Setup health checklist** (`SetupHealthCard`) replaced both the old every-tab "Setup incomplete" banner (deleted from `VrcaScreen`) and the Home "Setup Tutorial" card (deleted — onboarding owns the walkthrough, replayable from Settings): a Home-only tertiary card listing ONLY the currently-red items (VRChat not linked, IP not set, battery optimization on, Notification Access missing **gated on `spotifyEnabled`** so it can't be permanently red for non-media users), each row deep-linking to its fix; it disappears entirely when all green. Other tabs get at most a **red `Badge` dot on the Home nav icon** (`setupNeedsAttention` = !vrcLinked || !ipSet, with `vrcLinked` now reactive to `vm.vrchatLoggedOut`). **Sending uptime (`VrcaViewModel.sendingSinceMs`)**: stamped on a fresh Start, cleared on Stop/kill, persisted in `FeatureSessionStore` (`feature_sending_since` + a 60s `feature_sending_seen` heartbeat from `startUptimeHeartbeat()`); `restoreFeatureSession()` continues the counter across an OS-kill revival only when the heartbeat is within `UPTIME_RESTORE_GRACE_MS` (20 min — spans the watchdog's ~15-min recovery gap, same pattern as the Discord RPC counter), else sending resumes with a fresh counter. A deliberate swipe disarms restore so the counter always resets.

**Auto-restore after an OS kill (`FeatureSessionStore`)**: A deliberate **swipe** still starts clean (toggles OFF, not sending), but an **OS-initiated process kill** (memory pressure / Doze / OEM killer) is made invisible — the chatbox resumes on its own **only if the user was actually sending**. `FeatureSessionStore` (`com.vrca.app`, backed by the `vrca_remote` prefs file) persists the active toggle set **plus the `sending` gate** on every toggle/Start/Stop change and **arms** restore. `VrcaViewModel.restoreFeatureSession()` runs once after initial content load: it re-applies the toggle CONFIG directly (so the preview reflects what was set up), then resumes OSC sending via `startSending()` **only when the persisted `sending` was true AND something was enabled** — toggles configured but never started come back idle (no transmission). `AppShutdown.onTaskSwiped()` calls `FeatureSessionStore.disarm()` so a real swipe (the only deliberate stop) is NOT restored — an OS kill never runs `onTaskSwiped`, so it stays armed. This is the per-session source of truth for "was this stop deliberate?"; do not key restore on the `manual_kill_at` timestamp (it persists across sessions and can't distinguish a stale prior swipe from a fresh OS kill).

### Background Survival ("works as if on-screen; only a swipe stops it")
The runtime is decoupled from the Activity so everything keeps running while backgrounded, and recovers after an OS kill:
- **App-scoped ViewModel**: `VrcaApplication` implements `ViewModelStoreOwner` with a process-lifetime `ViewModelStore` (cleared only by `AppShutdown` on swipe). `VrcaViewModel` is obtained against it (`VrcaApp.kt`, `OverlayService.kt`), so the chatbox OSC senders, moderation/kill listener, NowPlaying consumer, and Firestore sync loops survive Activity destruction (`onCleared` only fires on a real swipe). The Factory uses `VrcaApplication.instance` (CreationExtras has no `APPLICATION_KEY` against an app store) and a plain `SavedStateHandle()` (an app `ViewModelStoreOwner` has no `SavedStateRegistry`, so `createSavedStateHandle()` would throw).
- **Headless ViewModel recreation**: after an OS kill the watchdog / `START_STICKY` / `BootReceiver` bring back `KeepAliveService` with no Activity — so everything the app-scoped VM owns would stay dead. `KeepAliveService.onStartCommand` calls `VrcaApplication.ensureRuntimeViewModel()` (idempotent, main-thread `ViewModelProvider` create) **ALWAYS on revival** (after the swipe guard, which returns `START_NOT_STICKY` for a deliberate swipe — so this only runs for a genuine OS kill). **This is NOT gated on `sending && anyEnabled`** — that gate was a real bug: the VM owns the chatbox senders AND the hourly liveness heartbeat (`startHourlyHeartbeat`) AND the moderation/watch/kill listener, so withholding it from a non-OSC-sending user (e.g. a Discord-RPC-only user) left their process genuinely alive (services + Discord RPC running) but reporting itself **permanently offline** to the admin — `lastActiveAt` froze (no heartbeat), and the moderation listener never re-attached so `watcherActiveAt` was never read → `AdminWatchState.isWatched` never flipped → neither the VM live-sync loop nor the service presence loop wrote → **the admin detail never updated and Kill/toggles never reached the device** (the symptom: "user is clearly online in Discord but shows offline / detail won't update no matter how long I spectate"). Liveness + moderation are runtime concerns independent of whether OSC is sending, so the VM must always come back. **OSC transmission stays separately gated** — `restoreFeatureSession()` only calls `startSending()` when the persisted session had `sending && anyEnabled`, so always-rebuilding the VM never makes a configured-but-idle user start transmitting. The cost of an idle revived VM is trivial (1 hourly heartbeat write + 1 moderation listener — the same cost any online user pays) and the process is already awake via the foreground services + wakelock.
- **Discord RPC / presence / notifications / timer** self-restore via the service layer: `VrchatPipelineService` restarts (sticky/watchdog/boot), auto-relogins from EncryptedSharedPreferences, and on `onOpen` re-reads `discord_rpc_enabled`+`discord_session_seeded` (DataStore) to auto-start `DiscordRpcService`; the RPC online timer has its own grace-window restore.
- **Discord WebView keep-alive WITHOUT an overlay**: the Discord gateway lives in a WebView whose JS Chromium throttles when it thinks the page is hidden (gateway drops ~45s after screen-off). Rather than a 1×1 `TYPE_APPLICATION_OVERLAY` window (which forces Android's "displaying over other apps" notification and looks sketchy), `DiscordRpcService` subclasses `WebView` and overrides `onWindowVisibilityChanged` to always report `View.VISIBLE`, plus `resumeTimers()` + `dispatchWindowVisibilityChanged(VISIBLE)` after creation. No overlay window, no system notification. **Three-layer defense against background JS throttling** (the one-time calls alone weren't enough — the RPC was silently dropping after extended background): **(1) Periodic `resumeTimers()` (every 10s via `pushActivity` + every 30s via session monitor)**: the 10s native presence timer calls `wv.resumeTimers()` before every `evaluateJavascript` push, keeping JS timers alive well within Discord's ~80s server heartbeat timeout; the 30s session monitor also calls `resumeTimers()` + `dispatchWindowVisibilityChanged(VISIBLE)` as a backstop. **(2) JS-level heartbeat keepalive**: `_vrca_sendPresence()` (called from native every 10s via `VRCA_setActivity`) checks if Discord's heartbeat has stalled (`Date.now() - lastAck > interval × 1.5`) and manually sends OP 1 (Heartbeat, `d: _vrca_lastSeq`) to keep the gateway alive even when Discord's own `setInterval` is frozen by Chromium. `_vrca_lastSeq` tracks the last sequence number from readable (uncompressed) inbound frames; `null` is valid per Discord's spec. **(3) Native JS freeze detection**: `lastJsResponseMs` (`@Volatile`) tracks when the last `evaluateJavascript` callback fired (updated in every push/health/shim callback). If no callback fires for `JS_FREEZE_RELOAD_MS` (120s), the session monitor forces `onSessionExpired()` (page reload) to recover — catches the case where even `resumeTimers()` doesn't work (some OEMs). Additionally, `sessionRecoveryCount` resets after `HEALTHY_RESET_MS` (5 min) of sustained healthy checks so intermittent drops over a long session don't exhaust `MAX_SESSION_RECOVERIES`.
- **Discord RPC "socket healthy but RPC gone from Discord" — the asset-resolution dead end (`VRCA_setActivity`)**: the PRIMARY cause of the user-reported "the app says connected but the RPC isn't on my Discord profile". EVERY image (including the default VRChat image) is an `https://` URL that must be resolved to a `mp:external/...` path via Discord's `external-assets` API before it can ride the OP 3 activity. The old `VRCA_setActivity` set `_vrca_activity` for an uncached image URL but **only called `_vrca_sendPresence()` inside the resolve success callback** — so if resolution failed (token not yet grabbed from `localStorage`, `fetch` deferred under background throttling, or the image URL just won't resolve), it retried 10× then gave up and the activity was **NEVER sent to Discord** even on a perfectly healthy socket. Native treated the `'resolving'` return as success (`consecutivePushFailures = 0`), the health monitor reported `alive`, and the UI showed CONNECTED — while the RPC was silently absent. The next 10s push re-entered with the same uncached URL → same dead end → permanent. **Fix: send the activity IMMEDIATELY without the unresolved image** (a deep-cloned copy with `large_image` stripped — Discord rejects a raw `https` `large_image`), store that image-less version as `_vrca_activity` so re-pushes keep showing it, THEN resolve the image and re-send the upgraded version. On permanent resolution failure the RPC stays visible (just without the world thumbnail). The RPC can no longer be blocked by image resolution. `VRCA_setActivity` now returns `'ok'` (presence already sent) instead of `'resolving'`.
- **Discord RPC world-switch stale-resolution race (`_vrca_activityGen`)**: each `VRCA_setActivity` starts its own `tryResolve` loop (up to 10 retries × 2s = 20s) to resolve the new world's image. On RAPID world/instance hops a PREVIOUS world's resolve could complete LATE and overwrite `_vrca_activity` with the stale world, then re-send it — RPC flicker / wrong-world / re-send churn that gets worse the faster the user hops (the user-reported "RPC stops/glitches more when actively on VRChat and switching worlds"). Fix: `VRCA_setActivity` bumps a `_vrca_activityGen` token on every call and `tryResolve` captures `myGen`; a resolve that lands after a newer world took over (`myGen !== _vrca_activityGen`) is ABANDONED (no overwrite, no further retries). `VRCA_clearActivity` bumps it too so a pending resolve can't re-add an activity after a clear. The `_vrca_asset_cache` write still happens so returning to a world is instant.
- **Discord RPC renderer-process recovery (`onRenderProcessGone`)**: a long-running BACKGROUND WebView's renderer process is reclaimed by the OS under memory pressure — MUCH more likely while the user is actively on VRChat (the phone app is doing heavy VRChat REST + WebSocket + presence work, so the whole device is under load). Without an `onRenderProcessGone` override that returns `true`, the OS can terminate the whole service process; and even when it doesn't, the page goes blank and the RPC is dead until the 120s JS-freeze detector eventually reloads. The handler now destroys the dead WebView and calls `loadWebView()` (fresh gateway) immediately, recovering in seconds instead of up to two minutes — and returns `true` so a renderer reclaim can never crash the process. This is a primary contributor to "RPC stops more often when on VRChat".
- **Discord RPC JS-side presence re-assertion (`_vrca_pushInterval`) — the "works for a bit then disappears while still connected" fix**: presence was re-asserted ONLY by the native 10s `presenceTimerJob` → `evaluateJavascript`. A backgrounded WebView has those native→JS calls throttled/queued by Chromium, **while Discord's OWN internal `setInterval` heartbeat keeps the gateway socket OPEN** — so the health monitor saw `alive` and the UI showed Connected, but the instant Discord dropped our activity (an idle transition, a session RESUME, or its own status-change OP 3) our native re-push couldn't run and the RPC vanished permanently on a live socket. (This — NOT the asset-resolution dead end, which only affects the FIRST appearance since the resolved image caches — is the cause of an RPC that shows initially then disappears.) **Fix: the shim now runs a `setInterval(_vrca_sendPresence, 10000)` INSIDE the page**, coupling re-assertion to the same JS engine that keeps Discord's heartbeat alive — if the socket stays up, our re-assert stays running regardless of native→JS throttling. It's deduped against the native push (`_vrca_lastSentMs` stamped in `_vrca_sendPresence`; the interval skips if a send happened within 7s) so the foreground doesn't double OP 3 — the JS interval effectively only fires when native pushes are being throttled. Guarded against duplicate intervals on shim re-injection; dies with the WebView on teardown; no-ops while `_vrca_activity` is null (after `VRCA_clearActivity`).
- **Discord RPC activity persisted in `localStorage` (survives a page RELOAD) — the "reconnected but the RPC didn't reappear until I reopened the app" fix**: the in-page re-assert interval above only re-pushes when `_vrca_activity` is set, but a page **reload** (`onSessionExpired` session recovery / cache maintenance) starts a FRESH page with `_vrca_activity = null`. The only thing that would re-set it is the native `evaluateJavascript` push — which Chromium **throttles while the app is backgrounded** — so after a reconnect-via-reload the activity stayed null and the RPC was absent until the app was FOREGROUNDED (foreground unthrottles the native push). Compounding it: under Discord's `zlib-stream` compression the `READY`/`RESUMED` frame is binary, so the shim can't detect session-ready and the only post-reconnect re-push is the socket-`open` one-shots (+3s/+8s). **Fix: `_vrca_sendPresence` writes the activity to `localStorage['_vrca_last_activity']` on every send, and the shim init (`WS_HOOK_JS`) RESTORES it into `_vrca_activity`** — so a reloaded page has the activity immediately and the in-page `_vrca_pushInterval` (which runs even backgrounded, coupled to Discord's own JS engine) re-lands the RPC within ~10s of the gateway becoming ready, with NO native push required. `VRCA_clearActivity` removes the key so a reload can't restore a cleared RPC. (A restored activity may be image-less/slightly stale for one cycle; the next full native push upgrades it — far better than an absent RPC.)
- **Discord RPC compression-independent zombie detection (`_vrca_lastFrameMs`)**: the health probe's only zombie signal was "no heartbeat-ACK for >2.5× interval", derived from parsing op11 frames — but Discord's `zlib-stream` compression makes those frames binary/unparseable, so `_vrca_lastAckMs` never updated under compression and a dead-but-open socket falsely reported `alive` (a second "socket healthy but RPC gone" path). The shim now stamps `_vrca_lastFrameMs = Date.now()` on EVERY inbound `message` event (string OR binary — the event fires regardless of compression), and `VRCA_gatewayHealth()` returns `zombie` when no inbound frame of any kind has arrived for 90s on an open socket (a healthy gateway streams heartbeat ACKs ~every 41s plus event frames continuously). This rides the existing `zombie`/`bad` → `DEGRADED_RELOAD_THRESHOLD` (4 checks) reload path.
- **Discord WebView cache cap (`startCacheMaintenance`)**: the long-lived WebView loads the full discord.com app, which persists an HTTP cache + Code Cache that grow **unbounded** — the dominant contributor to the app's runaway on-disk "cache" size. The cap measures **ONLY `cacheDir/WebView`** (HTTP cache + Code Cache — the user-visible "Cache" number in App Info) against `WEBVIEW_CACHE_CAP_BYTES` (**64 MB** — deliberately ABOVE Discord's ~30-50 MB natural working set so the clear doesn't churn+re-download every check; do not lower it below that). Checks every 30 min with the first check 3 min after start. When over cap: clears the HTTP cache (`clearCache(true)`), best-effort deletes `cacheDir/WebView` contents directly (Code Cache is NOT cleared by `clearCache`; deleting from cacheDir is safe — the OS may wipe it anytime), then **tears down the RPC state (`shimReady=false` + `stopPresenceUpdates()` + status→RECONNECTING) BEFORE `reload()`ing**. **The teardown-before-reload is critical**: the reload destroys the page's injected shim (`window.VRCA_gatewayHealth`/`VRCA_setActivity`) and the gateway, but if `shimReady` stayed `true` the session monitor kept probing the reloading page. Its probe returned `no_probe` (health function undefined) which was grouped with `"alive"` as HEALTHY — so a reloaded/dead page kept reporting CONNECTED and **never recovered on its own; only toggling the RPC off/on fixed it** (the user-reported "RPC randomly stops after a while, a toggle fixes it, seems tied to the cache clearing"). Two coordinated fixes: (1) the cache reload now resets `shimReady`/presence like a planned reconnect (but does NOT consume a `sessionRecoveryCount` attempt — it's maintenance, not a failure); `onPageFinished` re-injects the shim and resumes presence + the monitor. (2) **`no_probe` is reclassified as a gateway-missing condition** (grouped with `no_gateway`, not `alive`) — because the health probe only runs while `shimReady==true`, an undefined `VRCA_gatewayHealth` can ONLY mean the shim was destroyed by a reload, so it re-injects then reloads after the threshold. Backstop: the JS-freeze detector (`JS_FREEZE_RELOAD_MS` 120s) runs BEFORE the `if(!shimReady) continue` guard, so even a reload that never re-injects (leaving `shimReady=false`) forces `onSessionExpired()` → full WebView recreation within 2 min. **`dataDir/app_webview` (profile data — cookies, localStorage, IndexedDB) is deliberately NEVER cleared**: Discord's `localStorage.token` is the gateway auth — the JS client uses it (not the cookie alone) for gateway Identify and API calls. The old code called `WebStorage.getInstance().deleteAllData()` which wiped `localStorage` including the token, killing the Discord session every time cache maintenance ran — the cause of "RPC shows for a bit then disappears" and the WebView not appearing as a logged-in device. Removing that call fixed it: the session now survives cache clears because only expendable HTTP/Code cache is touched. The loop is started when the WebView is created and cancelled in `teardown()`/`onDestroy()`. (Affects BOTH builds — Discord RPC isn't admin-gated.) **Coil's disk cache is also explicitly capped**: `VrchatImageLoader` sets `diskCache { maxSizeBytes(10 MB) }` at `cacheDir/image_cache` — Coil's DEFAULT is 2% of FREE disk space (hundreds of MB on a modern phone), the second silent "Cache" grower; the LRU journal trims any oversized pre-existing cache down to the cap on use.
- **`PipelineWatchdogWorker`** (WorkManager, ~15 min): if logged in and the pipeline isn't connected, restarts `KeepAliveService` + `VrchatPipelineService`. Backs off when `AppShutdown.isManualKillFresh() || AppShutdown.isSwipedAway()` — the persistent `swiped_away` flag is what prevents this worker (which fires ~15 min after a swipe, long past the 15s freshness window) from resurrecting a deliberately-swiped app. An OEM/OS kill leaves the flag false, so the watchdog still recovers that. `BootReceiver` clears the swipe flag, restarts `KeepAliveService`, and reschedules the watchdog. `onTaskSwiped` best-effort calls `PipelineWatchdogWorker.cancel()`; `MainActivity.onCreate` re-schedules it on reopen.
- **`AdminRuntime`** (`com.vrca.admin`): process-lifetime singleton owning the force-kill staleness sweep and `watcherActiveAt` watcher heartbeat — moved out of Compose `LaunchedEffect` so they keep running while the admin app is backgrounded. Compose only registers intent (`setBrowsing`/`setSelectedUser`/`updateSweepData`). (The `config/adminPresence.browsingAt` heartbeat it used to write was REMOVED — see "Browse heartbeat (FULLY REMOVED)" above; `setBrowsing` now only gates the local sweep.) **Foreground gating (`setForeground`)**: the latched intent is the problem when the admin merely BACKGROUNDS the app (no swipe / Activity destroy) — `onDispose` never fires, so `browsing`/`selectedUser` stay set and the watcher loop kept writing `watcherActiveAt` every 30s indefinitely, holding that user in 10s live-sync while no admin was actually looking. `AdminScreen`'s `LifecycleEventObserver` now calls `AdminRuntime.setForeground(true/false)` on `ON_RESUME`/`ON_PAUSE`, and the watcher heartbeat, the immediate-on-select write, and the staleness sweep all gate their WRITES on `foreground.value`. The intent stays latched (not cleared), so returning to the foreground resumes exactly where it left off — but a backgrounded admin emits **zero** periodic writes. (This deliberately narrows the earlier "admin watches while backgrounded" intent in favor of cost: an admin who isn't on-screen shouldn't keep a user in live-sync.) **Directed-release APK picker state (`pickedApkState`/`ingestPickedApk`/`clearPickedApk`)**: the admin detail view's "Pick APK" (directed release) launches the system file picker (DocumentsUI). The heavy admin app (Discord WebView + foreground services + Coil loader) is routinely EVICTED while DocumentsUI is foreground, so on return the admin **Activity is recreated** — `AdminScreen.sharedUsers` (plain `remember`) resets to empty, the detail view (gated on `if (rawRow != null)`, i.e. the selected user being present in `users`) is briefly disposed, and any plain-`remember` picker state inside it is lost AND the file-copy coroutine on the composition scope is cancelled. That was the "picking an APK doesn't apply and resets the detail view" bug. Fix: the picked APK (cache path + parsed `versionCode`/`versionName` + filename + error, keyed by `docId`) lives in `AdminRuntime` and the URI→cache copy + `parseApkInfo` run on `AdminRuntime.scope` (process lifetime), NOT the composition. `UsersTab` reads it back via `pickedApkState.collectAsState()` filtered to the open `docId`, so the rebuilt detail view re-shows the pick. `ingestPickedApk(appContext, docId, uri)` uses the APPLICATION context (the Activity may not survive); `clearPickedApk(docId)` is called after a successful push (also deletes the cached file).
- **Safe foreground promotion (`Service.startForegroundSafely`, `com.vrca.app.ForegroundUtil`) — the "OEM kill → crash-loop on every reopen until reboot" fix**: the app targets **SDK 34**. On Android 12+ starting a foreground service from the BACKGROUND throws `ForegroundServiceStartNotAllowedException`, and on Android 14 the `dataSync` FGS type is itself background-start-restricted. After an OEM process kill, three background paths re-enter the FGS-promoting services — the OS's queued `START_STICKY` restarts (every service returns `START_STICKY`), the `PipelineWatchdogWorker` (`startForegroundService` from a background Worker), and the pipeline `onOpen` Discord-start. The `startForeground()` calls in `VrchatPipelineService.onStartCommand`, `DiscordRpcService.onStartCommand`, and `OverlayService.startAsForeground` were **UNWRAPPED**, so a background-initiated start threw an uncaught exception that crashed the whole (shared) process. This became a CRASH LOOP: every queued sticky-restart re-crashed the process, which **also took down the Activity the instant the user reopened the app** — so the app "kept crashing on open" until a phone REBOOT cleared the OS's pending sticky-restart queue (after which `BootReceiver`, an FGS-start-EXEMPT boot context, started the pipeline cleanly → `isConnected` → later starts re-assert safely). **`KeepAliveService` had the SAME latent crash by a different name**: its `startForeground` WAS wrapped, but the catch did NOT `stopSelf()`, and since the service is launched via `startForegroundService()`, a caught failure that leaves the service running triggers `ForegroundServiceDidNotStartInTimeException` ~5s later → crash. **Fix**: `startForegroundSafely(id, notif, tag)` wraps `startForeground` in try/catch and, on failure, logs + calls `stopSelf()` (satisfying the `startForegroundService` 5-second contract without a crash) + returns false; each `onStartCommand` returns `START_NOT_STICKY` on a false result so the OS does not immediately sticky-restart back into the same throw. Applied to all four services (KeepAlive's catch now also `stopSelf()`s; the pipeline's 10s notif-repost loop is wrapped too). Consequence: after an OEM kill the background watchdog/sticky paths can no longer crash — they degrade to a clean no-op, and the app recovers normally on the next user-foreground launch (or once battery-optimization exemption permits a background FGS start). This is purely a crash-safety fix; it does NOT change when a foreground start is *allowed* (still gated by Android's background-FGS rules), only that a *disallowed* one no longer kills the process.
- **OEM-specific power-management guidance (`OemPowerGuidance`, `com.vrca.ui.common`)**: the four FGS + wakelock + watchdog keep the process alive on stock Android, but Samsung/Xiaomi(MIUI/HyperOS)/Huawei-Honor/Oppo-Realme(ColorOS)/Vivo-iQOO/OnePlus ship proprietary killers that force-stop background apps **regardless** of the standard battery-optimization exemption — the only fix is the user adding VRC-A to that OEM's allow-list (Autostart / Protected apps / Never sleeping apps / App launch). `OemPowerGuidance.forThisDevice()` matches `Build.MANUFACTURER`/`Build.BRAND` and returns a `Guidance(brand, steps)` with exact per-OEM menu paths, or **null** on stock/Pixel/Motorola (UI shows nothing extra). Surfaced in the onboarding Permissions step's "Turn off background restrictions" card (replaced the old Samsung-only `TrustNote`) and in Settings → Help "Stops sending with screen off" FAQ. No em dashes (onboarding copy rule). This is the highest-leverage real-world survival improvement — no app code can override these killers, so guiding the user to the right setting is the only lever.
- **Bounded re-acquired wakelock (`KeepAliveService`)**: the `PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK` is acquired with a 6h timeout (`WAKELOCK_TIMEOUT_MS`) and **re-acquired on every 30s tick** (`TICK_MS`) instead of a single indefinite `acquire()`. Android explicitly warns against indefinitely-held wakelocks and some OEMs force-release them; a bounded acquire reset each tick is "held forever" in practice while self-releasing the moment the tick loop dies (process teardown). Non-reference-counted, so a fresh timed `acquire(timeout)` just resets the timeout — no release/re-acquire dance.
- **Revocable-grant ON_RESUME re-check (Home `SetupHealthCard`)**: an OEM update (notably Samsung One UI) can silently REVOKE the battery-optimization exemption or Notification Access while VRC-A is backgrounded. The Home page's `LifecycleEventObserver` re-reads `isIgnoringBatteryOptimizations` + `NotificationManagerCompat.getEnabledListenerPackages` on `ON_RESUME` (not just composition entry), so the setup-health card re-surfaces the now-red item the moment the user returns instead of staying falsely green until a full recomposition.
- **Limits (be honest with users)**: no app can guarantee the OS never kills it. Survival depends on the **battery-optimization exemption** (requested via `VrcaViewModel` / surfaced on Home + Settings) and the OEM allow-list guidance above (`OemPowerGuidance`). The architecture above maximizes survival AND makes the residual kill invisible via auto-restore.

### Storage Management
- **Firestore offline cache**: explicitly capped at 25 MB in `VrcaApplication.onCreate()` via `FirebaseFirestoreSettings.Builder().setCacheSizeBytes(25MB)` (default was 100 MB — far more than needed for the few collections the public build reads).
- **Discord WebView footprint**: `cacheDir/WebView` (HTTP cache + Code Cache) capped at 64 MB, first check 3 min after start, then every 30 min. Over-cap clear: `clearCache(true)` + direct sweep of `cacheDir/WebView`. **`dataDir/app_webview` (profile data incl. `localStorage.token`) is NEVER cleared** — wiping it kills the Discord session.
- **Coil image cache** (`VrchatImageLoader`): DISK cache capped at 10 MB (`cacheDir/image_cache`) — Coil's default is 2% of free disk space. **MEMORY cache capped at 12 MB** — Coil's default in-memory bitmap cache is **25% of app RAM**, and full-resolution event banners + world/instance thumbnails accumulating there (on top of the foreground services + Discord WebView) was a real **OEM low-memory KILL trigger** (users reported more frequent background kills after the event UI shipped); the hard 12 MB cap evicts old bitmaps instead of ballooning RAM.
- **Rich-content media (`RichMediaStore`, `filesDir/rich_media/{ann,upd}`)**: lives in DATA (not cache). `ann/` (announcement media) is culled live by `VrcaScreen`'s `gcAnnouncements` when an announcement is removed/swapped. **`upd/` (patch-note media) is wiped on every cold start** (`VrcaApplication.onCreate`) — it's view-scoped (re-downloads when the Update/What's New dialog shows), and a FORCED dialog killed mid-show never runs its `onDispose` `clearUpdateMedia`, so its cached video/images would otherwise linger forever. Culling is PURELY REFERENCE-BASED with NO size cap / LRU eviction (the `enforceTotalCap` backstop was removed): `RichMediaStore.reconcile(scope, referencedUrls)` deletes exactly the files whose URL isn't in the active set and nothing else, so ACTIVE media is NEVER evicted. This is REQUIRED because **video is download-first / local-only** (see below) — there's no URL-streaming fallback for video, so evicting an active clip would break it. `gcAnnouncements(urls, confirmed)` reconciles `ann/`; **`confirmed` MUST be true** (VrcaScreen passes `announcementsLoaded`, set once the listener delivers a snapshot) so the cull NEVER runs on the initial empty list (which would wipe all cached media before the list loads). Storage = exactly the media of your active announcements/updates, cached FULLY however big; removed/swapped content is freed on the next reconcile. **Video is DOWNLOAD-FIRST (`RichVideo`)**: the clip plays ONLY from the fully-downloaded local file (`Uri.fromFile`), NEVER streamed from the URL — the play button is replaced by a spinner + "Downloading video…" until `localFile` is set, then becomes tappable (a failed download shows a Retry). Streaming a not-yet-complete/remote clip caused playback issues, so the URL fallback was removed for video (images/gifs keep `cachedFile ?: url` — a URL load is a harmless loading state for them, and reference-culling keeps them local anyway). **`richImageLoader` (rich-content Coil loader) has its OWN capped disk cache** (`cacheDir/rich_image_cache`, 15 MB) + 12 MB memory cache — without it Coil defaults to 2% of free disk (a silent cache grower); separate dir from `VrchatImageLoader`'s `image_cache` to avoid Coil's shared-dir conflict.
- **Stale update APKs**: `VrcaApplication.cleanStaleUpdateApks()` deletes any `.apk` files from `getExternalFilesDir(DIRECTORY_DOWNLOADS)` on every app start. The update download (`vrc-a-update.apk`) was never cleaned up after the system installer ran — a ~20 MB file sitting forever until the next update.
- **Friends cache** (`vrca_friends_cache`): unbounded JSON blob keyed by userId, grows with friend count (~150-300 bytes/friend). No cap but bounded by VRChat's friend limit.
- **In-app alerts** (`vrca_in_app_alerts`): capped at 20 groups × 50 events (FIFO).
- **Seen notification IDs** (`vrca_seen_notifs`): capped at 500 (FIFO).
- **Crash log** (`vrca_crash`): capped at 80,000 chars, overwritten on next crash.

## Coding Conventions
- Use Jetpack Compose for all new UI — no XML layouts
- Follow existing file/package structure when adding new screens or components
- Admin-only features must be gated behind `BuildConfig.IS_ADMIN_BUILD`
- Firestore security rules must be updated alongside any schema changes
- Keep admin APK and public APK builds fully separated

## Git & PR Conventions
- **CRITICAL — Push ALL work to `claude/vrc-a-android-app-NUpaN`.** Commit on the local `VRC-A-Official` working branch, then push to the remote `claude/vrc-a-android-app-NUpaN` branch (e.g. `git push -u origin VRC-A-Official:claude/vrc-a-android-app-NUpaN`). **Do NOT push to `VRC-A-Official` directly — the remote consistently returns HTTP 503 on that branch.** Do NOT push to `main` or any other branch. Only deviate if the user explicitly names a different target branch in this turn.
- Commit messages: short, imperative ("Fix NowPlaying pause detection", "Add Releases tab filter")
- Include a brief description in the PR body of what changed and why
- Do NOT open pull requests automatically — the user merges the work themselves on their own schedule

## Autonomous Permissions
Claude is fully authorised to do all of the following without asking for confirmation:

- Read, create, edit, and delete any files in the repository
- Run any build, test, or lint commands
- Commit changes with descriptive commit messages
- Push branches to the remote repository
- Open pull requests with descriptions of what was changed
- Merge pull requests if all checks pass and the task is self-contained
- Create and push new releases to GitHub Releases
- Update Firestore security rules when schema changes require it
- Install dependencies or update `build.gradle` files as needed
- Fix any build errors, lint warnings, or test failures encountered along the way
- Refactor code for clarity or performance without being asked
- Add or update comments and documentation inline
- **Update this CLAUDE.md file** whenever new features, architecture changes, or conventions are added

## Keeping CLAUDE.md Up To Date
Claude must treat this file as a living document and update it as part of normal development:

- **When adding a new feature or screen:** Add a brief entry under Architecture & Stack describing what it does and any key implementation notes
- **When adding a new dependency or library:** Note it under Architecture & Stack with its purpose
- **When changing build commands or adding new Gradle tasks:** Update the Build Commands section
- **When changing Firestore schema or collections:** Update the relevant notes under Architecture & Stack
- **When establishing a new pattern or convention:** Add it to Coding Conventions so it applies to future work
- **When completing a significant PR:** Review this file and add anything that would help future sessions understand the codebase better

Claude should update CLAUDE.md in the same commit as the feature it describes, not as a separate task. The goal is that any new Claude Code session can read this file and immediately understand the full current state of the project without needing to explore the codebase from scratch.

## What Claude Should Always Do
- Run `./gradlew build` after making changes to verify nothing is broken before pushing
- Check that both admin and public build variants still compile after any change
- Never hardcode secrets, API keys, or credentials — use environment variables or Firebase config
- Keep `BuildConfig.IS_ADMIN_BUILD` gates intact on all admin-only features
- If a Firestore rule change is needed, update both the rules file and note it in the PR
- Update this CLAUDE.md file whenever the architecture, conventions, or features change

## What Claude Should Never Do
- Push to `main`, `VRC-A-Official` (it 503s), or any branch other than `claude/vrc-a-android-app-NUpaN` (always push to `claude/vrc-a-android-app-NUpaN` unless the user explicitly names a different target this turn)
- Remove or weaken `bannedDevices` Firestore security rules
- Mix admin-only UI into the public build
- Break the existing GitHub Actions CI pipeline without replacing it with something better
- Leave this CLAUDE.md file out of date after making significant changes
